## 11. AI-4 first bounded slice (2026-08-30, continued) — conversational business onboarding, plumbing VERIFIED, generation trace blocked on quota

Per the owner's explicit choice to start AI-4 (§5's provider/tenant-model prerequisites already resolved — provider is Gemini since AI-1, tenant model deferred per the roadmap's own recommendation), this slice ships mission §29-34's onboarding flow end-to-end: `create_business`, a new business-agnostic chat endpoint, and a chat UI replacing the default `/businesses/new` experience (manual form kept as an explicit fallback, per §2.7's own instruction not to destroy it).

### 11.1 Why onboarding is its own orchestration loop, not a call into `AiService`

Every existing AI tool (`ToolContext` in `ai.types.ts`) assumes a `businessId: string` that already exists — `AiService.runToolLoop` derives it once (`AiController`'s `AiOwnershipGuard`) and threads it through every tool call. Onboarding has no business yet — that's what the conversation produces — so reusing `ToolContext`/`ToolRegistryService` as-is was not an option without either (a) making `businessId` optional everywhere (weakens every *existing* tool's type-level guarantee that it always has one) or (b) building a second, narrower context/registry pair. Went with (b):

- `backend/src/modules/ai/onboarding/onboarding.types.ts` — `OnboardingToolContext` (`actorId` only, no `businessId`), `OnboardingToolDefinition`, and `OnboardingStreamEvent` (the same `tool_start`/`tool_result`/`message` shape as `AiStreamEvent`, plus one onboarding-specific variant, `business_created`, see §11.2).
- `onboarding-tool-registry.service.ts` — a second, separate `Map`-backed registry, deliberately not a generic parameterization shared with `ToolRegistryService`. This isn't just type hygiene: it's a real scope boundary — business-editing tools (`add_block`, `set_style`...) are structurally unreachable from the onboarding conversation, not just unlisted by convention.
- `onboarding.service.ts` (`AiOnboardingService`) — a second copy of the "model asks for tool → execute → feed result back" loop (~90 lines, same shape as `AiService.runToolLoop`, including the §7-flagged "tool already committed, follow-up LLM call failed" graceful-degradation branch). Duplicated deliberately, not out of oversight — see the class-level comment in `onboarding.service.ts` for the tradeoff reasoning. Only SSE, no synchronous `chat()` variant (unlike `AiController`, which has both for historical reasons — see the comment on `OnboardingController`): the frontend was always going to use the already-proven streaming path, so a synchronous twin nothing would call wasn't built.

### 11.2 `create_business` — the one onboarding tool

`backend/src/modules/ai/onboarding/tools/create-business.tool.ts` — `create_business(name, category, description?, currency?)`, `medium` risk (same level as `create_page`/`add_block` — an ordinary, reversible mutation, not `high`). Validates `category` against the real `BusinessCategory` Prisma enum and `currency` against `SUPPORTED_CURRENCY_CODES` by hand (same style as every other tool's `parseInput`, not class-validator — tools in this module have never used DTOs, see `create-page.tool.ts`). The handler is a thin call into the **existing** `BusinessesService.create` (`businessesService.create(ctx.actorId, dto)`), which already creates the business *and* its starter website in one transaction — no new data-access path, same "tool layer is a new caller of old code" principle as every prior AI-2/AI-3 tool. `BusinessesModule` needed one additive change to make this possible: it wasn't exporting `BusinessesService` (only `WebsitesModule` was already set up for cross-module use) — added `exports: [BusinessesService]`.

`AuditLog.businessId` is nullable in the schema specifically for this case (already true before this slice, just unused until now — see the model's own doc-comment about surviving business deletion). `AiOnboardingService` records `businessId: null` when the call fails (nothing was created) and `businessId: <newly created id>` when it succeeds — meaning a business's very first audit-log row, visible later in its own `AiActivityFeed`, is "AI created this business." `AiStreamEvent`'s new `business_created { businessId, slug }` variant is yielded immediately after a successful `create_business` result, ahead of the model's own follow-up text — so the frontend can redirect into the builder without waiting for (or parsing) a natural-language confirmation.

### 11.3 Backend endpoint — `POST /ai/onboarding/chat`, verified as far as the quota allows

`backend/src/modules/ai/onboarding/onboarding.controller.ts` — top-level `@Controller('ai/onboarding')` (not nested under `businesses/:businessId`, structurally impossible here), guarded by `SessionAuthGuard` + the **existing** `AiConfiguredGuard` (reused unchanged — it never depended on `businessId`). No `AiOwnershipGuard` equivalent needed: the only tenant check onboarding needs is "which user is asking," which `SessionAuthGuard` already provides via `CurrentUser`.

**Verified against the real running dev stack (Docker Compose backend+postgres+redis), real Gemini API, not mocked:**
- No session → `401` (curl-verified).
- Empty `message` → `400` from the reused `ChatRequestDto` (curl-verified) — same validation shape as the per-business endpoint, no new DTO needed.
- A real authenticated call reaches Gemini and gets a real `429` (daily free-tier quota, same `GenerateRequestsPerDayPerProjectPerModel-FreeTier` limit flagged in §10.4 — re-confirmed still exhausted via a direct curl probe to Gemini before starting this slice, and again live through the endpoint) — correctly surfaces as a clean `event: error` SSE frame, not a bare `500`, and correctly writes **no** `AuditLog` row (confirmed via `psql`: 0 rows for the test actor) since no tool ever executed — matches the intended "audit only records real executions" semantics, not a regression from `AiService`'s own equivalent path.
- Full round trip re-verified through the frontend proxy too (`curl` against `next dev`'s `/api/ai-onboarding`, real session cookie): same `429` frame arrives correctly formatted at the client, confirming the new Route Handler's piping works, not just the backend's.
- Test users created for this verification deleted afterward via `psql` — no dev-DB leftovers.

`backend/npm run check` — 55 tests (unchanged, no existing test touched), lint/format/typecheck clean.

**Not verified, same root cause as every prior AI-3/AI-4 Gemini-dependent gap (§10.4/§10.6/§10.7), not new**: an actual successful `create_business` call — the model asking a clarifying question, then calling the tool, then the `business_created` event, then a real business row landing in Postgres end-to-end through a live multi-turn Gemini conversation. Blocked purely by the daily quota, which this session could not reset or work around (re-probed directly against Gemini's REST endpoint before writing any code, and again mid-slice — still `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` both times). The orchestration loop's logic (tool-call parsing, audit recording, event ordering) is the same shape already proven live for `create_page`/`add_block` in §7/§8, just not re-exercised against a fresh Gemini call for this specific tool.

### 11.4 Frontend — `features/ai-onboarding` + `NewBusinessFlow` replacing the old `/businesses/new` page body

- `app/api/ai-onboarding/route.ts` — a second SSE proxy Route Handler, same shape as `app/api/ai-chat/route.ts` (see its own comment for why a Route Handler and not a Server Action is required for streaming), minus `businessId` in the forwarded body.
- `features/ai-onboarding/` (new FSD slice) — `model/types.ts` (frontend mirror of `OnboardingStreamEvent`), `api/stream-ai-onboarding.ts` (client-side SSE generator, same hand-rolled frame parser as `features/ai-chat/api/stream-ai-chat.ts` — kept as an independent ~20-line copy rather than a shared `shared/lib` utility, since FSD features aren't supposed to know about each other and the duplication is too small to justify the coupling), `ui/AiOnboardingChat.tsx` — a chat panel visually consistent with `AiChatPanel` (same bubble/typing-dot styling tokens) but without tool badges (onboarding has exactly one tool, and its "result" is a redirect, not a badge list) and without floating-window chrome (this one is embedded directly in the page, not a bubble-launched overlay).
- `widgets/businesses/ui/NewBusinessFlow.tsx` (new) — now what `/businesses/new/page.tsx` actually renders. Owns the page's single `<main>` (`SectionContainer`) and back-link/`PageHead` header (moved out of `CreateBusinessForm`, which is no longer a full-page component — see below), plus a two-way "С помощью AI" / "Вручную" mode toggle (same `role="tab"`/`aria-selected` pattern as `AiAssistantPanel`'s existing "Чат"/"История" switcher, reused for consistency). AI mode is the default, matching §2.7's framing that the manual form becomes the fallback/advanced path, not the reverse.
- `widgets/businesses/ui/CreateBusinessForm.tsx` — trimmed to just the form itself (name/category/currency/description/submit), no longer renders its own `SectionContainer`/back-link/`PageHead`. It had exactly one call site (`/businesses/new/page.tsx`, now replaced by `NewBusinessFlow`), so this was a safe, non-breaking reshape rather than a speculative refactor — its own submit/redirect logic (`createBusiness()` → `router.push('/business/[id]/edit')`) is completely unchanged.

**Verified**: `frontend/npm run check` (lint + format + typecheck + tests) clean — only the two pre-existing, unrelated `import/no-cycle` warnings remain. A real `next build` (production, not just `tsc --noEmit`) clean, `/api/ai-onboarding` and `/businesses/new` both present in the route manifest. SSR smoke test against a real running `next dev` server with a real session cookie: `GET /businesses/new` → `200`, page body contains the expected title and the "С помощью AI" toggle label, no error-boundary markers. A real `POST` to `/api/ai-onboarding` through that same dev server, with a real session, correctly proxied the backend's `429` SSE error frame byte-for-byte to the client — confirms the new proxy's piping, not just its error-JSON fallback path (which was the only path exercised for `/api/ai-chat` under the same quota block). Test users and dev server cleaned up afterward.

**Not independently verified — same category as §11.3, not new**: the actual chat interaction in a real browser (typing, watching bubbles stream in, seeing the mode toggle, redirect after `business_created`) — `claude-in-chrome` still not connected this session. Worth a manual click-through once a browser tool is available or the Gemini quota resets, same standing caveat as AI-2/AI-3's panel and activity feed.

**What's deferred, not silently skipped**: no non-streaming onboarding endpoint (never needed — see §11.1), no persistence of the onboarding conversation across a page reload (matches `AiChatPanel`'s own scope — conversation history isn't a requirement stated anywhere in mission §29-34).

---

### 11.5 AI-4 follow-up (2026-08-30, continued) — richer starter content via template selection, not free-form generation

`create_business` originally mirrored only the manual form's fields (name/category/description/currency), leaving every new AI-onboarded business with an empty home page — a real gap flagged in §11.4's closing note, filled here. Deliberately **not** solved by letting the model author `WebsiteBlock` content directly: that would reopen exactly the untrusted-input gap §3 already flagged (AI-generated `props`/`style` needs the still-unbuilt shared backend validator) for no real gain, since the project already has a curated, hand-designed starter-template system (`STARTER_TEMPLATES` in `frontend/src/entities/website/templates/index.ts` — `restaurant`/`agency`/`local-business`, each a real `WebsiteBlock[]` tree plus capability/seed-product/seed-service metadata) that a human already uses via `StarterTemplatePicker` on every empty site. The chosen design: **the model picks which existing template best fits the business it just created; the exact same trusted, hand-authored template code applies it** — no new content-generation surface, no new validation gap.

**Backend — `create_business` gains a `templateId` parameter:**
- `backend/src/modules/ai/onboarding/tools/create-business.tool.ts` — new optional `templateId` arg, enum `'restaurant' | 'agency' | 'local-business' | 'blank'` (a hand-kept copy of `STARTER_TEMPLATES`'s ids plus `'blank'` for "none fit" — same two-independent-copies pattern as `currencies.ts`/`ai-chat` model types elsewhere in this project). Unlike `name`/`category`/`currency`, an invalid or missing `templateId` does **not** throw — it's a cosmetic hint, not identifying data, so it silently falls back to `'blank'` rather than blocking business creation over a UX nicety. The parameter's description explicitly tells the model not to force a bad fit ("финтех/крипто/чисто онлайн-продукт → blank"), which is exactly the scenario the onboarding chat's own example now demonstrates (§10's crypto-exchange example) — a good real test that the honest "no template fits" path works, not just the happy path.
- `onboarding.types.ts`/`onboarding.service.ts` — `business_created` stream event gains `templateId`, extracted from the tool's output the same way `businessId`/`slug` already were.
- Backend never touches template *content* — no block trees, no seed-product data — only validates the model's choice of id. Keeps the "tool layer is a new caller of old code" principle intact instead of duplicating hand-authored block trees into a second language runtime, which would drift out of sync the moment someone edits a template on the frontend.

**Frontend — reused, not duplicated, template-application logic:**
- `widgets/website-builder/lib/apply-starter-template.ts` (new) — the capability-merge + seed-product/seed-service + block-building logic extracted verbatim out of `StarterTemplatePicker.tsx` (previously inline in its `handlePick`), so both the manual picker (human click) and the new AI-driven auto-apply path call the exact same function. Lives in `widgets/website-builder/lib`, not `entities/website`, because it imports `entities/business`/`entities/product`/`entities/service` alongside `entities/website` — `entities/website` is explicitly barred from sideways `entities`-layer imports (see `StarterTemplate.capabilities`'s own comment in `templates/index.ts`), so only a `widgets`-layer module can compose all four.
- `StarterTemplatePicker.tsx` — refactored to call the shared function; behavior unchanged (same seed-error handling, same `onSeeded` callback semantics), now returns `{ blocks, seeded, seedError }` instead of recomputing "was there seed work" twice.
- `WebsiteBuilderWidget.tsx` — reads a `?template=<id>` query param (via `window.location.search` in a lazy `useState` initializer, not `useSearchParams()` — this component's real content only ever renders after a client-side data fetch resolves, so there's no SSR/hydration concern to justify `useSearchParams()`'s Suspense-boundary requirement). If present and the page is genuinely empty, applies the matching template automatically via the shared function, shows a "Настраиваем стартовый сайт…" loader in place of the manual `StarterTemplatePicker` while doing so, then strips the query param via `window.history.replaceState` so a reload doesn't reapply it. An unknown/`blank` id falls through gracefully to the normal empty-state picker — same honest fallback as a human creating a business manually and not seeing a good-fit template.
- `NewBusinessFlow.tsx` — redirects to `/business/[id]/edit?template=<id>` on `business_created` (omitting the param entirely when `templateId === 'blank'`, keeping the URL clean for the common "no good fit" case).

**Verified:** `backend/npm run check` (55 tests, unchanged) and `frontend/npm run check` (10 tests) both clean; a real `npm run build` on the frontend clean, `/business/[id]/edit` still in the route manifest. Template-id consistency between the backend's hardcoded enum and the frontend's real `STARTER_TEMPLATES` ids checked directly (`restaurant`/`agency`/`local-business` match exactly). SSR smoke test: created a real business via the plain REST API, hit `/business/[id]/edit?template=restaurant` with a real session cookie through a real `next dev` server — `200`, no error-boundary markers.

**Not independently verified, and why — narrower than the usual quota-blocked gap**: this slice's actual template-application logic runs entirely client-side (a `useEffect` firing several REST calls — capability update, product/service seeding, `applyTemplate` store dispatch, autosave). curl can fetch the SSR shell (which is just a loading spinner regardless, since `useAsyncData` always starts `'loading'` on both server and client) but cannot execute that client-side effect — so unlike §11.3/§11.4's gaps, this one isn't actually blocked by the Gemini quota at all, it's blocked purely by the lack of a connected browser tool. `claude-in-chrome` was checked again this session (the owner reported installing it) but reported not yet connected to this session (`/chrome` needed) — worth a real click-through (pick a business type, watch the redirect, confirm blocks/products/services actually appear) once that's connected, independent of whether the Gemini quota has reset.

## 12. AI-4's last remaining gap closed (2026-08-30, continued) — real browser click-through, both onboarding and business chat

`claude-in-chrome` connected this session (owner ran `/chrome`) — the one gap §11.3/§11.4/§11.5 all flagged as needing an actual connected browser tool, not just curl/SSR smoke tests, is now closed.

**Onboarding (`create_business`, §11.1-§11.5), full real trace:** registered a fresh test user through the real signup form, typed "Открываю кофейню Ранкова кава в Киеве" into the `/businesses/new` AI chat, and watched it work end-to-end through two real Gemini calls (confirmed via backend logs — `[onboarding_chat] Gemini success` × 2, one asking a clarifying turn's worth of tool orchestration, one calling `create_business`): a business named "Ранкова кава" was created, redirected into `/business/[id]/edit?template=restaurant`, and the builder's starter-template picker opened with **"Кафе / ресторан" pre-selected** — confirming the model correctly matched a coffee shop to the `restaurant` template id, not just that *some* template landed. Selecting it applied the real starter block tree (hero section, "Забронировать столик"/"Посмотреть меню" CTAs) — the §11.5 client-side `useEffect` path (capability merge, template application) confirmed actually executing in a real browser, not just present in the bundle.

**Business chat (`AiService`/`get_project_tree`, AI-2/AI-3), same live session:** inside that same new business's builder, opened the floating AI assistant and asked "Сколько на сайте сейчас страниц?" — a real Gemini call correctly invoked `get_project_tree`, read the real (freshly-created, one-page) document, and answered "Сейчас на сайте 1 страница — «Главная»" with a "Прочитал структуру сайта" tool badge, matching §7-§9's shipped behavior exactly.

Test user and business cleaned up afterward (`psql DELETE`), consistent with every prior slice's verification hygiene.

**What this closes:** every "not verified"/"blocked on quota or browser" caveat left open by §10.4, §10.6, §10.7, §11.3, §11.4, and §11.5 is now resolved — AI-1 through AI-4 are fully verified end-to-end, not just plumbing-verified. AI-5 is the next unstarted phase per §4's table.

## 13. AI-5 first bounded slice (2026-08-30, continued) — Business Logic Engine v1, `order_completed` → `send_notification`, VERIFIED end-to-end

Per §2.5's own recommendation (`Rule { trigger, condition, actions }`, evaluated by a listener on an existing domain event, no arbitrary code execution) and the owner's instruction to continue per this plan. Scoped to exactly one real trigger and one real action — same "prove the whole pipe once before expanding coverage" discipline as AI-1's single `get_project_tree` tool — not the full 3–4-action-type table §2.5 sketched, because two of its own examples (`addLoyaltyPoints`, `setMembershipTier`) have zero schema support anywhere in this codebase today (confirmed by grep before writing any code, same audit-first discipline as §0.3) and would have meant designing new loyalty/membership concepts as a side effect of the rule engine itself, not extending it.

**Schema (`prisma/schema.prisma`):** `Rule` (businessId FK cascade, name, `trigger: RuleTrigger`, `condition: Json?`, `actions: Json`, `isEnabled`) and `RuleExecutionLog` (one row per rule per trigger firing — matched or not, which actions ran, any error) — `businessId` on the log stored as a plain string, not a relation, same "must survive business deletion" reasoning as `AuditLog.businessId`/`AiRequestLog.businessId`. `RuleTrigger` enum has exactly one value, `order_completed` — `appointment_booked`/`form_submitted` are real, equally-available candidates (same controller, same shape) but deliberately not wired yet.

**Condition format — hand-rolled, not JsonLogic:** `RuleCondition { field, operator: eq|neq|gt|gte|lt|lte, value }`, AND-only composition (no OR/nesting yet), validated on write by a hand-written parser (`rules/rules.types.ts`, `parseRuleCondition`/`parseRuleActions`) — same "tool args aren't trusted just because a DTO said `unknown[]`" principle §3 already established for AI tool inputs, not class-validator (JSON-blob-shaped, not a flat form). §2.5 suggested "JsonLogic-style" as one option, not a mandate; a new dependency plus an arbitrary-expression interpreter for one rule in the whole project would have been exactly the speculative infrastructure this project's own `AGENTS.md` warns against. Evaluation itself (`rule-condition.lib.ts`) is a pure function, unit-tested (7 cases: AND composition, dot-path field resolution into nested context, missing-field-doesn't-throw, no implicit type coercion) without needing Postgres — same pattern as `gemini-quota.lib.ts`/`ai-capacity.lib.ts` from the same session's earlier work.

**Action — `send_notification`, and a real bug it surfaced before it shipped:** the only implemented `RuleActionType`. Notifies the business owner (`Business.ownerId`) by reusing the existing `Notification` model/`NotificationsService` rather than inventing a parallel notification system (§2.7's "must be one system" principle applied here too) — but the existing model's `actorId` was a required FK, because every prior notification type (`friend_request`, `post_like`...) has a real human actor. An order placed by an anonymous storefront visitor (no Tavern session, no `User` row) has no actor to attach. Investigated before writing the migration, not discovered after: made `Notification.actorId` nullable (new `order_received` type), which TypeScript then correctly flagged as a breaking change at every existing call site (`NotificationWithRelations.actor: User` → `User | null`) — fixed each one, either with a justified `!` (the four other `notify*` methods always pass a real `actorId`, so their own `actor` relation is genuinely never null) or a runtime invariant check (`toDto`, which should structurally never see a null actor once the feed filter below is in place, but asserts it rather than trusting that silently).

That schema change also surfaced a real frontend risk, checked before shipping rather than assumed away: `entities/notification/ui/NotificationItem.tsx` and its mapper (`get-notifications.ts`) read `notification.actor.name`/`.avatarUrl`/`.id` unconditionally everywhere — an `order_received` row reaching the existing social notification feed would have crashed it the first time one appeared, not a hypothetical edge case. Fixing that render path properly (a distinct visual treatment for a system notification with no human actor, no "view profile" click-through) is real, separate frontend work, not something to rush as a side effect of a backend slice — so `NotificationsService.list()`/`unreadCount()` now explicitly exclude `order_received` from the general feed/badge count (`FEED_EXCLUDED_TYPES`) while still writing the row in full. The notification exists, correctly attributed, the moment a dedicated business-notifications screen is built to read it — this slice just doesn't wire it into the social feed it was never designed for.

**Backend — owner CRUD + the engine itself:**
- `backend/src/modules/rules/` — `RulesController` (`/businesses/:businessId/rules`, `SessionAuthGuard` + the same `assertOwnership`/`findOwnedRule` pattern as `DiscountsService`, not a new one), `RulesService` (CRUD + `evaluate(trigger, businessId, context)`).
- `evaluate()` never throws out of a failed *action* (catches per-rule, logs, still writes the `RuleExecutionLog` row with the error) — same "a broken automation must never break the real request that triggered it" principle as `AnalyticsService.record`. The one real caller, `PublicSitesController.createOrder`, wraps the call in an additional try/catch anyway, as defense against anything `evaluate()` itself doesn't anticipate — an already-committed, real customer order must never turn into a `500` because of the business's own automation.
- Wired at exactly one call site, `PublicSitesController.createOrder`, immediately after `OrdersService.createFromCart` resolves — same "Controller decides, Service doesn't know" placement §2.5 itself specifies, already proven in this codebase for Socket.IO emissions.

**Verified against the real running stack (Docker Compose backend+postgres+redis), not asserted by reading the code:**
- Created two rules on a real business: one with no condition (always fires) and one requiring `totalCents >= 999999`. Placed a real anonymous order (`POST /sites/:businessId/orders`, no session — same anonymous checkout path a real storefront visitor uses) for ₴200 (`totalCents: 20000`).
- `psql` after: the high-threshold rule's `RuleExecutionLog` row has `matched: false`, no action run — correctly did not fire. The unconditional rule's row has `matched: true`, `actionsRun: [{"type":"send_notification"}]`. A real `Notification` row exists with `type: order_received`, `actorId: NULL`, `recipientId` = the business owner.
- Confirmed the containment fix actually works, not just reads correctly: `GET /notifications` for that owner returns `{"items":[],...}` and `GET /notifications/unread-count` returns `{"count":0}` — the row is real but correctly invisible to the existing feed, exactly as designed, not by accident.
- Tenant isolation: a second, unrelated user hitting `GET /businesses/:businessId/rules` on the first user's business gets a real `403 {"message":"Это не ваш бизнес"}`.
- `backend/npm run check` (lint + format + typecheck + **93 tests**, 7 new for `rule-condition.lib.ts`, all prior tests unchanged) clean. A real `npm run build` (not just `tsc --noEmit`) clean. Test users/business/rules deleted afterward (`psql`/API `DELETE`) — verified 0 leftover rows in `rules`/`rule_execution_logs`/`notifications` after cleanup.

**What's deferred, not silently skipped:**
- ~~No rule-management UI~~ — shipped, see §15.3.
- ~~`appointment_booked`/`form_submitted` triggers~~ — wired, see §15.1.
- Any second `RuleActionType` (a real `addLoyaltyPoints`/`setMembershipTier` would need its own schema work first, see above) — the `actions` column is already an array specifically so adding one is additive, not a migration of existing rows. Still not started — needs the owner to decide on the underlying loyalty/membership concepts first, not a "just wire it up" gap.
- ~~OR/nested conditions~~ — shipped, see §15.2.
- ~~Surfacing `order_received` in the actual notification feed UI~~ — shipped, see §15.4.

## 14. AI-6 first bounded slice (2026-08-30, continued) — Custom Widget Engine v1, composition-only, VERIFIED end-to-end

Per §2.4's own recommendation — a widget as a constrained declarative JSON document, not executable code — scoped down the same way AI-5 was: ship the part with zero speculative infrastructure first (a saved, named, validated composition of blocks), defer the parts that need infrastructure this codebase doesn't have yet (`dataBindings` need an "approved data source connector" that doesn't exist per §2.6's own "clean slate" status; page-embedding needs id-remapping-on-instantiation logic that's real, separate work).

**Schema:** `CustomWidget` (businessId FK cascade, name, `schema: Json`, `status: CustomWidgetStatus` — `draft`/`published`, the latter reserved for when embedding exists, both behave identically today). No `dataBindings`/`capabilities` columns — adding them later is additive, not a migration of existing rows, same reasoning already applied to `Rule.actions` in AI-5.

**Validation — reused, not reinvented:** `schema` is a flat array of `{ blockType, props }`, each element validated by calling `BLOCK_SCHEMAS`/`buildValidatedProps`/`isAllowedBlockType` from `ai/tools/lib/add-block-schemas.ts` **directly** — the exact same curated allowlist (`heading`/`text`/`quote`/`spacer`) and the exact same per-field type/enum checks that AI-2's `add_block` tool already has unit tests and a live-verified track record for. This is arguably the cleanest instance yet of the "tool layer is a new caller of old code" principle (§1) in this whole initiative: not a new service method being called from a new context, but the same *validation library* enforcing the same rules for a second, independent caller. Each block gets a fresh `randomUUID()` regardless of what the client sends — same "don't trust a client-supplied id" reasoning as `AddBlockTool` itself.

**Backend — owner CRUD only, no AI tool yet:** `backend/src/modules/custom-widgets/` (`CustomWidgetsController` at `/businesses/:businessId/widgets`, `CustomWidgetsService` — same `assertOwnership`/`findOwnedWidget` pattern as `DiscountsService`/`RulesService`). Deliberately no `create_custom_widget` AI tool in this slice, same scope-narrowing already applied to AI-5 (the Rule engine also shipped API-only, no AI-authoring path) — proving the storage/validation is correct doesn't require the AI entry point yet, and adding one later is a thin tool wrapping this same service, not a rework.

**Verified against the real running stack, not asserted by reading the code:**
- Created a real widget (`heading` + `quote`) on a real business — response has fresh, distinct UUIDs per block and fully-defaulted, validated `props` (e.g. `heading`'s `level`/`size`/`color` filled in from `defaultProps`, not left partial).
- Confirmed all three rejection paths return real `400`s with the *same* error text `AddBlockTool` already produces: a disallowed `blockType` (`image`) → `"schema[0].blockType должен быть одним из: heading, text, quote, spacer"`; an unknown prop field (`href` on `heading`) → `"schema[0]: Неизвестные поля для блока..."`; an empty `schema` array → rejected outright (a widget with zero blocks is never useful, same reasoning as `RuleAction`'s minimum-one-action check in AI-5).
- Update (rename + `draft`→`published`), list, and tenant isolation (a second, unrelated user gets a real `403 {"message":"Это не ваш бизнес"}`) all confirmed live.
- `backend/npm run check` — lint/format/typecheck clean, **99 tests** (6 new for `custom-widgets.types.test.ts`, all prior tests unchanged). Real `npm run build` clean. Test users/business/widgets deleted afterward, verified 0 leftover rows in `custom_widgets`.

**What's deferred, not silently skipped:**
- ~~No page-embedding/rendering~~ — shipped, see §16.2.
- No `dataBindings`/`capabilities` — blocked on §2.6's Web3/data-connector work (or any other "approved data source") not existing yet, same dependency the roadmap's own phase table already recorded.
- ~~No widget-authoring UI and no AI tool~~ — both shipped, see §16.1/§16.2.
- Still only the same 4 curated block types AI-2 supports (no `image`/`button`/`productgrid`...) — extending the widget engine's coverage is the same work as extending `add_block`'s, not a separate effort.

## 15. Closing AI-5's remaining loose ends (2026-08-30, continued) — triggers, OR/nested conditions, rule-management UI, `order_received` in a real feed

Per the owner's instruction to close out already-started phases' deferred items rather than open a new phase, this closes four of §13's five "deferred, not silently skipped" bullets (the fifth — a second `RuleActionType` — still needs the owner to invent the underlying loyalty/membership concepts first, a real scope decision, not a "just wire it up" gap; left open, see §13's updated bullet list).

### 15.1 `appointment_booked` / `form_submitted` triggers wired

Both were "real, available candidates" per §13's own framing — same controller (`PublicSitesController`), same "Controller decides, Service doesn't know" placement immediately after the domain event already commits, same try/catch-wrapped `rulesService.evaluate()` call as `order_completed`. `RuleTrigger` enum gained both values (migration `20260831000000_add_rule_triggers` — `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`, applied via `prisma migrate deploy` rather than `migrate dev`, see the note below on why). `RULE_TRIGGER_CONTEXT_FIELDS` documents each trigger's available fields (`priceCents`/`durationMinutes`/`currency`/`status`/`serviceName` for `appointment_booked`; `formType`/`formLabel` for `form_submitted`).

`FormSubmissionsService.createFromRequest` changed its return type from `Promise<void>` to `Promise<FormSubmissionDto | null>` (`null` on the honeypot path) — the one non-additive change in this slice, needed so the controller has something to pass into `evaluate()`. Had exactly one call site (the controller itself), so this was a safe, non-breaking reshape, not a speculative API change.

**A real migration-tooling snag, worked around not ignored:** `prisma migrate dev` failed against this project's actual migration history with `P3006` (a shadow-database replay of an unrelated, much older migration — `20260821000000_add_post_images_and_link_previews` — errors on a column that a later migration adds first in real applied order but that the shadow DB's from-scratch replay hits out of order). This is a pre-existing latent issue in the migration history, not something this slice's schema change caused — confirmed by the error naming a migration with no relationship to `Rule`/`RuleTrigger`. Worked around by hand-writing the migration SQL (`ALTER TYPE "RuleTrigger" ADD VALUE ...`, the same DDL Prisma itself generated for `order_received` on `NotificationType` in AI-5's first slice) and applying it with `prisma migrate deploy`, which applies pending migrations directly against the real database without ever creating a shadow database — unaffected by the replay bug. Flagging the underlying `P3006` for whoever next needs `migrate dev` against this history; not fixed here (out of scope for a rules-engine slice, and fixing it safely needs its own investigation into which historical migration is actually out of order).

**Verified against the real running dev stack (Docker Compose backend+postgres+redis), not asserted by reading the code:**
- A real business with `booking` capability, a real 500₴ service, and a rule `{ condition: [{ field: 'priceCents', operator: 'gte', value: 40000 }, { any: [{ field: 'currency', operator: 'eq', value: 'UAH' }, { field: 'currency', operator: 'eq', value: 'USD' }] }] }` on `appointment_booked` — a real anonymous booking through `POST /sites/:businessId/appointments` produced a `RuleExecutionLog` row with `matched: true, actionsRun: [{"type":"send_notification"}]`, confirmed via `psql`.
- A second, cheaper (10₴) service's booking against the same rule produced `matched: false` — the `priceCents` leaf correctly gated the match, not a rule that always fires.
- A `form_submitted` rule (`formType eq 'contactform'`) fired for a real anonymous `POST /sites/:businessId/form-submissions` — confirmed via `psql`. A second submission with the honeypot field filled produced **no** `RuleExecutionLog` row at all (not even a `matched: false` one) — confirms `evaluate()` is genuinely skipped for a bot submission, not just its actions.
- Tenant isolation re-verified on `GET .../rules` after these changes: a second user gets `403`.
- `backend/npm run check` — **112 tests** (13 new — 7 for the new group-condition cases in `rule-condition.lib.test.ts`, plus a new `rules.types.test.ts` with 6 cases for `parseRuleCondition`'s group validation — all 99 prior tests unchanged), lint/format/typecheck clean. Real `npm run build` clean.
- Test users/business/services/rules deleted afterward (`psql`), 0 leftover rows verified in `rules`/`rule_execution_logs`.

### 15.2 OR/nested conditions

`RuleCondition` (the leaf shape — `field`/`operator`/`value`) is unchanged. New `RuleConditionGroup = { any: RuleConditionNode[] } | { all: RuleConditionNode[] }` (exactly one key), and `RuleConditionNode = RuleCondition | RuleConditionGroup` — a node is a leaf or a group, and a group's children are themselves nodes, so nesting depth is unbounded by the type, not by a hardcoded limit. The top level of `Rule.condition` stays an array evaluated with implicit AND, exactly as before — **every rule saved under the first AND-only slice is already a valid `RuleConditionNode[]`, with identical evaluation semantics, no data migration needed**: a flat array of leaves is just the degenerate case of the new format.

`parseRuleCondition` (`rules.types.ts`) validates recursively (`parseConditionNode`), producing path-qualified error messages for nested failures (e.g. `condition[0].any[1].field должен быть непустой строкой`) rather than a generic top-level error — a nested validation failure should point at exactly which branch is malformed. `evaluateConditions`/`evaluateNode` (`rule-condition.lib.ts`) mirror the same recursion: a group's `any` is `.some(...)`, `all` is `.every(...)`. Both are pure functions, tested without Postgres (same discipline as the rest of this module) — 7 new tests in `rule-condition.lib.test.ts` (flat OR, flat AND-group, nested-inside-nested, top-level leaf combined with a group) and 6 in the new `rules.types.test.ts` (parsing/rejecting groups: missing both keys falls through to leaf validation and fails there — not a special-cased error, since an object with neither `any`/`all` nor a valid leaf shape *is* a malformed leaf; both keys present is rejected explicitly; empty group array is rejected).

**Verified live, not just unit-tested**: the §15.1 nested-condition rule above (`[leaf, { any: [...] }]`) is the real proof — a real HTTP `POST` created it, a real anonymous booking evaluated it correctly against real `RuleExecutionLog` output, exercising both the parse path and the evaluate path for a genuinely nested condition, not a synthetic example confined to a unit test.

### 15.3 Rule-management UI

New `entities/rule` FSD slice (`model/types.ts` mirroring the backend contract — `Rule`/`RuleCondition`/`RuleConditionGroup`/`RuleConditionNode`/`RuleAction`, plus a frontend-only `RULE_TRIGGER_FIELDS` describing which context fields each trigger exposes, the same "two independent copies of one contract" pattern already used for `WebsiteDocument`/`ChatResult` elsewhere in this project; `api/{get,create,update,delete}-rule.ts` Server Actions, same shape as `entities/discount`'s). `widgets/business-dashboard/ui/RulesSection.tsx` (list) + `RuleFormModal.tsx` (create/edit) added as a new "Автоматизация" tab in `BusinessDashboardWidget` — not gated by any capability, same reasoning as `FormsSection` (a rule references a trigger's fields, but creating one doesn't require `commerce`/`booking` to be enabled).

**A deliberate UI scope narrowing, not an oversight**: the form builds and edits only a **flat list of leaf conditions with implicit AND** — it does not expose `any`/`all` group authoring, even though the backend fully supports arbitrary nesting (§15.2). Same "one real scenario before generalizing" discipline already applied elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `set_style`'s AI tool doesn't expose `BlockStyle`'s independent-per-side "Advanced" padding mode either, see its own comment). If a rule already has a group condition — created directly via the API, not through this form — the form detects it (`hasUnsupportedCondition`) and falls back to leaving `condition` untouched on save (only `name`/`isEnabled` are still editable), rather than either crashing on an unrepresentable shape or silently discarding the group the next time someone saves the rule. `RulesSection`'s list view, by contrast, **does** render groups correctly (`describeCondition` recurses through `any`/`all` and renders `(a ИЛИ b)`/`(a И b)`) — reading is honest about the full model even though writing is deliberately narrower.

`trigger` is not editable on an existing rule (disabled `<select>`, matching the backend's own `UpdateRuleDto`, which never accepts a `trigger` field) — changing it client-side would silently invalidate the rule's condition, since each trigger has a different field set.

**Verified:**
- `frontend/npm run check` (lint + format + typecheck + tests, 10 tests unchanged) clean — only the two pre-existing, unrelated `import/no-cycle` warnings remain. A real `npm run build` (production) clean, `/business/[id]/dashboard` still in the route manifest.
- SSR smoke test: a real business created via the plain REST API, `GET /business/[id]/dashboard` with a real session cookie against a real `next dev` server → `200`, no error-boundary markers. Confirms the new `RulesSection`/`RuleFormModal` import graph compiles and server-renders cleanly.
- **Not independently verified — a real gap, not silently assumed away**: an actual interactive click-through (opening the "Автоматизация" tab, adding a condition row, saving, seeing the new rule in the list) in a live browser. `claude-in-chrome` was checked this session and is **not connected** (`tabs_context_mcp` failed with "No group with id" on every retry, the same class of failure §9.5/§11.5 hit before `/chrome` was run in a prior session) — this is a fresh session, and the connection from a prior session does not carry over. The SSR/build checks above confirm the code compiles and the initial (loading) shell renders without crashing, which is real signal, but not a substitute for watching the form actually work. Worth a manual click-through once `claude-in-chrome` is reconnected (the owner runs `/chrome`) — same standing caveat this project has flagged and then closed before (§12).

### 15.4 `rule_triggered` surfaced in a real, dedicated business-notifications feed

Closes §13's last remaining bullet. `Notification.type`'s `order_received` value was **renamed to `rule_triggered`** (`ALTER TYPE ... RENAME VALUE`, safe — 0 rows of that type existed at migration time, confirmed via `psql` before writing the migration) because it now fires for all three Business Logic Engine triggers (`order_completed`/`appointment_booked`/`form_submitted`, §15.1), not just orders — keeping the old name would have been actively misleading now that a coffee-shop's "new appointment" alert would have been stored as `order_received`.

**Schema additions, both nullable and populated only for `rule_triggered`** — additive, no other type's rows touched:
- `Notification.businessId` — same "no `@relation`, survives business deletion" principle as `AuditLog.businessId`/`RuleExecutionLog.businessId` (see their own comments): a `psql`-level orphan after a business is deleted is an acceptable, precedented outcome, not a new gap.
- `Notification.summary` — a short human string (`"Новый заказ на 500.00 UAH"`, `"Новая запись: Стрижка (500.00 UAH)"`, `"Новая заявка: Обратная связь"`), built by a new pure function `buildRuleSummary(trigger, context)` (`rules.types.ts`, unit-tested — 7 new cases covering all three triggers plus their "field missing" fallback text) from the same `context` object `RulesService.evaluate` already threads through condition-matching. Deliberately a plain string, not a JSON blob for the frontend to interpret — each trigger's context shape is different (`RULE_TRIGGER_CONTEXT_FIELDS`), and there's exactly one place that ever renders this, so structured data would be indirection with no real consumer.

**Backend — `GET /businesses/:businessId/notifications`:** a new `BusinessNotificationsController` (own file, `businesses/:businessId/notifications` prefix) in the *same* `NotificationsModule`, reusing `NotificationsService` unchanged in spirit — same "two controllers, one service" shape this codebase already has for `OrdersController`/`StripeWebhookController` when a module's routes need genuinely different URL prefixes. `NotificationsService.notifyOrderReceived(recipientId)` became `notifyRuleTriggered(recipientId, businessId, summary)`; a new `listForBusiness(businessId, ownerId)` returns a lean `BusinessNotificationDto` (`id`/`summary`/`isRead`/`createdAt` — no `actor`/`post`/`group`, the same "don't reuse the actor-shaped DTO with nulled fields" principle §13's own frontend-risk paragraph already established), capped at 30 rows with no cursor pagination — same "мини" scope precedent as `AuditLogService.listForBusiness` (AI-3, §10.7).

**Frontend:** `entities/notification` gained `BusinessNotification` (a genuinely separate type from the social-feed `Notification`, not a nulled-out copy) and `getBusinessNotifications`. `widgets/business-dashboard/ui/RulesSection.tsx` grew a small "Правила"/"Уведомления" tab switcher (same `role="tab"`/`aria-selected` pattern as `AiAssistantPanel`'s "Чат"/"История", §10.7) — the notifications feed lives inside the same "Автоматизация" Dashboard tab rather than a new top-level tab, since it's the direct output of the rules configured right next to it. `RuleNotificationsFeed.tsx` (new) lists them with a "Прочитано" button that reuses the **existing**, already-generic `markNotificationRead` (`entities/notification`) — it only ever filtered by `recipientId`, never by type, so it needed no backend change to work here.

**Verified against the real running dev stack (Docker Compose backend+postgres+redis), not asserted by reading the code:**
- A real business, a real `order_completed` rule with no condition (always fires), a real product, and a real anonymous order through `POST /sites/:businessId/orders` produced a `GET /businesses/:businessId/notifications` response of `[{"summary":"Новый заказ на 150.00 UAH","isRead":false,...}]` — confirmed the summary text is built from the *real* order total, not a placeholder.
- The same row is correctly **absent** from `GET /notifications` (`{"items":[],...}`) and doesn't count toward `GET /notifications/unread-count` (`{"count":0}`) — the exclusion from the general feed still holds after the rename.
- Tenant isolation: a second user hitting the first user's `GET /businesses/:businessId/notifications` gets a real `403 {"message":"Это не ваш бизнес"}`.
- `backend/npm run check` — **119 tests** (7 new for `buildRuleSummary`, all 112 prior unchanged), lint/format/typecheck clean. Real `npm run build` clean.
- `frontend/npm run check` (10 tests unchanged) clean, real `npm run build` clean, `/business/[id]/dashboard` still in the route manifest. A fresh `next dev` server (a second, ad-hoc instance on a spare port — the port-3000 process already running in this environment predates this session, same one §10.6 flagged and deliberately left alone) served the dashboard route at a real `200` with no error-boundary markers for a real business/session, confirming the new `RuleNotificationsFeed`/tab-switcher import graph compiles and server-renders cleanly. One real mid-session mistake caught and fixed, not silently worked around: running a production `next build` in the same directory while that ad-hoc `next dev` was still running corrupted its `.next` dev cache (a stale "module not found" for a file that demonstrably existed and typechecked) — restarting the dev server cleanly resolved it; not a code defect, a self-inflicted process conflict from running build and dev against the same `.next` output directory simultaneously, worth remembering for any future verification pass in this environment.
- Interactive click-through (opening "Уведомления", clicking "Прочитано") **not independently verified** — same standing `claude-in-chrome`-not-connected gap as §15.3, not a new one.
- Test business/rule/product/order/users deleted afterward (`psql`/API `DELETE`) — verified 0 leftover `notifications` rows for the test business.

All four of §13's originally-deferred bullets are now closed except the second `RuleActionType`, which still needs the owner's decision on the underlying loyalty/membership concepts (§13's updated list).

## 16. Closing AI-6's remaining loose ends (2026-08-30, continued) — AI-authoring tool, page-embedding/rendering

Closes two of §14's four deferred bullets (`dataBindings`/`capabilities` stay blocked on §2.6's Web3/data-connector work not existing; the fourth-curated-type limit stays tied to `add_block`'s own allowlist, same as before).

### 16.1 `create_custom_widget` — AI-authoring entry point

`backend/src/modules/ai/tools/create-custom-widget.tool.ts` — the same "tool layer is a new caller of old code" principle as every prior AI tool: a thin wrapper around the *existing* `CustomWidgetsService.create`, `medium` risk (same tier as `add_block`/`create_page` — an ordinary, reversible mutation). `parseInput` only checks the outer shape (`name` a non-empty string, `schema` a non-empty array of objects); the real per-block validation (allowed `blockType`, `props` shape) is the exact same `parseWidgetSchema`/`add-block-schemas.ts` allowlist the owner-CRUD API already enforces inside the service — not duplicated in the tool. `AiModule` gained one import (`CustomWidgetsModule`) and one provider line, the same two-line addition every prior tool needed.

**Verified:** `backend/npm run check` (119 tests, unchanged — this tool has no new pure-function logic worth unit-testing beyond what `parseWidgetSchema` already covers) and a real `npm run build` both clean. The module wiring itself verified live: the backend container restarted cleanly with `AiModule dependencies initialized` and no DI errors, confirming `CreateCustomWidgetTool`'s constructor dependencies (`CustomWidgetsService`, `ToolRegistryService`) resolve correctly. **Not verified — blocked on the same Gemini daily-quota exhaustion flagged throughout AI-3/AI-4 (§10.4 etc.), re-confirmed via a real `429` from a live chat call, not assumed**: an actual multi-turn conversation asking the model to save a widget, correctly parsing the model's `schema` argument, and creating a real row. This session did not retry against the quota (same "don't hammer a rate limit" discipline as everywhere else) — worth a real run once the quota resets or a paid key is configured.

### 16.2 Page-embedding/rendering — snapshot insertion from the builder's own block library

§14 left this as "real, separate work" needing a decision between two designs — **resolved in this slice: snapshot at insertion, not a live link back to the saved `CustomWidget`**. Reasoning, not a coin flip: this codebase already has exactly one precedent for "insert a pre-built set of blocks into a page" — `applyTemplate`/starter templates — and that precedent is snapshot-only (editing `STARTER_TEMPLATES` later never changes a business's already-inserted blocks). A live link would be genuinely new infrastructure (versioning, sync-on-edit, orphan handling when the source widget is deleted) with no existing pattern to reuse and no concrete request for it — the same "don't build speculative infrastructure" discipline this whole roadmap has applied everywhere else (`Rule.condition` AND-only before OR, one curated block allowlist before a full registry port, etc.).

**What shipped:**
- `entities/website/model/block-tree.ts` gained `remapBlockIds(block)` — recursively reassigns fresh `id`s to a block and its `children` (pure, exported, unit-tested). `website-store.ts` gained `insertWidgetBlocks(blocks, parentId, index)`: remaps every block in the given array once, then inserts them in sequence via the *existing* `insertBlock` primitive (same one `addBlock`/`insertBlockBeside` already use) — goes through `withHistory`, so inserting a widget is undoable exactly like any other builder action, for free.
- `ComponentLibraryPanel.tsx` (the "Блоки" panel) gained a "Мои виджеты" section, fetched via the existing `getCustomWidgets(businessId)` — shown only when the list loads successfully and is non-empty (loading/empty states produce no visible section, since widgets are an optional feature; a real fetch **error** still surfaces as visible text, not swallowed, per `frontend/AGENTS.md` §4). Clicking a widget calls `insertWidgetBlocks` with the widget's own `schema`, appending to the end of the active page — the same default-click behavior as any other component in the same panel.
- `AddBlockModal.tsx` (the "+" button flow between existing blocks) gained the same widget section via a new `onAddWidget` override, mirroring the existing `onAdd` override pattern exactly. **A deliberate scope narrowing, documented in the code**: widgets don't participate in the side-crosshair "wrap in columns" flow (`insertBlockBeside`'s behavior for single blocks) — a widget is already several blocks, and there's no non-arbitrary way to decide how N blocks should share one side of a two-column wrap. Side-triggered widget insertion falls back to plain sibling insertion right after the anchor, computed with the exact same `findParentId`/`findBlock` logic `insertBlockBeside` itself uses internally.
- Widget items in the library are **click-only, not draggable** — a real, documented scope line, not an oversight: drag-and-drop would require teaching `Canvas.tsx`'s `onDragEnd` a second drag-payload kind (`'widget'` alongside the existing `'library'`) that inserts N blocks instead of one, real additional work with no demonstrated need yet (click already gives a complete way to insert a widget).
- `CustomWidget.status`'s schema comment and `CustomWidgetsService`'s class comment updated to reflect that embedding now exists and is snapshot-only — stale documentation left uncorrected would have actively misled the next reader into re-litigating a decision already made.

**Verified:**
- `frontend/npm run check` — **15 tests** (5 new, in a new `block-tree.test.ts`: `remapBlockIds` assigns fresh ids without touching type/props, recurses into `children`, doesn't add a spurious `children` key to a leaf block; a loop-shape test proving the same widget schema inserted twice produces four distinct, correctly-ordered block ids — the exact same batch-insert logic `insertWidgetBlocks` uses, exercised without needing the Zustand store or a browser). All prior tests unchanged. Lint/format/typecheck clean (only the two pre-existing, unrelated `import/no-cycle` warnings remain).
- A real `npm run build` (production) clean, `/business/[id]/edit` still in the route manifest.
- SSR smoke test: a real business created via the plain REST API, `GET /business/[id]/edit` with a real session cookie against a real `next dev` server → `200`, no error-boundary markers — confirms the new `ComponentLibraryPanel`/`AddBlockModal` import graph (now depending on `entities/custom-widget`) compiles and server-renders cleanly.
- Backend regression check: creating a widget via the real owner API after this slice's changes still works exactly as in §14 (same request/response shape) — the only backend touch in this slice was two doc comments, no logic changed.

**Not independently verified — the same category of gap §11.5 first flagged for template-application, not a new one**: the actual insertion runs entirely client-side (a button click dispatching a Zustand action, then the builder's own autosave persisting the result) — curl can fetch the SSR shell (a loading spinner regardless) but cannot click a button. `claude-in-chrome` was checked again this session and is still not connected (fresh session, no carry-over from any prior session's `/chrome`). The `block-tree.test.ts` tests above verify the actual id-remapping/insertion-order algorithm in isolation, which is real signal beyond "it compiles" — but a genuine click-through (open a business with a saved widget, click it in the "Блоки" panel, confirm the blocks appear on the canvas and survive a page reload) is still owed once a browser tool is available.

Attempted both this session:

- **Gemini daily quota**: a direct, bare REST ping straight to Gemini's `generateContent` endpoint (bypassing this app entirely) returned a real `200` — the *provider's own* daily quota has reset. However, a real authenticated call through this app's own `/businesses/:id/ai/chat` still returned `429` with this project's own message ("Дневной лимит... по тихоокеанскому времени") — **`GeminiQuotaService`'s Redis-backed RPD counter is a separate, self-imposed budget that persists across sessions independently of whether Gemini itself would currently accept a call**, and it was still showing exhausted at the time of this check. Correctly did not attempt to raise `GEMINI_RPD_LIMIT` or otherwise bypass this to force a green check — that counter exists specifically as a cost-control safety mechanism (§9 intro's "quota-control layer"), and circumventing it to manufacture a verification isn't a legitimate way to close the gap. §16.1's `create_custom_widget` live-trace gap (and any other quota-blocked trace) remains open until the app's own tracked budget resets, independent of Gemini's real one — worth remembering this distinction specifically, since a future session could otherwise waste a bare-Gemini-ping check assuming it proves the app-level path is unblocked too.

Treated the owner's present "close out what's open" instruction as sufficient authorization for this specific item (pure documentation, zero execution risk, mirrors a pattern `frontend/AGENTS.md`/`frontend/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` already establishes, and has been re-flagged unresolved across six prior updates without ever being acted on) — created both, documenting conventions that were already followed in practice throughout AI-1 through AI-6 (provider-abstraction pattern, ownership-check pattern, AI-tool self-registration, the `@Sse()` guard-timing gotcha from §10.2, the `P3006` migration workaround from §15.1) rather than inventing new ones. `backend/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` also documents the Payment Plans work from this same live conversation (gating, subscription switching, ad/commerce-commission copy) — that initiative predates and sits outside this file's own scope, so it gets a one-paragraph summary there rather than a parallel `*_ROADMAP.md` file being started retroactively for it.

Flagging plainly rather than padding this section with busywork.

## 18. Two of the owner's product decisions closed — second `RuleActionType` and `image`/`button` in `add_block` (2026-08-31, continued)

The owner made both decisions §17 flagged as blocking: loyalty mechanic = both `add_loyalty_points` (fixed points, set in the rule) and `set_membership_tier` (free-text tier, set in the rule), customer identified by `customerEmail` already on `Order`/`Appointment`; `image`/`button` scope = wide v1 immediately, including `addToCart`/`bookAppointment` link variants, not just the "simple" ones.

**Loyalty (`RulesService`)**: new `CustomerLoyaltyAccount` model (`businessId`+`email` unique, `points`, nullable `tier`), two new `RuleActionType` values. `runActions` silently skips (not an error) when the trigger's context has no `customerEmail` — expected/common, not a failure. `PublicSitesController` now threads `customerEmail` into `order_completed`/`appointment_booked` context. New `GET .../rules/loyalty-accounts` read endpoint. Frontend: `RuleFormModal` now actually lets the owner pick all three action types (previously hardcoded to `send_notification` only, despite the backend already supporting `actions` updates) plus a new "Лояльность" tab listing accumulated points/tiers. **Verified live**: two real orders to the same email via the real anonymous checkout — points accumulated 10→20 across both, tier flipped to `gold` only once the condition's threshold was crossed, an order with no email correctly created zero rows (no crash), cross-tenant read got a real `403`. 10 new unit tests for the action validation, 119→129 backend tests (then 129→139 after the `image`/`button` work below, same session). `backend/npm run check` and a real `npm run build` both clean on both sides.

**`image`/`button` in `add_block`/`update_block_props`**: extended `add-block-schemas.ts`'s curated allowlist from 4 to 6 types. Two new field kinds — `mediaAsset` (image.src: only a URL already present in the business's uploaded media library, checked against a pre-fetched set; the model can never invent an upload, since no LLM API this project uses supports sending binary data through tool-calling) and `linkTarget` (image.link/button.url: full 7-variant `LinkTarget` union, validated by a new pure `lib/link-target-schema.ts` — `page`/`addToCart`/`bookAppointment` check real existence against pre-fetched id sets, `email`/`phone` reuse `class-validator`'s `isEmail`/the project's existing `LOOSE_PHONE_PATTERN`). A new low-risk read tool, `list_media_assets`, lets the model discover what it's allowed to reference. `buildBlockRefs` (new, `tools/build-block-refs.ts`) assembles the id/URL sets via direct Prisma queries (products/services) + the existing `MediaAssetsService.list` — called only when `blockType` is actually `image`/`button`, not on every tool call. `CustomWidgetsService`'s `parseWidgetSchema` (§14) technically inherits `image`/`button` through the shared `BLOCK_SCHEMAS` too, but was NOT extended to pass business-scoped refs in this slice — documented explicitly in its own comment as a real, intentional boundary (it validates at the DTO layer, without a `businessId` in scope), not a silent gap: an `image`/`button` block inside a saved widget today can only use `src: null` and the four refs-free link variants, same safe-default-rejects-rather-than-wrongly-accepts posture as everywhere else in this codebase.

**Verified**: 15 new unit tests (`add-block-schemas.test.ts` — accept/reject for both new field kinds, including addToCart/bookAppointment/email/unknown-link-type cases) plus the two pre-existing tests that hardcoded `'image'` as an example of a *disallowed* type (now factually wrong since it's allowed) fixed to use `'gallery'` instead — a real, if trivial, regression these changes would otherwise have silently introduced. `backend/npm run check` (139 tests) and a real `npm run build` both clean. DI wiring verified live: backend container restarted cleanly with `AiModule dependencies initialized` and no errors, confirming `AddBlockTool`/`UpdateBlockPropsTool`'s new `PrismaService`/`MediaAssetsService` constructor dependencies resolve. **`buildBlockRefs`'s real Prisma queries specifically** (the one piece of new logic the unit tests couldn't cover, since they pass hand-built fake ref sets) were verified against the real running stack via a throwaway, not-committed script (same pattern as §10.3's SSE-parser scratch verification) — a real business, product, service, and a directly-`psql`-inserted media asset row all correctly appeared in the assembled refs, and `buildValidatedProps` then correctly accepted an `image` block referencing the real upload + a real `addToCart` link, accepted a `button` with a real `bookAppointment` link, and rejected both a fabricated media URL and a fake service id with the expected error messages. Script and test business/user deleted immediately after.

**Not verified — same quota-blocked category as every prior AI-3/AI-4 gap, not new**: an actual live Gemini conversation choosing to call `add_block`/`update_block_props` with an `image`/`button` type, or calling `list_media_assets` on its own initiative. The app-level Gemini daily quota (`GeminiQuotaService`, distinct from the provider's own — see §17) was still exhausted at the time of this slice. The tool-calling plumbing itself (parsing, validation, execution, `AuditLog` write) is unchanged from the already-live-verified `add_block`/`update_block_props` code path (§8/§9) — only the schema/refs layer underneath it is new, and that's what the scratch script above exercised against real data.

## 19. AI-7 first bounded slice (2026-08-31, continued) — Web3 read-only wallet/NFT display, VERIFIED end-to-end against real Alchemy data

Per §2.6's own recommendation — `Web3Provider` abstract class, one real adapter, read/display operations only, no AI-initiated signing — and the owner confirming an Alchemy API key was already provisioned for the project (unblocking the one open item §5/§17 had flagged for this phase). Scoped to exactly what §2.6/§4's phase table describe: a **read-only** display of the business owner's **own** wallet (balance + NFTs), not a customer-facing wallet-connect/payment flow — that remains explicitly out of scope, to be built client-side with wagmi/viem against the visitor's own wallet extension, never through the backend or AI tool layer (§2.6's own "financial transactions always require an explicit client-side wallet-signed confirmation, never AI-initiated").

**Backend**: `Web3Provider`/`AlchemyAdapter` (`modules/web3`) — same provider-abstraction pattern as `PaymentProvider`/`LlmProvider` (`AGENTS.md` backend §4), direct `fetch` against Alchemy's JSON-RPC (`eth_getBalance`) and NFT REST API (`getNFTsForOwner`), no SDK dependency added, same reasoning as `GeminiAdapter`. One new nullable column, `Business.web3WalletAddress` — reused the existing `UpdateBusinessDto`/`BusinessesService.update` path (regex-validated `0x` + 40 hex, empty string clears it) rather than a parallel CRUD, since it's one flat field with the exact same shape as `email`/`phone`. `Web3Service.getWalletInfo` does the ownership check inline (no owned sub-entity, same pattern as `DiscountsService`/`BillingService`) and never throws for "not configured" states — `walletAddress: null` (no address set yet) and `providerConfigured: false` (operator hasn't set `ALCHEMY_API_KEY`) are both normal DTO shapes the frontend renders directly, not error paths; only a real Alchemy call failure after both are present becomes a soft `error` field, not a `500`, so the read-only widget never breaks the whole dashboard tab over an external API hiccup.

**AI tool**: `get_wallet_info` (`low` risk, read-only, zero parameters) — deliberately does **not** accept an address argument from the model; it only ever reads the wallet already saved on `ctx.businessId`, so the AI can't be turned into a general block-explorer over arbitrary third-party wallets.

**Frontend**: new `entities/web3` slice (mirrors `entities/rule`'s shape) + a "Web3" dashboard tab (`Web3Section`, ungated by any capability, same principle as "Автоматизация"/"Виджеты") — shows an inline form to set/clear the address when none is saved, otherwise the shortened address, an ETH balance stat, an NFT count stat, and a responsive NFT grid (image + name, `EmptyState` when the wallet holds none).

**A real, previously-undiscovered bug found and fixed as a direct blocker of this slice's own verification**: `create_custom_widget`'s `schema` parameter (`type: 'array'`) had no `items` field. Gemini's function-calling API requires `items` on every array-typed parameter — without it, `generateContent` rejects the **entire request** with `400 INVALID_ARGUMENT` before the model ever gets to pick a tool, which broke `business_chat` tool-calling wholesale (every tool, not just the malformed one, since all `functionDeclarations` are sent together in one list). This had never surfaced before because every prior live-Gemini attempt to exercise `create_custom_widget` was blocked by the Gemini daily quota first (§16.1's own "not verified" caveat) — this session's `get_wallet_info` call was the first real `generateContent` request to actually reach Gemini's schema validation with that tool registered. Fixed by adding a proper `items: { type: 'object', properties: { blockType, props }, required: ['blockType'] }` (matching what `parseInput` already validates); confirmed by re-running the exact live call that had failed.

**Verified live, not just by code reading**:
- `curl` straight to `eth-mainnet.g.alchemy.com` with the real project key returned a real balance and a real NFT list for a real public address (vitalik.eth) before any of this code existed — confirmed the key itself works and the response shapes match what `AlchemyAdapter`'s types assume.
- A real test user/business through the real REST API: `GET .../web3/wallet` before setting an address → `{ walletAddress: null, providerConfigured: true, balance: null, nfts: [] }`; `PATCH .../businesses/:id` with the real address → `GET` afterward returned a real balance (`6.6421781652213403` ETH) and 25 real NFTs with names/images, sourced live from Alchemy, not fixtures.
- Tenant isolation: a second real user hitting the first business's wallet endpoint got a real `403`.
- Input validation: `PATCH` with a malformed address (`"not-an-address"`) got a real `400` with the DTO's message; an empty string correctly cleared the stored address back to `null`.
- **A real multi-turn Gemini conversation** (`POST .../ai/chat`, `"Какой баланс ETH и сколько NFT на моём кошельке? Используй get_wallet_info."`) actually called `get_wallet_info`, got the real balance/NFT count back, and answered in natural language quoting the exact address/balance — the full pipe (chat → Gemini function call → tool handler → `Web3Service` → live Alchemy call → result back to Gemini → final message), not a mocked or code-read trace. `AuditLog` confirmed a `low`-risk `success` row with the correct `resultSummary` for this exact call.
- `backend/npm run check` (139 tests, unchanged — no new pure-function logic worth a unit test beyond what `add-block-schemas.test.ts` already covers; `formatWeiToEth`'s wei→ETH conversion is arguably a candidate but is single-purpose enough that the live-verified real balance above already covers it more convincingly than a synthetic `BigInt` fixture would) and a real `npm run build` both clean on the backend. `frontend/npm run check` (15 tests, unchanged) and a real `next build` both clean, `/business/[id]` dashboard's new tab compiles into the existing route.
- Test users/business (and the wallet address on them) deleted after verification — no leftover rows in the dev DB, same discipline as every prior phase's live-verification sessions.

**What remains deliberately out of scope, not silently skipped**: customer-facing wallet-connect (visitor's own wallet, client-side signing, wagmi/viem) — explicitly deferred to whenever a real product need for accepting crypto payments or NFT-gated content emerges, per §2.6's own sequencing note. Multi-network support (only `eth-mainnet` by default, one `ALCHEMY_NETWORK` for the whole backend, not per-business) — narrow-v1, nobody has asked for a second chain yet. A real browser click-through (`claude-in-chrome` not connected this session) — same standing caveat as every prior phase's frontend.

### 19.1 BYOK — business-owned Alchemy API key (2026-08-31, continued)

**Superseded by §19.2** — the platform-key fallback described in this section was removed the same day, per a follow-up owner request: Web3 now works exclusively with a business's own key. Left here as the historical record of what shipped first, not the current behavior.

Per the owner's request: businesses can now set their own Alchemy API key (`Business.web3AlchemyApiKey`) instead of relying solely on the platform-wide `ALCHEMY_API_KEY`. `Web3Provider`'s three methods each grew an optional `apiKey` parameter; `AlchemyAdapter.resolveApiKey` is the single place that decides which key wins (business's own, falling back to the platform default) — `Web3Service.getWalletInfo` resolves it once per call and passes it through, so a business without its own key behaves exactly as before, and a business with one gets its own Alchemy rate limit and works even when the platform hasn't configured a key at all.

The key is write-only end to end — `BusinessDto` exposes only `hasOwnWeb3ApiKey: boolean` (never the value itself, same principle as `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY` never round-tripping to a client, just achieved differently since this one genuinely lives in the DB, not only `.env`), and `WalletInfoDto` adds `usingOwnApiKey: boolean` purely as a UI transparency signal ("using your key" vs "the platform's"). Reused the existing `UpdateBusinessDto`/`BusinessesService.update` path (empty string clears, `undefined` leaves untouched) rather than a dedicated endpoint — same precedent as `web3WalletAddress` itself.

Frontend: `Web3Section`'s edit form gained a password-type API-key input (never pre-filled, since the backend never returns the real value) plus a short "where to get one" block with a live link to `dashboard.alchemy.com/apps` and a one-line "Alchemy account → create an App on Ethereum Mainnet → copy the API Key" instruction, and a separate immediate "Удалить сохранённый ключ" action so clearing a key is a deliberate act, not an accidental side effect of leaving the field blank while editing the wallet address.

**Verified live**: `PATCH` with a real key → `hasOwnWeb3ApiKey: true`, the key itself absent from the response body; `GET .../web3/wallet` immediately afterward returned `usingOwnApiKey: true` with a real balance/NFT list sourced through that key. Clearing it (`PATCH` with `web3AlchemyApiKey: ""`) flipped `hasOwnWeb3ApiKey` back to `false` and the very next wallet fetch correctly fell back to the platform key (`usingOwnApiKey: false`), still returning live data — confirms the fallback path, not just the override path. A 201-character key correctly got a real `400` from the DTO's length check. `backend/npm run check` (154 tests, unchanged) and both `npm run build`s clean.

### 19.2 Platform-key fallback removed — Web3 is now business-key-only (2026-08-31, continued)

Per a same-day follow-up owner request: drop the platform-wide `ALCHEMY_API_KEY` entirely, both the fallback behavior and every mention of it — Web3 now works exclusively through each business's own Alchemy key (`Business.web3AlchemyApiKey`), with no shared/fallback path at all. `ALCHEMY_API_KEY` removed from `configuration.ts`/`env.validation.ts`/`.env.example` (only `ALCHEMY_NETWORK` remains — the network setting isn't a "key", stays platform-wide). `Web3Provider`'s `apiKey` parameter is no longer optional on the read methods (`getWalletBalance`/`getNftHoldings` now require a real key — `Web3Service` only calls them once it has confirmed the business has one); `AlchemyAdapter.resolveApiKey`/`platformApiKey` deleted outright, not just made unreachable. `WalletInfoDto.usingOwnApiKey` removed too — with no fallback left to distinguish, the field had nothing left to say ("using your key" vs "using the platform's" is meaningless when there is no platform key).

**Frontend**: every "используется общий ключ платформы" / "администратор ещё не подключил провайдера" string removed from `Web3Section` — replaced with a direct "нужен API-ключ Alchemy" framing that doesn't reference a platform-level alternative at all, since there isn't one. Added, per the owner's explicit ask: a line noting free-tier Alchemy keys have limited request quotas, so a business relying on one (especially one also getting hit by the AI assistant's `get_wallet_info` calls) may see it exhausted faster than expected — not a specific number (Alchemy's own free-tier limits aren't something this session can treat as stable enough to hardcode into user-facing copy), just the fact that a limit exists.

**Verified**: `backend/npm run check` (154 tests, unchanged) and a real `npm run build` clean — confirms nothing else in the codebase still referenced `config.alchemyApiKey`/`AlchemyAdapter.platformApiKey` after removal (a stale reference would have been a compile error, not a silent runtime gap). `frontend/npm run check` (15 tests, unchanged) and a real `next build` clean. Not re-run against the live Docker stack this time — no new *behavior* to verify beyond what §19/§19.1 already exercised live (a business's own key working, and the "no key" empty state), this change only removes a code path and rewrites copy; a full grep across `backend/`+`frontend/`+`AI_PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md` for `ALCHEMY_API_KEY`/"общий ключ"/"платформенн.*[Aa]lchemy" turned up nothing outside this section's own historical §19.1 text and this section's own explanation of what was removed.
