## 20. AI-8 first bounded slice (2026-08-31, continued) — Custom Database Builder v1, declarative EAV model, VERIFIED end-to-end including through a real Gemini conversation

Per §2.2's own explicit warning — arbitrary user-defined schema + real migrations is "the highest-complexity, highest-risk item in the whole mission", recommend NOT building a generic dynamic-entity engine in the first iteration — this slice applies exactly the anti-risk pattern §2.4 already proved out for Custom Widgets: a **declarative** model over two fixed, pre-existing tables, not real Postgres DDL per business. "Create an entity"/"add a field" is a JSON write, not a schema migration. Scoped to what mission §2.2 itself names: `create_entity`/`add_field` — record CRUD is included too (a schema nobody can put data into isn't a usable feature), but AI-authored *records* are explicitly not in scope this slice, matching the mission's own tool naming (only the shape is AI-authorable, not the data).

**Backend** (`modules/custom-entities`): `CustomEntity` (`businessId`, `name`, `fields: Json`) and `CustomEntityRecord` (`entityId`, `data: Json`, no own `businessId` — same "owned-through-parent" pattern as `OrderItem`/`Order`, not duplicated). `lib/custom-entity-schema.lib.ts` — pure, framework-agnostic parser/validator (`AGENTS.md` backend §6/§8: JSON-blob input, hand-written parser, unit-tested without Prisma/DI): `parseEntityFields` (initial batch, unique keys), `appendField` (**additive-only** — the same "additive, not migration" discipline already applied to `Rule.actions`/`CustomWidget.schema`: fields can only be added, never removed or renamed, so old records can never be broken by a schema change), `validateRecordData` (rejects unknown keys instead of silently dropping them, no implicit type coercion — same posture as `buildValidatedProps`/`rule-condition.lib.ts`). 4 field types day-one (`string`/`number`/`boolean`/`date`) — same "curated allowlist, narrow v1" precedent as `add-block-schemas.ts`'s original 4 block types. `CustomEntitiesService.rename` deliberately cannot touch `fields` — only `addField` can grow the schema, preventing an owner from accidentally dropping a field existing records depend on through a careless rename call.

**A real bug found and fixed during this slice's own live verification** (not by code reading): the pure `lib` functions throw plain `Error` (framework-agnostic, shared by both the AI tool's `parseInput` and the REST DTO path — same reasoning as `add-block-schemas.ts` itself throwing plain `Error`, wrapped by its *caller* into `BadRequestException` where needed). `CustomEntitiesService` initially called them without that wrapping, so a validation failure (missing required field, unknown field key) surfaced as a bare `500` instead of a `400` — caught live via curl (`POST .../records` with a missing required field returned `Internal Server Error`, not the DTO's validation message), fixed with a small `parseOrBadRequest` helper wrapping all four call sites, re-verified the same curl calls now return real `400`s with the exact validation message.

**AI tools**: `create_entity` (`medium` risk — creates a new schema; reversible, the owner can delete the entity), `add_field` (`medium` risk — grows an existing schema; additive-only so not reversible by removing, hence not `low`), `list_entities` (`low` risk, read-only — lets the model discover existing `entityId`s/keys before calling `add_field`, same "list before write" precedent as `list_media_assets` unblocking `add_block`'s `image` support). `add_field` deliberately takes `entityId` from the model (not a name lookup — ambiguous if two entities share a similar name) but never `businessId` (always `ctx.businessId`, same tenant-isolation principle as every other tool in this codebase).

**Frontend**: new `entities/custom-entity` slice + a "База данных" dashboard tab (`CustomEntitiesSection`, ungated by any capability, same principle as "Автоматизация"/"Виджеты"/"Web3"). Entity list → selecting one drills into `CustomEntityRecordsSection` (a table whose columns are generated from `entity.fields`, not hardcoded) with create/edit/delete records and an "Добавить поле" action inline. `CustomEntityRecordModal` is the one genuinely novel piece of frontend logic in this slice: a form built entirely at runtime from `entity.fields` (text/number/date/checkbox inputs chosen by `field.type`), the same "form is driven by schema, not by a fixed set of known fields" property that makes this feature a database builder rather than another domain-specific form.

**Verified live, not just by code reading**:
- Real REST CRUD through a real test business: created an entity (`Клиенты CRM`, 2 fields) → real row with the expected `fields` JSON; created a valid record → `201` with normalized `data`; a record missing the required field and one with an unknown key both correctly got real `400`s (after the `parseOrBadRequest` fix above) with the exact validation messages, not generic errors; `add_field` grew the entity to 3 fields, and the **pre-existing** record (created before that field existed) still listed fine with the old, smaller `data` shape — confirms additive-only doesn't retroactively break anything. A duplicate-key `add_field` call correctly got `400` "уже существует".
- Tenant isolation: a second real user hitting the first business's `custom-entities`/`records` endpoints got real `403`s on both.
- **A real multi-turn Gemini conversation**: `"Создай сущность «Заявки на ремонт»..."` correctly called `create_entity` and created a real 2-field entity; a follow-up turn (`"Добавь ещё одно поле... Сначала посмотри список сущностей"`) correctly called `list_entities` **then** `add_field` in sequence within one turn, using the real `entityId` it had just discovered — the full intended multi-tool pipe, not a single isolated call. `psql` confirmed the entity ended up with all 3 fields in the right order; `AuditLog` confirmed three rows (`create_entity`/`list_entities`/`add_field`) with the correct risk levels and `success` status.
- `backend/npm run check` (154 tests — 15 new for `custom-entity-schema.lib.ts`, unchanged elsewhere) and a real `npm run build` both clean. `frontend/npm run check` (15 tests, unchanged — no new pure logic on the frontend worth a unit test, the dynamic-form logic is exercised by the live click-through instead where possible) and a real `next build` both clean, `/business/[id]` dashboard's new tab compiles into the existing route.
- Test users/businesses deleted after verification — no leftover rows in the dev DB, same discipline as every prior phase.

**Not verified — `claude-in-chrome` still not connected this session, re-checked before starting and again after finishing**: an actual interactive click-through of the new "База данных" tab (creating an entity through the modal, adding a field, filling the dynamic record form). The dynamic-form logic (`CustomEntityRecordModal`) is the one piece of this slice's frontend that a backend curl trace can't exercise at all — worth a real click-through once browser tooling is available, same standing caveat as every prior phase's UI.

---

## 21. AI-9 first bounded slice (2026-08-31, continued) — HIGH/CRITICAL confirm-flow, first real high-risk tool (`publish_website`), VERIFIED end-to-end including through a real Gemini conversation

Per §2.8's own spec: "a rule that any `HIGH`/`CRITICAL` tool call returns a 'confirm?' step to the chat UI instead of executing immediately" — never exercisable before this slice because every tool shipped through AI-2/AI-5/AI-6/AI-7/AI-8 was deliberately kept `low`/`medium` (narrow-v1 discipline applied consistently across the whole initiative). This is the last not-started phase from §4's table; with it done, everything in AI-1 through AI-9 is either shipped-and-verified or an explicitly-scoped deferral, not a silent gap.

**Mechanism, reusing existing storage, not a new table**: `AuditLog.status` turned out to already be a plain Prisma `String` column, not an enum (`schema.prisma`'s own model comment already documented it that way) — so extending it with two new values, `pending` and `rejected`, needed **no migration at all**. `AuditLogService` grew `findOwnedPending`/`resolvePending` alongside the existing `record` (now returning the created row's `id`, needed to hand back a `confirmationId`). `AiService.runToolLoop`: when a tool call resolves to a `high`/`critical` `riskLevel` (checked only AFTER `parseInput` succeeds — a genuinely invalid call still gets an immediate `error`, no confirmation requested for garbage), it writes a `pending` `AuditLog` row instead of calling `tool.handler`, yields a new `AiStreamEvent` variant (`confirm_required`, carrying `confirmationId`), and **returns immediately** — the whole turn stops, not just that one tool call. Any other tool calls the model may have batched in the same turn after the blocking one are simply not attempted (a known, explicitly-scoped simplification, not an oversight — see the closing note). Two new `AiController` routes, `POST .../ai/confirm/:confirmationId` and `POST .../ai/reject/:confirmationId`, neither needing `AiConfiguredGuard` (they don't call Gemini at all — confirming executes the tool with the exact already-validated arguments that were stored at pending-time, no re-parse, no second LLM round-trip). This sidesteps a real architectural fact worth naming: `AiService.chat`/`chatStream` are genuinely stateless per call (no conversation history sent to Gemini between messages, confirmed by reading `runToolLoop` — each call starts a fresh `messages` array) — a confirm/reject flow built as "send another chat message and hope the model remembers" would not have worked at all; routing it through a stored `confirmationId` instead makes the whole mechanism independent of that limitation.

**First real `high`-risk tool — `publish_website`** (`modules/ai/tools/publish-website.tool.ts`): a thin wrapper around the already-existing `WebsitesService.publish` (the same call the dashboard's own "Опубликовать" button makes) — not a new write path, same "tool is a new caller of existing code" principle as every other tool in this codebase. `high`, not `critical`: it makes the site publicly live (real visitors, potentially real orders/appointments) but isn't destructively irreversible — re-publishing a draft overwrites the published snapshot, no data is lost, just no version history (a pre-existing `WebsitesService.publish` limitation, not new).

**A real, live-only finding, not discoverable by reading the code**: the tool's first description wording ("Требует подтверждения владельца перед выполнением" — "requires owner confirmation before execution") made Gemini ask permission in plain natural-language text instead of calling the tool at all, in 2 of 3 identical live attempts — the model reasonably but incorrectly inferred it should get confirmation itself before acting, not understanding that calling the tool triggers the system's own confirmation step automatically. Rewording the description to explicitly state "вызови этот инструмент напрямую... система сама покажет владельцу запрос на подтверждение" ("call this tool directly... the system itself will show the owner a confirmation prompt") fixed it to 3/3 across a fresh set of live attempts. Worth remembering for any future `high`/`critical` tool: describing the confirmation requirement to the model can backfire into the model performing its own, redundant, ineffective confirmation-by-text instead of the real mechanism.

**Frontend** (`features/ai-chat`): `AiChatMessage` gained an optional `pendingConfirmation` (tool/riskLevel/confirmationId/resolution) — rendered as a card with "Подтвердить"/"Отклонить" buttons while `resolution: 'awaiting'`, and a plain status line (`Выполняем…`/`Отклоняем…`/`Выполнено`/`Отклонено`/`Не удалось выполнить`) once acted on; a successful confirm still runs the same `onMutationApplied` live-canvas-refresh path as an ordinary `tool_result` (AI-3's mechanism, unchanged). `AiActivityFeed` renders `pending`/`rejected` rows distinctly (accent color + "ждёт подтверждения"/"отклонено" suffix), not just success/error. `tool-labels.ts` also picked up the 6 tools that had been falling back to their raw snake_case name in badges since AI-6/AI-7/AI-8 shipped (`create_entity`, `add_field`, `list_entities`, `create_custom_widget`, `get_wallet_info`, `list_media_assets`) plus `publish_website` — a small, cheap, directly-in-scope fix while already touching this exact file, not a separate quality-gate pass.

**Verified live, not just by code reading**, against the real running Docker stack (after regenerating the container's Prisma client volume and restarting — the same "host build vs. container watch-build race" hazard flagged in the AI-8 session recurred once here too, fixed the same way, `docker compose restart backend`):
- A real `POST .../ai/chat` with `"Опубликуй мой сайт."` returned `{ message: "", toolExecutions: [], confirmRequired: { tool: "publish_website", riskLevel: "high", confirmationId, args: {} } }` — no execution happened (`psql`: `websites.publishedAt` still `null`, `audit_logs` row `status: pending`).
- Confirming that exact id executed the real `WebsitesService.publish` — `psql` confirmed a real `publishedAt` timestamp appeared, and the `audit_logs` row flipped to `status: success` with the real `resultSummary`.
- Re-confirming the same, now-resolved id correctly got a real `404` ("Подтверждение не найдено или уже обработано") — the `status: 'pending'` filter in `findOwnedPending` naturally excludes it once resolved, no separate "already used" check needed.
- A second live turn, rejected instead: `POST .../ai/reject/:id` → `204`, `audit_logs` row `status: rejected`, and confirming that same id afterward also correctly `404`d.
- Cross-tenant: a second real user hitting the first business's `confirm` endpoint got a real `403` (`AiOwnershipGuard`, unchanged, already covers these new routes since it's applied at the controller class level).
- `chat/stream` (SSE) emits the exact same `tool_start` → `confirm_required` frame pair and then closes — confirmed via a raw `curl -N` trace of the actual event stream, not inferred from the non-streaming path.
- `backend/npm run check` (154 tests, unchanged — no new pure-function logic in this slice worth a unit test; the confirm/reject state machine is inherently Prisma/DI-bound, exercised by the live curl trace instead per `AGENTS.md` backend §8) and a real `npm run build` clean. `frontend/npm run check` (15 tests, unchanged) and a real `next build` clean.
- Test users/business deleted after verification — no leftover rows in the dev DB, same discipline as every prior phase.

**Not verified — `claude-in-chrome` still not connected this session**: an actual interactive click-through of the confirmation card (clicking "Подтвердить"/"Отклонить" in a real browser, watching the button states/status text change) — the one piece of this slice a curl trace can't exercise, same standing caveat as every prior phase's UI.

**What remains deliberately out of scope, not silently skipped**: the phase table's own "full quality-gate pass (§67) across all shipped tools" is explicitly NOT this slice — reclassifying any *existing* `medium` tool's risk level, auditing every tool's error messages/edge cases, or adding a second `high`/`critical` tool were all considered and deliberately left out, matching this whole initiative's "prove the pipe once with one real case" discipline rather than doing a speculative broad sweep. A batched turn where the model requests multiple tool calls and one of them is `high`/`critical` only processes calls up to and including the blocking one — later calls in that same batch are silently not attempted this turn (documented in `runToolLoop`'s own comment, not hidden) rather than queued for after confirmation; a real multi-call-with-confirmation-in-the-middle scenario would need this addressed, not encountered yet. No TTL/expiry on a `pending` confirmation — an old, forgotten one stays confirmable indefinitely; not a problem observed in practice, flagged as a real design gap rather than assumed away.

**What remains deliberately out of scope, not silently skipped**: AI-authored records (mission itself only names `create_entity`/`add_field`, not a record-creation tool) — a human enters data through the dashboard, same as the mission's own scoping. Field deletion/rename, and any notion of relations between entities — real product decisions with real risk (a rename could orphan data readers don't expect), left for a real request, not spec'd preemptively. AI-9 (HIGH/CRITICAL-risk confirm flows, final quality-gate pass) is now the only remaining not-started phase — it depends on AI-7/AI-8 both being done, which they now are.

## 22. `claude-in-chrome` finally connected — a real cross-phase browser verification pass, one real bug found and fixed live (2026-08-31, continued)

Per the owner's own `/chrome` connection this session — the recurring, cross-cutting gap flagged at the end of nearly every phase since AI-2 ("не проверено интерактивно, `claude-in-chrome` не подключён") is finally closable. `list_connected_browsers` found exactly one real connected browser; per this tool's own instructions the choice was put to the owner via `AskUserQuestion` rather than picked unilaterally, then `select_browser` connected it. This section covers one real click-through session spanning AI-2 through AI-9 in a single continuous flow (registered a fresh test user through the real signup form, not a seeded fixture), not a separate slice per phase — closes the standing browser-verification caveat left open by §9.5/§9.6 (AI-2 chat panel), §10.4/§10.6/§10.7 (AI-3 streaming/live-canvas/activity feed), §11.4/§11.5 (AI-4 onboarding chat + template auto-apply), §15.3 (Rules management UI), §19 (Web3 tab), §20 (Custom Database Builder tab), and §21 (AI-9 confirmation card) all at once.

**A real, self-inflicted infrastructure problem hit and fixed before any app testing could even start**: navigating to the builder route returned Chrome's own "This page couldn't load" in a fresh tab, with the console showing `ChunkLoadError: Failed to load chunk ... from module`. Root cause: a stray `next start` (**production** server) process on port 3000 — the same unexplained process §10.6 first noticed and deliberately left alone, never knowing what started it — had its `.next` build output repeatedly overwritten by this session's own `npm run build` runs (AI-7/AI-8/AI-9/BYOK verification passes), so the server was serving an HTML shell referencing chunk hashes that no longer existed on disk. Exactly the "host build vs. running server" class of hazard §15.3 already flagged once for the backend/Docker side, encountered here for the frontend too. Fixed by killing that stray process and starting a clean `npm run dev` — the correct process for interactive testing anyway. Worth remembering for any future session in this environment: **never run a frontend `npm run build` while any `next start`/`next dev` is serving port 3000** — it corrupts the running server's chunk manifest out from under it, not a real product bug when this specific error shape appears.

**Real, live click-through confirmed working, in order**:
- **Registration + AI-4 onboarding**: signed up a fresh account through the real form, typed `"Открываю кофейню "Утренний Луч" в Киеве"` into `/businesses/new`'s AI chat — a real `create_business` call landed, correctly redirected to `/business/:id/plan?template=restaurant` (the model correctly matched a coffee shop to the `restaurant` template, matching §11.5's earlier curl-only finding, now seen actually happen in a browser).
- **Payment Plans gate**: selected the Free tier via the real "Начать бесплатно" button, redirected into the builder — the plan-gate flow (a feature with no AI_PLATFORM_ROADMAP.md phase of its own, documented only in `backend/PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`) confirmed working end to end for the first time too.
- **Builder + starter template**: the `restaurant` starter template had, in fact, actually applied — a real hero section ("Добро пожаловать / Утренний Луч", the real business name interpolated), real nav (О нас/Услуги/Контакты), real CTAs (Забронировать столик/Посмотреть меню) — confirms §11.5's client-side `useEffect` template-application path, until now only confirmed by code reading.
- **AI chat panel + AI-9 confirmation card**: opened the floating chat bubble, sent `"Опубликуй мой сайт."` — got the real `confirm_required` card (`Опубликовал сайт — высокий риск, требуется подтверждение`, `Отклонить`/`Подтвердить` buttons) exactly as built in §21, purely from code + curl until now. Clicked **Подтвердить** — the card flipped to `Выполнено`, and `psql` confirmed a real `publishedAt` timestamp landed, closing §21's own last open caveat.
- **Activity tab**: switched to "История" — showed `Опубликовал сайт` and `create_business` with real timestamps, confirming `tool-labels.ts`'s label for `publish_website` renders correctly against a real audit row (not just the curl-inspected JSON shape).
- **Dashboard → Web3 tab**: real wallet-address + real Alchemy API key entered through the actual form and saved — the page re-rendered with a real balance (`6.6421781652213403 ETH`) and a real 25-item NFT grid with images actually loading from Alchemy's CDN, closing §19's last open caveat. Confirms the BYOK form (§19.1/§19.2) renders with none of the removed "общий ключ платформы" language — the live page matches the copy audit that was previously only checked by `grep`.
- **Dashboard → Custom Database ("База данных") tab**: created a real entity (`Клиенты CRM`, two fields, `string` + `number`) through the field-builder modal, then added a real record through the resulting dynamic form (`CustomEntityRecordModal` — the one piece of AI-8 a curl trace structurally cannot exercise, since it's built entirely from client-side `entity.fields`) — the record appeared correctly in the dynamically-columned table. Closes §20's last open caveat.
- **Dashboard → Automation ("Автоматизация") tab**: created a real rule (`Заказ оформлен · если totalCents = 1000 · уведомить владельца`) through the condition-builder modal — confirms this pre-AI-9 UI (§15.3, previously flagged as its own unverified click-through) still works correctly.

**One real, previously-undiscovered UI bug found and fixed live, not by code reading**: the field-builder row inside `CustomEntityFormModal` (three inputs/a select in a CSS Grid reusing `RuleFormModal.module.scss`'s `.condition` class, `grid-template-columns: 1.4fr 1fr 1fr auto`) rendered with its `type` dropdown and delete button clipped off the right edge of the modal, inaccessible — a classic CSS Grid overflow (`fr` tracks don't shrink below their content's min-content size by default; `<input>`/`<select>` elements have a non-trivial one). Never caught by any build/typecheck/test pass across the AI-8 session, only visible by actually opening the modal. Fixed with `min-width: 0` on the grid's direct children — a one-line, well-understood fix for this exact class of bug, applied to the shared SCSS file so both `CustomEntityFormModal` and `RuleFormModal`'s own condition rows benefit (the latter's row is narrower and wasn't visibly broken, but was equally exposed to the same underlying issue). Re-verified live after the fix: both modals now show their full row, including a real rule created afterward through the now-fixed layout.

**Verified**: `frontend/npm run check` (15 tests, unchanged — the fix is pure CSS, nothing to unit-test) and a real `npm run build` both clean after the fix. Test user, business, custom entity/record, and rule all deleted from the dev DB after verification — no leftovers.

**What this closes, concretely**: every phase-level "не проверено, `claude-in-chrome` не подключён" caveat from AI-2 through AI-9 is now resolved with a real, positive click-through — not just plumbing/curl/SSE verification. This was the one structural, cross-cutting gap that had persisted across the entire initiative regardless of how thoroughly any individual phase's backend was tested; it's now closed the same way every other gap in this document has been: by actually doing the thing, not by assuming it would probably work.

## 23. AI-10 — Universal Builder Expansion (2026-08-31, continued)

The owner handed over a large brief ("UNIVERSAL BUILDER EXPANSION — ANY DESIGN / ANY FUNCTIONALITY"), 26 sections asking the site builder to become a Webflow/Framer-scale platform: a full CSS/typography/animation engine, an arbitrary widget library spanning business/e-commerce/social/AI/Web3/payments/advertising/analytics, a CMS collections engine, a custom-component system, design tokens, pixel-accuracy AI design mode, a full builder tool architecture, versioning, performance, security. The brief's own §25 explicitly asks for an audit first and a REUSE/EXTEND/REFACTOR/DEPRECATE decision before any code — not a rewrite. Per this project's own narrow-v1/bounded-iteration discipline (every AI-1 through AI-9 phase above was a single small slice, verified live, before the next started), this is **not one phase** — it is a new sub-numbered track, `AI-10`/`AI-11`/`AI-12`/…, one narrow slice at a time, each independently verified the same way as every phase above. This section covers only the first slice; the rest of the brief is intentionally not scheduled yet.

### 23.1 Audit finding: the builder is already schema-driven, not template-driven

A read-only audit (block-tree model, style system, AI tool schemas, `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`/this document's own AI-6 history) found the architecture already matches most of the brief's own target shape, just narrower in scope than "universal":

| Piece | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Block model (`WebsiteBlock{id,type,props,style,hidden,children}`) | REUSE | Flat, recursive, arbitrary nesting already works |
| Block/widget registry (`BlockDefinition`/`FieldSchema`: text/textarea/richtext/number/select/color/toggle/image/url/link/dataSource/list) | REUSE | Already a component system with props, variants (via `select`), and live data binding (`dataSource`) |
| Style system (`BlockStyle`, responsive desktop/tablet/mobile, `WebsiteTheme` → `--site-*` CSS vars) | EXTEND | Real, not a stub — but values are closed enum tokens (5 background presets, 5 system font stacks, a 5-step spacing scale), not free CSS values |
| AI tool schema pattern (`add-block-schemas.ts`, `block-style-schema.ts`) | REUSE | Clean per-field validation, the right shape to widen incrementally |
| Web3 (`get_wallet_info`, AI-7) / Custom Entities (AI-8) as builder widgets | EXTEND | Real REST APIs already exist with zero site-builder exposure — wrapping them as blocks is glue, not new backend work |

Confirmed: per-block style customization already existed before this slice (`BlockStyle` + `LayoutSection.tsx` inspector, responsive-aware) — the brief's ask is to widen it, not build it from zero. The `dataBindings`/`capabilities` gap AI-6 (§14/§16) left open, blocked on "Web3/data-connector work not existing yet", is now unblocked (AI-7 shipped) — a real candidate for AI-11 or AI-12, not built in this slice.

### 23.2 This slice: `BlockStyle.background` gains a free-value `'custom'` option

Scope, deliberately narrow: `Background` gained a `'custom'` member alongside the existing `none/surface/muted/primary/dark` presets, paired with a new `BlockStyle.customBackgroundColor?: string` (`#rrggbb`, independent of theme tokens). Reuses the exact `control: 'color'` field type `ThemePanel.tsx`'s theme colors already use (a real hex-input + swatch, not new UI). `LayoutSection.tsx`'s inspector shows the color field conditionally, spliced right after "Фон" only when `background === 'custom'` — not a permanent 8th field everyone sees. Backend: `block-style-schema.ts`'s `set_style` validator (AI-2, §9.2) gained the same `'custom'` enum value plus a dedicated hex-regex branch for `customBackgroundColor` — the AI can set a custom block background through `set_style` exactly like any other style field, no new tool.

**A real, pre-existing contrast bug found and fixed in the same pass, not scope creep**: `contrastOverrides()` (`block-style.ts`) has always (since before this slice — affects the pre-existing `primary`/`dark` presets too) only overridden the `--site-text`/`--site-muted`/`--site-border` CSS *variables* on a block's wrapper, never `color` itself. Any block whose own CSS reads `color: inherit` rather than `color: var(--site-text)` directly — confirmed live with the Hero block (`business.module.scss`'s `.hero__heading { color: inherit }`) — inherits the *already-resolved* color computed once at `WebsiteRenderer`'s root, not the wrapper's overridden variable, because CSS inheritance propagates computed values, not custom-property re-reads. Result: white-on-black intent silently rendered as near-invisible dark-on-black. Root-caused live (a hero block set to a dark custom color visibly failed), not by code reading — found because this slice happened to touch exactly the code path AI-9's §22 click-through never exercised (no prior phase tested a dark/primary background on the Hero block specifically). Fixed with a one-line, backward-compatible addition: `outer` in `computeBlockWrapperStyle` now always also sets `color: 'var(--site-text)'` — for every block that had no background override before, this resolves to the exact same value as before (the default theme's `--site-text`, unchanged), so no visual regression; for an overridden wrapper, any descendant using `color: inherit` now correctly re-reads the local, overridden variable instead of the stale root-level one. Fixes the bug for `custom` **and** the pre-existing `primary`/`dark` presets project-wide, not just the new value.

**Verified**: `backend/npm run check` (157 tests, +4 new for `customBackgroundColor` validation — invalid hex, valid hex, null-removal, `'custom'` added to the background enum test) and `frontend/npm run check` (20 tests, +5 new for `computeBlockWrapperStyle`'s custom-color/contrast behavior) both clean. A real `npm run build` (stopping the live `next dev` first, per §22's own documented "never build while a dev/start server is serving the same `.next` output" lesson, then restarting it) succeeded both before and after the contrast fix. Live-verified in a real browser against the CryptoVault test business's actual Hero block: selected "Свой цвет" in the compact-mode property dropdown (window <1024px triggers `FieldGroup`'s one-property-at-a-time compact UI, not a bug — confirmed by inspecting `FieldGroup.tsx`), set `#0a0a0a`, confirmed white heading/subtitle text on both the builder canvas and the "Предпросмотр" modal (same renderer, per `BlockRenderer.tsx`'s own design), then reverted to "Без фона" and confirmed the block returned to its default appearance with no residual color — a full round trip, not just the one-direction happy path. The AI-tool path (`set_style` with `background: 'custom'`) is covered by the new backend unit tests only, not a live Gemini call — the daily Gemini quota (`GeminiQuotaService`, §1's own note on this being a deliberately unbypassed cost-control budget) was already exhausted earlier this same session from AI-7/8/9 verification traffic, a disclosed limitation, not a silently skipped check.

**Deliberately not in this slice** (candidates for AI-11/AI-12, not started): arbitrary spacing values (`SpacingSize`'s 5-step enum → free `px`/`rem`), Google Fonts / custom font loading (`FontChoice`'s 5 system stacks), gradients, and the rest of the brief's 26 sections (animation engine, widget-platform expansion beyond what AI-7/8 already exposed, CMS collections, pixel-accuracy AI design mode, etc.) — each is its own bounded slice by design, not a silent scope cut.

---

## 24. AI-11 — real Google Fonts, slice 2 of the Universal Builder Expansion track (2026-08-31, continued)

Second narrow slice of the same brief (§23), picked as the next candidate over arbitrary spacing (§23.2's "deliberately not in this slice" list) because it is a self-contained vertical slice — `FontChoice` is read in exactly two places (`WebsiteTheme.fonts.heading`/`.body`, both under `ThemePanel.tsx`), unlike `SpacingSize` which is shared across 8 responsive fields (4 basic + 4 advanced per-side) and would either need to widen all 8 at once or leak an unsupported `'custom'` option into fields with no companion UI built for it. No backend/AI-tool changes needed at all this slice — fonts are theme-level, and no AI tool sets the theme (`set_style` only touches per-block `style`, not `WebsiteTheme`).

**What shipped**: `FontChoice` gained a `'google'` value alongside the 5 existing system stacks, paired with `WebsiteTheme.fonts.googleFontHeading`/`googleFontBody: GoogleFontId`. `GoogleFontId` is a **curated list of 10 fonts** (Inter, Playfair Display, Poppins, Montserrat, Lora, Space Grotesk, DM Sans, Merriweather, Bebas Neue, Roboto — `entities/website/model/google-fonts.ts`), deliberately not a free-text font name: a free string would go straight into a `fonts.googleapis.com` URL with no way to verify it's a real font rather than junk or an injection attempt, the same curation principle already governing the 7 named theme-color roles and the 5 system font stacks. `ThemePanel.tsx`'s "Шрифты" section shows the Google Font picker conditionally, right after the heading/body select, only when that slot is actually set to `'google'` — same splice-in pattern AI-10 used for `customBackgroundColor` in `LayoutSection.tsx`.

**Loading the real webfont**: a new shared `GoogleFontLink` component (`entities/website/ui/GoogleFontLink.tsx`) renders a real `<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=...">` — `null` (nothing rendered) when no slot is set to `'google'`, so a site not using this feature makes zero extra requests, same principle FONT_STACKS's own doc-comment already stated for system stacks. Rendered in two places: `WebsiteRenderer.tsx` (covers both the public site and the Preview modal, since Preview nests `WebsiteRenderer`) and `Canvas.tsx` (the builder canvas, which has its own separate `buildThemeCssVars` call and does not nest `WebsiteRenderer`). Relies on React 19's native support for hoisting `<title>`/`<meta>`/`<link>` tags to `<head>` from anywhere in the component tree, with automatic de-duplication by `href` — confirmed this project is on React 19.2.8, and confirmed live (not just from the release notes) that the tag actually lands in `<head>` and the font actually loads (see Verified below). No changes needed to page-level `generateMetadata`/layout files.

**Verified**: `frontend/npm run check` (30 tests, +10 new — `google-fonts.test.ts` for `googleFontFamily`/`googleFontsStylesheetUrl`'s null/single/multi/de-dup cases, `theme-tokens.test.ts` for `buildThemeCssVars` resolving system stacks vs. a chosen Google Font vs. `'google'` with no font picked yet vs. heading/body picking two different Google Fonts independently) clean, plus a real `npm run build` (dev server stopped first, same §22 discipline as AI-10). Live-verified in a real browser against CryptoVault's Hero block: picked "Google Font…" for the heading, selected "Playfair Display", confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that the heading's actual `font-family` resolved to `"Playfair Display", serif` (not just the CSS variable — the literal computed value used for rendering), confirmed a real `<link>` to `fonts.googleapis.com` was present in the document, and confirmed `document.fonts` (the browser's own `FontFaceSet`) contained an actually-loaded "Playfair Display" face — not just a CSS declaration pointing at an unfetched font. Verified in the Preview modal too (same `WebsiteRenderer`, real Playfair Display rendering visible). Reverted to the default "Витринный серф" system stack afterward and confirmed a clean round trip — computed `font-family` back to `Georgia, "Times New Roman", serif`, no residual Google Font link or variable.

**Deliberately not in this slice**: arbitrary/free-text font names (curated list only, see above for why), font-weight/style selection beyond the fixed weight sets baked into each curated font's `googleParam`, and — still open from §23.2 — arbitrary spacing values and the rest of the brief's 26 sections. AI-12 (arbitrary spacing) remains the next candidate, not started.

---

## 25. AI-12 — arbitrary pixel spacing, slice 3 of the Universal Builder Expansion track (2026-08-31, continued)

Third narrow slice of the same brief (§23). §24 explicitly flagged this as the harder of the two remaining candidates and picked Google Fonts first for exactly that reason: `SpacingSize` (the 5-preset padding/margin scale) is read in **8 places** — `paddingY`/`paddingX`/`marginTop`/`marginBottom` (the "Просто" tab) plus `paddingTop`/`paddingRight`/`paddingBottom`/`paddingLeft` (the "Дополнительно" per-side tab, each also `| null` for "inherit from the pair") — not 1 (`background`) or 2 (`fonts.heading`/`.body`) like the prior two slices. Naively adding a `'custom'` sentinel to the shared `SPACING_SIZE_OPTIONS` array (reused by all 8 fields) would have leaked an unsupported option into 7 fields with no companion UI built for them.

### 25.1 Design: widen the value slot itself, not the enum

Instead of a sentinel + companion field (the AI-10/AI-11 pattern), this slice widens what the padding/margin *slots themselves* can hold: a new type in `types.ts`,

```ts
export type SpacingValue = SpacingSize | number;
```

A `number` means literal pixels (`18` → `18px`), distinguishable from a preset by `typeof` alone — no separate toggle, no second field. `BlockStyle`'s 8 fields all changed from `StyleValue<SpacingSize>`/`StyleValue<SpacingSize | null>` to `StyleValue<SpacingValue>`/`StyleValue<SpacingValue | null>`. Resolution is one shared helper in `block-style.ts`:

```ts
function spacingToPx(value: SpacingValue | undefined): string | undefined {
  if (value === undefined) return undefined;
  return typeof value === 'number' ? `${value}px` : SPACING_PX[value];
}
```

`resolveSide` (the "Дополнительно" per-side inherit-from-pair logic) and the 4 basic `readResponsiveProp` reads widened from `SpacingSize` to `SpacingValue` generically — no field-specific branching needed, since `readResponsiveProp`/`writeResponsiveProp` were already fully generic over `T`.

**A real bug caught before it shipped, not after**: the pre-existing code computed padding/margin as `top ? SPACING_PX[top] : undefined` — a truthy check that happened to work only because every `SpacingSize` member is a non-empty string (`'none'` included). The moment `number` became a valid value, `0` (a legitimate "zero custom padding") is falsy in JavaScript and would have silently collapsed to `undefined` — the exact same class of bug §23.2 found and fixed for `contrastOverrides`, this time caught during implementation rather than by a live test surfacing it. Fixed by making `spacingToPx` the single point of truth (`value === undefined` is the only early-return condition), replacing all 6 `? SPACING_PX[x] : undefined` call sites in `computeBlockWrapperStyle`. Locked in with a dedicated unit test (`renders a custom 0px spacing, not the same as "no value set"`) and a live browser check.

### 25.2 UI: one new control, not 8 bespoke ones

A new `FieldSchema` variant, `{ control: 'spacing'; options: SelectOption[] }` (`registry.ts`), reuses the same `options` shape as `'select'` — `LayoutSection.tsx`'s `BASIC_FIELDS` (4 fields) and `PADDING_SIDE_FIELDS` (4 fields) just changed `control: 'select'` → `control: 'spacing'`, no other changes needed there. `FieldControl.tsx` renders it as a `<select>` (the field's normal preset options plus one synthetic "Свой (px)" option) paired with a conditionally-shown `<input type="number">` — visually the same two-part row pattern AI-10 already established for `customBackgroundColor`, just reusing `typeof value === 'number'` as the switch instead of a separate boolean flag. Picking "Свой (px)" calls `onChange(0)` to enter custom mode (a sane, visible starting value, not an empty/invalid state); typing in the number input calls `onChange(Number(...))` directly — the select and number input are simultaneously visible and interactive, so switching back to a preset at any time is just picking a different select option, no separate "cancel custom mode" affordance needed.

### 25.3 Backend: `set_style` tool widened, bounded

`block-style-schema.ts`'s `buildValidatedStyle` gained a numeric branch for exactly the 4 basic fields this tool already scoped itself to (`paddingY`/`paddingX`/`marginTop`/`marginBottom` — the per-side "Дополнительно" fields were already explicitly out of this tool's scope before this slice, per its own doc-comment, and stay out now). A number is accepted only if `Number.isFinite` and within `0–400` — AI-authored input is untrusted (§3), and an unbounded value could push a block's spacing far enough to visibly break the page layout. `set-style.tool.ts`'s parameter description updated to mention the numeric option and its bound.

### 25.4 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (162 tests, +5 new: numeric px accepted, `0` accepted and distinguished from "no value", out-of-range/`Infinity` rejected, a non-numeric non-enum string still rejected, a number rejected for a non-spacing field) and `frontend/npm run check` (36 tests, +6 new: numeric paddingY/paddingX/marginTop/marginBottom render as literal px, the `0`-regression case, a preset still resolves normally, a numeric override on one advanced side correctly leaves the others inheriting the pair, and the fully-unset case stays `undefined`) both clean. A real `npm run build` (dev server stopped first, same §22 discipline as every prior slice) succeeded. Live-verified in the browser against CryptoVault's Hero block: switched "Отступы сверху/снизу" to "Свой (px)" (confirmed it defaults to `0`, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that the real ancestor wrapper rendered `paddingTop: 0px` — the exact regression case, not just unit-tested), set it to `60`, confirmed the wrapper's computed `paddingTop` was `60px`, then switched back to the `lg` preset and confirmed `56px` rendered — a full round trip through both directions, not just the one-way happy path.

### 25.5 What remains of the brief

AI-10/AI-11/AI-12 close the "enum-token `BlockStyle`/`WebsiteTheme` fields → free values" thread the brief's §1 opened (background, fonts, spacing) — gradients, the animation/interaction/state-system sections, the wider widget-platform expansion beyond what AI-7/AI-8 already exposed, CMS collections, a custom-component system, pixel-accuracy AI design mode, and the rest of the brief's 26 sections remain unscheduled, each its own future bounded slice, not a silent scope cut.

---

## 26. AI-13 — first real Web3 block for the site builder (2026-08-31, continued)

A different kind of slice from AI-10/AI-11/AI-12: not another widening of the style-engine enums, but the widget-library-expansion candidate §23.1's audit flagged directly: *"Web3 (`get_wallet_info`, AI-7) / Custom Entities (AI-8) as builder widgets: EXTEND — real REST APIs already exist with zero site-builder exposure — wrapping them as blocks is glue, not new backend work."* This slice tests that claim for Web3 specifically — it turned out to be *mostly* glue, with one real, deliberate piece of new backend work: the dashboard's `GET /businesses/:businessId/web3/wallet` (AI-7, §19) is owner-only (`SessionAuthGuard`); a block on the actual public site needs an anonymous visitor to see the same data, which required a genuinely new, not just wrapped, endpoint.

### 26.1 Backend: a new anonymous endpoint, not a repurposed one

`Web3Service.getWalletInfo(businessId, ownerId)` (AI-7) checks `business.ownerId === ownerId` before returning anything — the wrong shape for a public block. Added `Web3Service.getWalletInfoPublic(businessId)`, sharing the actual balance/NFT-fetching logic via a new private `resolveWalletInfo(walletAddress, apiKey)` helper (both methods now just look up the business, then delegate) — no duplicated Alchemy-calling logic between the owner and public paths. Wired into `PublicSitesController` as `GET /sites/:businessId/web3/wallet`, the same anonymous-controller pattern as `getPublicProducts`/`getPublicServices` (§0.2's audit already established this controller as the one genuinely anonymous entry point in the whole backend). The business's Alchemy API key is used server-side to call Alchemy and is never included in the response — `WalletInfoDto` never carried it, so this required no new redaction logic, just reuse of a DTO shape that was already safe to expose.

**Real, deliberate deviation from the `getPublicProducts` precedent, not an oversight**: this new route has a `@Throttle({ limit: 20, ttl: 60_000 })` that `getPublicProducts`/`getPublicServices` don't have. Those two read from this app's own Postgres — cheap, and abuse just means extra DB load on infrastructure this project controls. This endpoint calls out to Alchemy's API on every single request, against **the business's own** API key and **their own** free-tier quota (the same quota §19.2's BYOK design deliberately made per-business, not platform-shared) — unthrottled anonymous traffic to a business's public page could exhaust a real third party's rate limit that has nothing to do with this app's own infrastructure. No caching layer was built this slice (a `GET` still hits Alchemy fresh every time within the throttle window) — a known, disclosed gap for a future slice, not a silently accepted one: a business getting real traffic on a page with this block would benefit from a short server-side cache (e.g. 30–60s) shared across all visitors, cutting Alchemy calls dramatically without staling the data in any way a visitor would notice.

### 26.2 Frontend: one new block, following the `productgrid` blueprint exactly

`entities/website/blocks/web3/index.tsx` — `web3wallet`, category `business` (no `capability` gate: the dashboard's own Web3 tab isn't gated behind `Business.capabilities` either, so the block follows the same everyone-sees-it-but-gracefully-empty-until-configured convention). `entities/web3/api/get-public-wallet-info.ts` — a `'use server'` Server Action calling the new anonymous route, mirroring `entities/product/api/get-public-products.ts` exactly. The renderer fetches via its own `useAsyncData`, same as `ProductGridRenderer` — nothing about the wallet is stored in `block.props`, only presentation config (`eyebrow`/`heading`/`description`/`nftLimit`).

Five graceful states, reasoned through against `Web3Section.tsx`'s already-proven state machine rather than invented fresh: loading, fetch error, `!walletAddress` ("владелец ещё не подключил кошелёк" — visible to real visitors too, matching `productgrid`'s own precedent of showing an "add data in the dashboard" message to anyone, not just the owner), `!providerConfigured` ("нужен API-ключ Alchemy"), `data.error` (a real Alchemy call failure), and the real-data state (shortened address, ETH balance, NFT count, an NFT grid capped by `nftLimit` reusing the `simple-card`/`simple-grid` primitives `productgrid` already established). Only the real-data state was exercised live this slice (see below) — the four placeholder states were reasoned through against the already-live-verified dashboard equivalent, not independently triggered, a disclosed scope limit rather than an assumed one.

### 26.3 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (162 tests, unchanged — this slice's new logic is a thin service/controller wrapper, not new pure-function validation worth a dedicated unit test, matching AI-7's own precedent of verifying this Prisma-touching service live rather than with mocks) and `frontend/npm run check` (36 tests, unchanged, same reasoning for the new block/API call) both clean, plus a real `npm run build` (dev server stopped first, same §22 discipline). Live-verified end to end against the CryptoVault test business's own real, already-configured wallet (address `0xd8dA...6045`, real Alchemy key): a direct anonymous `curl` to the new endpoint returned real balance/NFT data with no session cookie at all; added the `Кошелёк Web3` block through the builder's "Блоки" search (found instantly under the "Бизнес" category); confirmed on the canvas it rendered the real `6.6421781652213403 ETH` balance, `25` NFT count, and an NFT grid with real images loading from Alchemy's CDN (`vitalik.wei`, `vitalik.cloud`, a Bored Ape Nike Club piece) alongside correct placeholder icons for the 2 NFTs with no metadata; confirmed the identical result in the Preview modal (same `WebsiteRenderer`).

### 26.4 What remains of the brief

Confirms §23.1's audit call was right: Custom Entities (AI-8) as a builder widget is the same shape of work (an anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId/entities/:entityId/records`-style route plus a data-driven block), not built this slice. Response caching for this endpoint (§26.1) is a real, named follow-up. The rest of the original 26-section brief — gradients, animation/interaction system, wider e-commerce/social/AI/payments/advertising widget categories, CMS collections, custom-component system, pixel-accuracy AI design mode — remains unscheduled.

---

## 27. AI-14 — gradient backgrounds, slice 4 of the style-engine track (2026-08-31, continued)

Fourth slice of the AI-10/11/12 style-engine track (AI-13 was a one-off widget-library detour in between). Closes the original brief's §1.7 "Background Engine" ask for gradients — specifically **linear, two-stop gradients**, a deliberately narrow v1: not radial/conic, not 3+ color stops, not multiple background layers, matching the same "one concrete piece at a time" discipline as every prior slice in this track.

### 27.1 Design: same shape as AI-10's custom color, one field wider

`Background` gained `'gradient'` alongside the existing `'custom'`. `BlockStyle` gained three new optional fields:

```ts
gradientFrom?: string;   // #rrggbb
gradientTo?: string;     // #rrggbb
gradientAngle?: number;  // 0-360, defaults to 135 if unset
```

`backgroundValue()` (`block-style.ts`) renders `linear-gradient(${angle}deg, ${from}, ${to})` — returns `undefined` (no background) if either stop is still unset, the same "an incomplete custom choice fails safe to nothing, not a broken CSS value" principle `customBackgroundColor` already established. `LayoutSection.tsx`'s inspector shows all three fields spliced in right after "Фон", only when `background === 'gradient'` — literally the same `basicItems.splice(1, 0, ...)` pattern AI-10 wrote for `customBackgroundColor`, just with three items instead of one; no new mechanism needed.

**Contrast rule, extended deliberately conservatively**: `contrastOverrides()` already decided light-vs-dark text via `isDarkColor()` for `'custom'`. For `'gradient'`, light text now applies only when **both** `gradientFrom` and `gradientTo` are dark. A gradient that's dark on one end and light on the other has no single text color that reads correctly across its whole surface — rather than guess (average the two colors' luminance, pick based on the midpoint, etc.), the conservative choice is the same one already used for an unset/malformed custom color: don't override, leave the default dark text, which is at least correct on the lighter portion. A real design limitation for a genuinely half-and-half gradient, not silently glossed over — noted here rather than solved with a heuristic likely to be wrong as often as right.

### 27.2 Backend: `set_style` widened, same bounding discipline as AI-12

`block-style-schema.ts`: `background`'s enum gained `'gradient'`; `gradientFrom`/`gradientTo` validate via the same `HEX_COLOR_RE` branch `customBackgroundColor` already used (now one shared branch keyed on `key === 'customBackgroundColor' || key === 'gradientFrom' || key === 'gradientTo'`); `gradientAngle` is a new numeric branch bounded to `0–360`, the same "untrusted AI input needs a sane bound, not just a type check" reasoning as AI-12's `0–400px` spacing cap. A minor implementation note worth recording: the first attempt used `HEX_COLOR_FIELD_KEYS.has(key)` (a `Set` lookup) instead of literal `key === '...'` equality checks for the three-way hex-field branch — this compiles fine at the call site but does **not** narrow TypeScript's type for `key` on subsequent branches the way a literal comparison inside an `if` does before a `continue`, so the later `ENUM_STYLE_FIELDS[key]` lookup stopped type-checking. Caught immediately by `tsc --noEmit` before it ever reached a build or commit — fixed by reverting to literal equality checks, the same style the file already used for `customBackgroundColor`.

### 27.3 A real infrastructure issue hit mid-slice, not a code bug

After the owner asked to stop the dev server (`localhost:3000`), restarting it for this slice's live verification silently came up on **port 3001** instead, with Next.js logging *"Port 3000 is in use by an unknown process"*. `ss -ltnp` showed the actual culprit: a `next-server` worker process from an *earlier* `npm run dev` in this same session was still `LISTEN`-ing on port 3000, despite `pkill -f "next dev"` having reported success. Root cause: `pkill -f "next dev"` matches the command line of the `sh -c "next dev"` wrapper and the `node .../next/dist/bin/next dev` process, but Next.js's dev server sometimes forks a separate `next-server` child process whose command line doesn't contain the string `"next dev"` at all — that child survives the pattern-matched kill and keeps holding the port. This is the same underlying class of issue §22 first documented (a stray, hard-to-explain process squatting on port 3000), recurring for a now-understood, not mysterious, reason. Fixed by finding the actual `LISTEN`-ing PIDs directly (`ss -ltnp | grep :3000`) and killing those, rather than trusting a command-line pattern match. **Worth remembering for any future session**: after `pkill -f "next dev"`, verify with `ss -ltnp` (or `lsof -i :3000`) that the port is actually free before assuming the next `npm run dev` will land on 3000 — a silent fallback to 3001 means every subsequent `curl`/browser check against port 3000 is silently talking to a stale process, not the one just started.

### 27.4 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (166 tests, +4 new: gradient with valid hex stops and an angle, invalid hex on either stop rejected, angle out of `0–360` rejected including a non-numeric value, gradient fields removable via `null`) and `frontend/npm run check` (40 tests, +4 new: renders the exact `linear-gradient()` string with a given angle, defaults to 135° when angle is unset, renders no background when a stop is missing, and the both-dark/mixed contrast distinction) both clean. A real `npm run build` succeeded (dev server correctly stopped first this time, verified via `ss -ltnp` this time, not just `ps`/`pkill`, per §27.3's lesson). Live-verified in the browser against CryptoVault's Hero block, working around this session's own screenshot-capture flakiness (Chrome's CDP `Page.captureScreenshot` intermittently timed out — unrelated to the app, `getComputedStyle` calls through the same connection worked every time) by asserting on `getComputedStyle` directly instead of relying on visual screenshots for most of the pass: set `#ff6b6b`→`#4ecdc4` at the default 135°, confirmed the exact computed `background-image`; changed the angle to `90` and confirmed the computed value updated; set both stops to near-black and confirmed the heading's computed `color` switched to white; reverted to `background: 'none'` and confirmed a clean return to the default dark text with no residual gradient.

### 27.5 What remains of the brief

Radial/conic gradients, multi-stop gradients, and multiple background layers (all named in the brief's §1.7) remain out of scope for this narrow v1, along with everything else not yet scheduled: the animation/interaction/state systems, wider e-commerce/social/AI/payments/advertising widget categories, CMS collections, custom-component system, and pixel-accuracy AI design mode.

---

## 28. AI-15 — radial gradients, slice 5 of the style-engine track (2026-09-01)

Small, fast follow-on to AI-14: the two-stop gradient work landed linear-only; this slice adds the other named option from the brief's §1.7 ("linear gradients, radial gradients"), radial specifically (conic remains out of scope, still not asked for by name in any concrete way that would justify it yet).

**Design**: `BlockStyle.gradientType?: 'linear' | 'radial'`, defaulting to `'linear'` when unset — every existing AI-14 document (in this dev DB, there's exactly one: CryptoVault's Hero block, and it was `'none'` again by the end of that slice anyway) keeps rendering identically with zero migration. `backgroundValue()` branches on it: `'radial'` → `radial-gradient(circle, ${from}, ${to})`, anything else → the existing linear branch unchanged. `LayoutSection.tsx` adds one more spliced-in field (`gradientType`, a `control: 'select'` with `GRADIENT_TYPE_OPTIONS` — "Линейный"/"Радиальный") right after the background picker, and the `gradientAngle` field is now added to the splice conditionally — only when `gradientType` resolves to `'linear'` (the default when unset) — so picking "Радиальный" makes the angle control disappear from the inspector entirely rather than sitting there doing nothing. Backend: `block-style-schema.ts` gained a `gradientType` branch validating against `['linear', 'radial']`, and `STYLE_FIELD_KEYS`/`set_style`'s description text updated to match.

**A third occurrence of the recurring stray-port-3000 process**, hit again restarting the dev server for this slice's live verification — `ss -ltnp` showed a `next start` (production) process already listening, started before this slice began and not attributable to any command run in this session. Same fix as §27.3 (find the real `LISTEN` PID via `ss -ltnp`, kill that, not a `pkill` pattern match) — no new investigation needed since the method was already known, but worth noting this has now recurred three separate times (§22, §27.3, here) with no session ever identifying what actually starts it. A real, unresolved environmental mystery — see §29 for the explicit handoff note.

**Verified**: `backend/npm run check` (168 tests, +2: valid `gradientType` values accepted, an invalid one rejected) and `frontend/npm run check` (42 tests, +2: a radial gradient renders `radial-gradient(circle, ...)` and ignores a set angle; an explicit `'linear'` still renders the angled linear form) both clean. A real `npm run build` succeeded (port confirmed free via `ss -ltnp` before building, not just `pkill`). Live-verified in the browser against CryptoVault's Hero block — this time including an actual screenshot, not just DOM assertions (Chrome's screenshot capture, flaky throughout AI-14's verification pass, worked normally again this time): picked "Радиальный", set two bright stops, confirmed both via `getComputedStyle` (`radial-gradient(circle, rgb(255, 217, 61), rgb(255, 107, 157))`) and visually via screenshot that a real circular gradient rendered behind the Hero content, then reverted to `background: 'none'` and confirmed a clean return to the default appearance.

## 29. Known open items — handoff for future agents (2026-09-01)

Written at the owner's explicit request, after AI-10 through AI-15 closed out this session's run through the "Universal Builder Expansion" brief's style-engine and widget-expansion threads. This section is a **consolidated, standalone map of everything genuinely still open across the whole AI initiative** — not just the builder track — so a future agent (or the owner) can pick up any thread without re-reading the entire document history above. Each item names its current state and, where relevant, why it wasn't done this session (scope, an owner decision needed, or a real blocker) rather than left as an unexplained gap.

### 29.1 Universal Builder Expansion track (AI-10 → AI-15 shipped this session)

What's done: custom background colors (AI-10), Google Fonts (AI-11), arbitrary pixel spacing (AI-12), a real Web3 widget (AI-13), linear + radial gradient backgrounds (AI-14/AI-15). What's still open from the original 26-section brief, roughly in order of how tractable each looks given the current architecture:

- **Custom Entities (AI-8) as a public site widget** — the same shape of work as AI-13's Web3 widget (an anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId/entities/:entityId/records`-style endpoint + a data-driven block), flagged as the natural next candidate in §26.4 but **not started**. Real design question before writing code: unlike products/services (inherently public "what we sell") or a wallet address (already-public blockchain data the owner explicitly opts to show), a `CustomEntity` is general-purpose storage that could easily hold private data (a "Клиенты CRM" entity, tested earlier this session, is exactly that) — exposing records publicly needs an explicit, owner-controlled, default-`false` visibility flag (e.g. `CustomEntity.isPublic`, a real schema migration) before any public endpoint can safely exist. Don't build the public endpoint without that flag.
- **Conic/multi-stop gradients** — AI-14/AI-15 covered linear/radial two-stop only, matching the brief's own named examples; conic and 3+ color stops remain unbuilt, low priority unless specifically requested.
- **Multiple background layers, background images/patterns/noise/mesh gradients** — §1.7 of the brief named these; none built. A real image-as-block-background feature already partially exists via the Hero block's own `backgroundImage` prop (see `blocks/business/index.tsx`) — worth checking whether that pattern generalizes to `BlockStyle` before building a parallel mechanism.
- **Border & shadow system (§1.6)** — theme-level `cardBorder`/`cardShadow` already exist (Phase 11, pre-AI-initiative) but are theme-wide, not per-block. Per-block border/shadow controls are unbuilt.
- **Size engine (§1.5)** — width/height/min/max/aspect-ratio per block: unbuilt.
- **Typography engine beyond font family (§1.1)** — font-size/line-height/letter-spacing/text-transform/text-decoration per block: unbuilt. `SectionHeading`'s heading size already has *some* responsive control via existing block props (see `heading.size` mentioned in `add-block-schemas.ts`), but nothing at the generic `BlockStyle` level the way color/spacing/font-family now are.
- **Effects (§1.8)** — opacity/blur/backdrop-filter/grayscale/mix-blend-mode: unbuilt.
- **State system (§2)** — per-component hover/focus/active/disabled style overrides: unbuilt, a genuinely large feature (would need a new style-storage shape, not just widened enums).
- **Animation/interaction system (§4/§5)** — transitions, keyframes, triggers/actions: entirely unbuilt, the largest single remaining piece of the original brief.
- **Custom-component system, design tokens beyond the current theme, component variants (§10/§11/§20)** — the theme (colors/fonts/radius/etc.) already *is* a real, if narrow, design-token system; a user-authorable component/variant system on top of it is unbuilt.
- **CMS/Collections engine (§9)** — Custom Entities (AI-8) cover the data-storage half; a generic "define a collection, bind it to a page template" authoring flow on top is unbuilt.
- **Pixel-accuracy AI design mode, screenshot-based design matching (§16/§17)** — entirely unbuilt; would need a render→screenshot→compare→refine loop the current AI tool architecture doesn't have pieces of yet.
- **Wider e-commerce/social/AI/payments/advertising widget categories (§7.2–§7.7)** — Commerce/Booking/Blog blocks already exist from before this AI initiative; Web3 is now a widget (AI-13); social/payments/advertising blocks specifically are unbuilt.

### 29.2 Pre-existing AI-1 → AI-9 loose ends (not touched this session)

- **AI-6's `dataBindings`/`capabilities` for `CustomWidget`** — blocked on Web3/data-connector work at the time (§14/§16), which is now unblocked (AI-7 shipped, and AI-13 proved the "wrap an existing service as public data" pattern works). Wiring `CustomWidget` to bind to Web3 or Custom Entity data is a real, tractable next slice — genuinely unblocked, just not picked up yet.
- **AI-9's "full quality-gate pass across all shipped tools" (§21's own closing note)** — explicitly out of scope for AI-9's first slice. Would mean auditing every tool's risk-level correctness, error-message quality, and edge cases across the whole tool registry — a real, sizeable audit task, not started.
- **Batched multi-tool-call-with-confirmation** (§21) — when a model requests several tool calls in one turn and one is `high`/`critical`, `runToolLoop` stops at that call; later calls in the same batch are silently not attempted (documented in code, not hidden, but not handled either — they aren't queued for after confirmation). Not encountered in practice yet, but a real gap if it ever is.
- **No TTL on pending confirmations** (§21) — an old, forgotten `pending` `AuditLog` row stays confirmable indefinitely. Not observed as a real problem, but a real design gap.
- **AI-8: no field deletion/renaming** — `CustomEntity.fields` is additive-only by design (§20), matching the project's schema-safety discipline; if the owner ever wants real field removal, that's a deliberate product decision to make, not an oversight to silently fix.
- **AI-7's response caching for the new public Web3 endpoint** (§26.1) — every anonymous page view with a `web3wallet` block currently calls Alchemy fresh; a short (30–60s) server-side cache shared across visitors would cut real API usage significantly with no visible staleness. Named, not started.

### 29.3 Environmental/infrastructure issues (not code bugs — worth a future session's attention)

- **The stray `next start` (production) process on port 3000 has now recurred FOUR times** (§22, §27.3, §28, §32.3) across multiple sessions, with **no session ever identifying what actually starts it**. It is not started by any command any session has run — confirmed each time by checking command history before killing it. This is no longer an occasional fluke — it has now happened in nearly every session that touches the frontend dev server. **Strongly worth asking the owner directly**: is there a cron job, a systemd service, an IDE run-configuration (WebStorm, visible in `ss -ltnp` output alongside this process, is already running on this machine — worth checking its run configurations specifically), or some other tool that launches `next start` in this project directory? The recurring workaround (`ss -ltnp | grep :3000`, kill the actual `next-server` PID, not just `pkill -f "next dev"`) is well-documented and reliable, but the root cause is still genuinely unknown after four occurrences.
- **`git push` fails every single time this entire session** (`fatal: could not read Username for 'https://github.com'`) — no stored HTTPS credentials in this environment. Every commit this session (15 and counting, starting from AI-7) is local-only as a result. Needs either the owner configuring credentials in this environment, or the owner running `git push` themselves from a machine that has them.
- **`prisma migrate dev`/`--create-only` fails with `P3006`** on shadow-DB replay because of one older migration — doesn't affect the real, already-applied migration history, workaround documented in `backend/AGENTS.md` §9, but which specific migration causes it has never been root-caused.

### 29.4 Non-AI, adjacent product gaps (found along the way, not part of this initiative's own scope)

- **Payment Plans' commission/advertising infrastructure is marketing copy only** — the gate and tier-switching UI genuinely work, but real Commerce-sales commission withholding needs Stripe Connect (not built, currently one platform Stripe account serves everyone), and the entire advertising-slot/revenue-share subsystem described in the tier cards doesn't exist as code at all.

### 29.5 Suggested entry point for the next session

If picking up the builder-expansion thread specifically: **AI-6's `dataBindings` for `CustomWidget`** (§29.1's first bullet) is the most clearly-scoped, fully-unblocked next slice — it closes a named gap from three phases ago rather than opening new brief territory, and reuses AI-13's exact "wrap an existing service as a public/bindable data source" shape. If picking up general project health instead: the stray-process mystery (§29.3) is worth 10 minutes with the owner before it recurs a fourth time.

## 30. AI-16 — per-block text color, per the owner's "every widget customizable" request (2026-09-01, continued)

The owner's own words: *"нужно сделать так чтобы каждый виджет был кастомизируемым, не только глобальные стили сайта"* ("every widget needs to be customizable, not just the site's global styles"). Two real, different interpretations exist for that sentence, and this slice deliberately picked one — worth stating plainly rather than silently assuming, since the other interpretation is real future work, not a rejected idea:

1. **Universal per-block-instance overrides** (what this slice built): every block, regardless of type, gets more style properties it can override beyond the site-wide theme — the exact mechanism AI-10 (background) through AI-15 (gradients) already established and proved out four times over. This directly answers "not just global site styles," since `BlockStyle` already IS the per-block (not global) customization layer — it just didn't yet cover text color.
2. **Per-widget-type deep customization** (not built this slice): each block TYPE exposing its own specific visual choices as configurable fields — e.g. icon color specifically for `RepeatableIconCards`, a card-style variant for the pricing block, button color specifically within the Hero's own CTA. This is a fundamentally different, much larger shape of work: touching each of the ~13 block category files individually rather than one shared mechanism, and the original brief's "Component Variants" (§20) is really asking for this second thing.

This slice built (1) because it's the direct, immediate, low-risk answer that's proven to compound (every future universal property — border, shadow, opacity — slots into the same mechanism for free), and it was ambiguous which the owner meant. If (2) is what's actually wanted, that's real, unscheduled future work — flagging it here rather than guessing wrong silently.

### 30.1 What shipped

`BlockStyle.textColor?: string` (hex) — a fourth field alongside `customBackgroundColor`/`gradientFrom`+`gradientTo`/(now `gradientType`), added as a permanent (not conditionally-spliced) entry in `LayoutSection.tsx`'s `BASIC_FIELDS`, right after `textAlign` — unlike the background-family fields, text color isn't conditional on another field's value, so it's always available, defaulting to empty (inherit from theme). `block-style.ts`'s `contrastOverrides()` now checks `blockStyle.textColor` **first**, before any of the existing dark-background heuristics — an explicit user choice always wins over an automatic guess, and (deliberately) only overrides `--site-text`, not `--site-muted`/`--site-border`, since this is a text-color choice, not a full re-theme of the block. `set_style`'s validator and description text widened the same way as the three prior color fields.

### 30.2 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (171 tests, +3: valid textColor accepted, invalid rejected, null removes it) and `frontend/npm run check` (46 tests, +4: applies as `--site-text`, wins over the dark-background auto-contrast heuristic, falls back to the heuristic when unset, and an empty string is treated as unset) both clean. A real `npm run build` succeeded (port 3000 confirmed free via `ss -ltnp` first). Live-verified against CryptoVault's Hero block: set `#e63946`, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that both the heading (`<h1>`) and description (`<p>`) picked it up — both read `--site-text` — while the "РЯДОМ С ВАМИ" eyebrow (reads `--site-primary`, unaffected by design) and the "Позвонить" button (its own fixed button styling) correctly did not change; screenshot confirmed the same visually; cleared the field and confirmed the heading returned to the theme's default `#0f172a`.

### 30.3 What this closes, and what it doesn't

Closes the immediate, literal gap the owner named for interpretation (1): text color joins background/spacing/gradient as a real per-block override, not a global-only theme setting. Border width/color, shadow, and opacity are the natural next slices under the exact same mechanism (§29.1 already named these as open) — each is now a small, well-understood addition given four prior examples of the same pattern.

### 30.4 What "every widget customizable" would still need, under interpretation (2)

If the owner's intent was closer to interpretation (2) above, the honest next step is a real audit — not a guess — of which per-widget-type visual choices are currently hardcoded across the ~13 block category files (`blocks/business`, `blocks/content`, `blocks/commerce`, `blocks/booking`, `blocks/media`, etc.) and worth exposing as fields: candidates spotted in passing this session include the Hero block's button style (currently always the theme's global `buttonStyle`), icon color in `RepeatableIconCards`, and card-style variants across the various card-grid blocks. This is real, unscheduled work — a future session should ask the owner directly which specific widgets/properties matter most before picking a first slice, rather than guessing across 13 files speculatively.

## 31. A second, much larger brief — reality check against a full builder rewrite (2026-09-01, continued)

The owner handed over a second, larger brief titled "ПОЛНОСТЬЮ ПЕРЕРАБОТАТЬ КОНСТРУКТОР В УНИВЕРСАЛЬНЫЙ AI-FIRST VISUAL WEBSITE BUILDER" — a from-scratch rewrite proposal: a new `Widget = {id, type, content, structure, styles, responsiveStyles, states, interactions}` schema, a unified style engine (`layout/size/spacing/typography/colors/background/borders/effects/animation`), Simple/Advanced UI modes, a visual nested-box spacing editor, color/border/shadow visual editors, a states system (hover/focus/active/disabled), an animation system, drag-and-drop with snap lines/smart guides, a composition system built from primitives (Section/Container/Stack/Flex/Grid/Text/Image/Video/Button/Icon/Input/Divider), widget variants, and — in a second part of the same brief — a full "Builder Introspection API" for AI (`getSiteStructure`/`getWidget`/`getWidgetSchema`/`getWidgetCapabilities`, context levels 1–4, revision numbers for conflict detection, batch operations). Its own §28 lays out 7 phases (Audit → Architecture → Core Engine → Builder UI → Widget Migration → AI Integration → QA) — realistically a multi-month, multi-person engineering program, not a task.

### 31.1 Point-by-point: what the brief asks for that already exists

| Brief asks for | Already exists as |
|---|---|
| §5 Design Tokens (`theme: {colors, typography, spacing, borderRadius, shadows, breakpoints}`) | `WebsiteTheme` (`entities/website/model/types.ts`) — colors, fonts, radius, button style, container width, section spacing, card border/shadow. Edited in `ThemePanel.tsx`. |
| §2/§3 per-widget styling beyond global theme | `BlockStyle` — after AI-10 through AI-17 this session, now covers background (solid/gradient linear+radial), spacing (preset + arbitrary px), text color, border, shadow — 6 axes, all per-block, none of them theme-only. |
| §8 Responsive Design (Desktop/Tablet/Mobile, inheritance) | `Viewport` system (`desktop`/`tablet`/`mobile`) with real inheritance (`readResponsiveProp`/`writeResponsiveProp`) — already used by both `props` and `BlockStyle` fields marked `responsive: true`. |
| §14 AI-First Integration (AI uses the same API as the visual user) | Already the architecture — every AI tool (`add_block`/`update_block_props`/`set_style`) calls the exact same `WebsitesService`/block-tree functions the builder UI's own actions call; no separate AI-only write path exists. |
| §12 Composition System / primitives | Partially — `section`/`container`/`columns`/`column` are real container block types (`blocks/layout`), and every block already composes from the same `computeBlockWrapperStyle` + `FieldSchema` machinery. Not a full arbitrary-primitive system (Stack/Flex/Grid as freely nestable primitives), but not the "hardcoded JSX per widget" the brief assumes either. |
| §15A `getSiteStructure`-style introspection | Partially — `get_project_tree` (a real, existing `low`-risk read tool) gives the AI the page/block hierarchy today. Not the full graduated API (context levels, per-widget schema/capabilities queries, revision numbers) the brief describes. |

### 31.2 What's genuinely new and not built

Simple/Advanced UI mode split, a visual spacing box-model editor, a dedicated shadow/border visual editor UI (presets exist as of AI-17, but not a drag-adjustable X/Y/Blur/Spread advanced panel), a states system (hover/focus/active per element), any animation system, drag-and-drop snap lines/smart guides, widget variants (§13), a graduated AI context-level API with revision numbers and batch operations (§15A) — all real, all unbuilt, all legitimately large.

### 31.3 Decision: continue the bounded-slice track, not a rewrite

The brief's own §22 ("Backward Compatibility... не ломать существующие сайты... Не переписывать всё хаотично одним большим изменением") and §28's phased structure both argue against attempting this as one change, matching this project's own narrow-v1 discipline that's carried every phase since AI-1. Presented the owner 4 concrete candidate next slices (border/shadow per block, Simple/Advanced mode split, the AI introspection API, a visual spacing editor) rather than picking unilaterally, given how different in shape and risk each is. The owner picked border/shadow — see §32. The other three remain real, well-scoped candidates for future slices, not rejected ideas.
