## 32. AI-17 — per-block border and shadow (2026-09-01, continued)

Seventh slice of the AI-10 track, picked by the owner from §31.3's four candidates as the most directly continuous with the proven per-block-override mechanism (background → spacing → gradient → text color → **border/shadow**).

### 32.1 Design: distinct from the existing theme-level card border/shadow

`ThemeCardBorder`/`ThemeCardShadow` (pre-dating this AI initiative, Phase 11) already exist — but they only style card-shaped *surfaces inside* blocks (`.icon-card`/`.people-card`/`.simple-card`, read from `--site-card-border-width`/`--site-card-shadow`). This slice adds a structurally different thing: an outline/elevation around the **whole block wrapper itself**, for any block type, not just card-shaped content. `BlockStyle` gains:

```ts
borderWidth?: 'none' | 'thin' | 'medium' | 'thick';  // 0/1/2/4px
borderColor?: string;                                 // #rrggbb, only when borderWidth isn't 'none'
shadow?: 'none' | 'soft' | 'medium' | 'strong' | 'floating';
```

`borderColor` follows the exact `textColor` pattern from AI-16: unset falls back to `var(--site-border)` (the theme's own border color), an explicit hex wins. When either border or shadow is active, the block also picks up `var(--site-radius)` — the same rounding language the site's cards already use — rather than adding a seventh per-block field for radius this slice (a real, disclosed narrowing, not an oversight). Two new exported lookup tables in `theme-tokens.ts` (`BORDER_WIDTH_PX`, `BLOCK_SHADOW_VALUE`) resolve the presets to real CSS, consumed by a new `borderAndShadowStyle()` helper in `block-style.ts`. `LayoutSection.tsx` gained two permanent fields (`borderWidth`, `shadow`) plus one conditionally-spliced field (`borderColor`, shown only once `borderWidth` isn't `'none'` — found by `Array.findIndex` on the field key, since its position shifts depending on how many background/gradient fields are already spliced in ahead of it).

### 32.2 Backend

`block-style-schema.ts`: `borderWidth`/`shadow` joined `ENUM_STYLE_FIELDS` (closed value sets, validated the same way as `background`/`textAlign`); `borderColor` joined the existing hex-color literal-equality branch alongside `customBackgroundColor`/`gradientFrom`/`gradientTo`/`textColor`. `set_style`'s description text and `STYLE_FIELD_KEYS` updated to match.

### 32.3 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (176 tests, +5: valid/invalid `borderWidth`/`shadow` enum values, valid/invalid `borderColor` hex, all three removable via `null`) and `frontend/npm run check` (52 tests, +6: no border/shadow/radius when unset, a border with a custom color, the theme-border-color fallback, `borderColor` ignored when `borderWidth` is `'none'`, a shadow preset applying the theme radius, border+shadow together) both clean. A real `npm run build` succeeded — port 3000 confirmed free via `ss -ltnp` first (a **fourth** independent occurrence of the stray `next start` process from §22/§27.3/§28 was hit again restarting for this slice's verification, same fix, no new investigation — see §29.3, now updated to reflect four occurrences). Live-verified against CryptoVault's Hero block: set a thick border (confirmed `4px solid rgb(226, 232, 240)` — the theme's default border color, via `getComputedStyle`), added a `floating` shadow (confirmed the exact preset shadow value and a real visible screenshot), set a custom border color (`#2a9d8f`, confirmed via `getComputedStyle`), then reverted all three and confirmed a clean return to no border/shadow/radius.

### 32.4 What remains

Individual per-side border width/color, per-corner radius, and a fully custom X/Y/Blur/Spread/Opacity shadow editor (all named in the original brief's §7) remain out of scope for this narrow v1 — presets only, matching the same "narrow first, exact-value editor later if ever needed" discipline as every prior slice in this track. The three other candidates from §31.3 (Simple/Advanced mode, a visual spacing box-model editor, the AI introspection API) remain open, real, unscheduled next slices.

## 33. AI-18 — live visual spacing box-model diagram (2026-09-01, continued)

Second of §31.3's four candidate slices, picked to continue after the owner's bare "продолжай" — no re-ask needed since all four are legitimate, roughly-comparable-priority options and this one carries the least risk (pure UI, zero data model changes). Directly answers the original brief's §7 complaint, quoted almost exactly: *"Пользователь не должен видеть просто набор непонятных input полей... должен визуально понимать, что он редактирует"* — with the brief's own nested margin/padding/content ASCII diagram as the literal target shape.

### 33.1 Design: a visualization layer, not a second editor

The brief's mockup implies an editable diagram (numbers directly adjustable in the boxes). A fully editable version would need to either reimplement the existing `control: 'spacing'` select+number interaction a second time inside the diagram (real risk of the two surfaces drifting out of sync) or make the diagram the *only* way to edit spacing (a much bigger, riskier redesign of an already-working control). Chose a narrower, safer shape instead: `SpacingBoxModel` (`widgets/website-builder/inspector/SpacingBoxModel.tsx`) is **read-only** — it renders the block's current resolved `paddingY`/`paddingX`/`marginTop`/`marginBottom` values as live numbers inside nested boxes, sitting directly above the existing (unchanged) spacing controls in `LayoutSection.tsx`'s "Просто" tab. One source of truth (the existing controls) stays the only way to change a value; the diagram is a reactive preview that re-renders from the same component state on every keystroke/selection, so it can never itself go stale.

Deliberately scoped to the "Просто" tab only — the "Дополнительно" tab (independent per-side padding) already shows each side's value directly in its own field labels (Сверху/Справа/Снизу/Слева), so a second visual diagram there would be redundant, not additive.

**A real, structural difference from generic CSS worth noting**: this system's `BlockStyle` has no `marginLeft`/`marginRight` at all — blocks are always full-width in a vertical page flow, so horizontal margin has no meaning here the way it does in general CSS. The diagram reflects this honestly (the margin ring shows only top/bottom values) rather than inventing fields that don't exist just to match a generic 4-sided mockup.

A small helper, `toPx()`, converts whatever a resolved field value currently is (`SpacingSize` preset string, a custom `number`, or `undefined`) into a plain px number for display, reusing the already-exported `SPACING_PX` lookup table — no new resolution logic, just a display-format conversion of values `LayoutSection.tsx` was already computing for the controls themselves.

### 33.2 Visual design

Devtools-style colors deliberately, not Tavern's own `--tavern-*` palette: orange for the margin ring, green for padding, blue (Tavern's own `--tavern-accent`, for consistency with the rest of the app) for the content placeholder — the same convention every browser's own devtools box-model inspector already uses, chosen specifically so it reads as immediately familiar rather than needing new visual language invented from scratch.

### 33.3 Verified

`frontend/npm run check` (52 tests, unchanged — this slice added a new presentational component with no independent logic worth a dedicated unit test beyond what `toPx`'s trivial, already-tested `SPACING_PX` dependency covers) clean, plus a real `npm run build` (port 3000 checked via `ss -ltnp` first — this time it was just this session's own leftover `next dev` from AI-17's verification, not a new stray-process occurrence; stopped and rebuilt normally). Live-verified against CryptoVault's Hero-adjacent Section block: the diagram rendered real values (96px padding top/bottom, 0px left/right, 0px margin) matching the block's actual configured spacing; selected "Отступы сверху/снизу" and changed it from the `xl` preset to `sm`, confirmed the diagram's numbers updated to 16px in the same interaction (no separate refresh needed) and the canvas's own rendered block visibly shrank to match; reverted to `xl` and confirmed the diagram showed 96px again. Also confirmed the diagram does not appear on the "Дополнительно" tab, as designed.

### 33.4 What remains

Two candidates from §31.3 are still open: Simple/Advanced UI mode split, and the AI introspection API (`getWidget`/`getWidgetSchema`/etc.). A fully editable version of this diagram (numbers directly adjustable in-place) remains a real, larger follow-up if the read-only preview turns out not to be enough — not attempted this slice given the sync-risk reasoning in §33.1.

---

## 34. AI-19 — first real slice of the Builder Introspection API (2026-09-01, continued)

Last of §31.3's four candidate slices from the builder-UX brief. Different in kind from AI-10 through AI-18 (all style-engine/widget-UI work) — this one closes a gap in what the AI itself can *see*, not what a human can customize, directly answering the second brief's §15A ("AI CONTEXT & BUILDER INTROSPECTION API").

### 34.1 The real gap, confirmed by reading the code, not assumed

`get_project_tree` (AI-1, §6) — the project's first-ever AI tool — returns exactly this per page: `{ id, slug, title, blockCount }`. No block ids. No block types. No content. No style. Every subsequent tool that operates on an *existing* block (`update_block_props`, `set_style`) takes a `blockId` as a required argument, with no tool anywhere in the registry that could tell the model what a valid `blockId` even *is* for a page it didn't just create in the same conversation turn. In practice this meant the model's only source of block ids was its own short-term memory of tool results earlier in the same conversation — completely blind to blocks the human user created or edited in the visual builder, or to any turn further back than the model's own context window reaches. This is precisely the failure mode the brief's §11 names: *"AI НЕ должен... придумывать ID... выполнять изменения без проверки context."*

### 34.2 Two tools, not one, mirroring the brief's own two-level shape

**`get_page_blocks(pageId)`** — brief's `getWidgetTree()`/`getPageStructure()`. Returns a lightweight recursive tree: `{ id, type, hidden?, children? }` per block — deliberately no `props`/`style`, matching the brief's own §3/§9 principle of graduated context levels (a full page's block tree with every block's full content and style would be a lot of tokens for a question that's often just "what's the id of the pricing block"). `hidden` is a plain boolean here (collapsed from the real `Partial<Record<Viewport, boolean>>`) — "is this hidden on any viewport" is enough signal for the model to notice and ask, without spending tokens on the full per-viewport shape.

**`get_block(blockId)`** — brief's `getWidget(widgetId)`. Returns one block's actual current `props`, `style`, `hidden`, and whether it has children, found anywhere in the document (not just top-level) via `findBlockInPages` — the exact same tree-search utility `set_style`/`update_block_props` (AI-2) already use to *write*, now reused to *read*. No new tree-walking logic, no risk of the read and write paths disagreeing about what "the block with this id" means.

Both `low` risk (read-only, same classification as `get_project_tree`), registered in `ai.module.ts` following the file's own documented convention exactly: one new provider file, one new line in the module's `providers` array, no changes to `AiService`/`ToolRegistryService` themselves.

### 34.3 Deliberately not built this slice

The brief's fuller API (§15A) also names `getSiteStructure()`, `getWidgetSchema(widgetType)`, `getWidgetCapabilities(widgetType)`, `getCurrentEditorContext()`, `getAvailableWidgets()`, revision numbers for conflict detection, batch operations, and screenshot-based visual context. None of these are built this slice. `get_project_tree` + `get_page_blocks` + `get_block` together already cover the two things that matter most in practice — "what pages/blocks exist" and "what does this specific block currently contain" — which is what actually blocked real usage; the rest are real, legitimate future slices once (or if) a concrete need for them shows up, not built speculatively ahead of one.

### 34.4 Verified

`backend/npm run check` (176 tests, unchanged — both new tools are thin wrappers around already-tested `WebsitesService.getDraft`/`findBlockInPages`, matching this project's own established precedent of verifying this class of tool live rather than with new unit tests, same as `get_project_tree`/`get_wallet_info` before it) clean. Confirmed the backend container hot-reloaded with no DI errors after adding the two new providers (`docker compose logs` showing a clean `Nest application successfully started`). **Live-verified through two real, separate multi-turn Gemini conversations, not curl and not mocked**: first asked "какие блоки есть на главной странице" — after one transient Gemini `503` (high-demand, not this project's own quota limiter — a legitimate one-time retry, not a repeated hammer against a rate limit) the model correctly chained `get_project_tree` → `get_page_blocks` and listed all 9 real blocks on CryptoVault's home page with their real types (`businessheader`, `hero`, `servicegrid`, `openinghours`, `location`, `reviews`, `contact`, `footer`, `web3wallet`); then asked "какой сейчас текст заголовка и подзаголовка у блока hero" as a follow-up (deliberately not reusing any id from the first answer, to force a fresh lookup) — the model re-ran `get_project_tree` → `get_page_blocks` → `get_block` and reported the Hero block's exact real content (`CryptoVault` / `Рядом с вами` / `Проверенный местный сервис, которому доверяют соседи.`), matching what was actually visible on screen, not a guess.

### 34.5 What this closes

This is the last of the four candidate slices §31.3 presented after the second, larger "rewrite the builder" brief was scoped down. All four are now shipped: border/shadow (AI-17), the visual spacing diagram (AI-18), and now the introspection API's first real slice (AI-19) — Simple/Advanced UI mode is the one candidate from that original list not yet built. §29's consolidated open-items list remains the reference for what's next across the whole project.

## 35. AI-20 — `web3wallet` joins the AI's curated block allowlist (2026-09-01, continued)

Picked up from §29.5's suggested next step ("AI-6's `dataBindings`/`capabilities` for `CustomWidget`" — binding a widget to Web3/Custom Entity data), after §31.3's fourth candidate (Simple/Advanced UI mode split) turned out, on inspection, not to decompose into a bounded slice: most blocks in the registry have only 4–10 props (checked directly, `grep -c "key:"` across `blocks/*/index.tsx`), so there's no real "too many fields" problem to solve, and building the split anyway would mean touching field declarations across all ~13 block-category files for speculative benefit — the exact "don't build for hypothetical future need" anti-pattern this project's own `AGENTS.md` warns against. Declined, not silently dropped — flagging it here so a future agent doesn't waste a slice re-discovering the same thing.

### 35.1 What the §29.5 candidate actually required, once inspected

Sent an `Explore` subagent to check the real current shape of `CustomWidget`/`add_block` before scoping anything. Findings that reshaped the slice:

- **`CustomWidget.schema` composition is scoped to `ALLOWED_BLOCK_TYPES`** — the exact same 6-type curated allowlist `add_block` uses (`heading`/`text`/`quote`/`spacer`/`image`/`button`), not the full ~50-type frontend block registry. `productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`web3wallet` were never composable into a widget, or addable by the AI at all — a real, previously-undocumented gap distinct from the "dataBindings" framing.
- **A `CustomWidget` is never a live, persistent node** — `insertWidgetBlocks` (`website-store.ts`) copies its `schema` blocks into a page's `blocks` array with fresh ids at insertion time (`ComponentLibraryPanel.tsx`); there is no `CustomWidgetRenderer`, and editing a widget later never affects already-inserted copies. So "binding a widget to live data" doesn't need new live-reference architecture — a copied block that already re-fetches its own data at render time (like `productgrid`/`web3wallet` already do via `business.businessId` from render context) works correctly regardless of whether it arrived via direct `add_block` or via a widget-template copy.
- **Custom Entities' public read path is genuinely blocked**, confirmed directly against `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`: `CustomEntity`/`CustomEntityRecord` have no `isPublic` field or equivalent. Exposing entity records publicly is a real product decision the owner needs to make (a `CustomEntity` can hold private data, unlike a wallet address or a product), not something to add silently — matches §29.1's own prior reasoning, re-confirmed rather than re-litigated.
- **Web3 has no such blocker** — `web3wallet`'s props (`eyebrow`/`heading`/`description`/`nftLimit`, `blocks/web3/index.tsx`) are four plain scalars, no `mediaAsset`/`linkTarget`/entity-reference fields at all. It was simply never added to the curated allowlist.

Given this, the honestly-scoped slice became: **add `web3wallet` to `ALLOWED_BLOCK_TYPES`**, closing the `add_block` gap directly and the `CustomWidget` composition gap as a side effect of reusing the same allowlist — rather than attempting the originally-imagined (and, per this audit, largely already-solved-by-existing-architecture) "dataBindings" mechanism.

### 35.2 What shipped

- `backend/src/modules/ai/tools/lib/add-block-schemas.ts`: new `CuratedField` kind `'number'` (with `min`/`max`, mirroring the frontend `FieldSchema`'s `control: 'number'`) — the first non-string/enum/linkTarget/mediaAsset field kind, needed for `nftLimit`. `AllowedBlockType` gained `'web3wallet'`; `WEB3WALLET_SCHEMA` (`eyebrow`/`heading`/`description` strings + `nftLimit: 0–24`) added to `BLOCK_SCHEMAS`, sverено 1:1 with `web3WalletFields`/`defaultProps` in `blocks/web3/index.tsx`.
- No `refs`/`needsBlockRefs` changes needed — `web3wallet` has zero fields requiring existence-checking (unlike `image`/`button`), so it passes through `EMPTY_REFS` safely by construction, both from `add_block`/`update_block_props` (which build real refs only for `image`/`button`) and from `CustomWidgetsService`'s `parseWidgetSchema` (which always uses empty refs, by design, since a widget isn't yet tied to a page at validation time).
- `AddBlockTool`'s description/parameter text and `ai.tools/lib/add-block-schemas.ts`'s/`add-block.tool.ts`'s doc comments updated to mention `web3wallet` alongside the existing 6 types.
- `CustomWidgetsService`/`custom-widgets.types.ts`'s doc comment extended to note `web3wallet` needs no special-case reasoning the way `image`/`button` do — it carries no data-referencing fields at all.

### 35.3 Verified

`backend/npm run check` — 183 tests (+7: `number` field validation in `add-block-schemas.test.ts` — default/in-range/rejects-string/below-min/above-max/boundary values; `web3wallet` accepted end-to-end in `custom-widgets.types.test.ts`), lint/format/typecheck all clean. Backend hot-reloaded with 0 compile errors (`docker compose logs`). **Live-verified through a real Gemini conversation**, not curl: on CryptoVault's real business, asked the AI chat to "add a Web3 wallet block to the end of the home page, show 8 NFTs" — the model chained `get_project_tree` → `add_block` (visible via the "Добавил блок" tool-call badge; the model's own final text summary hit a transient Gemini provider hiccup — the same "safe to note, not a tool failure" pattern documented earlier this session — but the tool call itself completed). Confirmed via the canvas: a new block appeared after the footer, at the very end of the page as asked, with the real live wallet balance and **exactly 8** real NFT cards from Alchemy (matching the requested `nftLimit`, not the default of 6) — proof the AI-supplied `props` were validated and applied correctly, not just defaults. Since CryptoVault already had a manually-added `web3wallet` block from AI-13's testing, the AI-added duplicate was deleted afterward via the canvas's own delete control (confirmed via the builder's "Сохранено" autosave indicator) to leave the real business's site as it was before this verification pass — the pre-existing wallet block, showing its default 6 NFTs, is unaffected.

### 35.4 What this does and doesn't close

Closes: the AI can now add (`add_block`) or edit (`update_block_props`) a `web3wallet` block itself, and a `CustomWidget` template can now include one — the first widget/AI-addable block with genuinely live, externally-sourced data, not just static content. Doesn't close: `productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid` and the rest of the `control: 'dataSource'` family remain outside both `add_block`'s and `CustomWidget`'s allowlists — their value is a compound `{limit, sort}` shape gated by an `entity` capability flag, not a bare scalar, so extending the curated schema to cover them is a real, larger follow-up (a new `'dataSource'` `CuratedField` kind, plus capability-gating logic this file doesn't have yet), not a one-line addition like `web3wallet` was. Custom Entities' public-read blocker (§29.1/§35.1) is unchanged and still needs an owner decision on an `isPublic` flag before any public endpoint or AI/widget binding can safely exist.

## 36. Google OAuth login for the dashboard (2026-09-02) — not part of the AI initiative

Owner's request, unrelated to the AI-native platform track (AI-1 through AI-20 above) — plain product work, no `AI-N` label. Scoped down from "add Google/Facebook/Apple" to Google only first, after confirming with the owner: this is for the single existing `User` account system (site members / business owners logging into the Taverna dashboard), not a second consumer-facing account system for a business's public-site customers (those remain intentionally anonymous — see `Order`/`Appointment`'s own schema comments, unchanged).

### 36.1 What shipped

- **Prisma**: `User.passwordHash` is now `String?` (was required) — an OAuth-only account has no password at all, not a dummy hash. New `OAuthAccount` model (`provider` enum `google|facebook|apple`/`providerAccountId`/`userId`, unique on `(provider, providerAccountId)`) — a join table, not flat `googleId`/`facebookId` columns on `User`, so one account can later link multiple providers. Migration written by hand via `prisma migrate diff` + `migrate deploy` (the documented `P3006` shadow-DB replay bug, §29.3, hit again exactly as expected).
- **Backend** (`modules/auth/`): `GoogleOAuthAdapter` — plain `fetch`-based Google authorization-code flow (mirrors `GeminiAdapter`'s style), deliberately not `passport`/`passport-google-oauth20` (that pulls in Express session middleware, foreign to this project's opaque-Redis-token session model). Two new Redis one-shot services, same shape as the existing `SocketTicketsService`: `OAuthStateService` (CSRF `state` for the authorization-code flow) and `OAuthExchangeService` (a one-shot code handed to the browser in a redirect URL, exchanged for the real session token — the real token itself never appears in a URL/browser history/Referer). `GET /auth/google` → `GET /auth/google/callback` → `POST /auth/exchange`, all gated by a `GoogleOAuthConfiguredGuard` (same `isConfigured()`/503 pattern as `AiConfiguredGuard` for Gemini) so a deployment without Google credentials degrades gracefully instead of failing to boot. Email-collision policy (confirmed with the owner): auto-link to the existing password account when the Google-verified email matches — Google already proved ownership of that email (`email_verified` checked), so this is treated as the same person, not silently creating a duplicate account.
- **Frontend**: `AuthForm.tsx` gained a "Войти через Google" link plus an `?error=oauth_failed` banner. The button is a real `<a href="/auth/google">` (full navigation, not a Server Action) routed through a thin Next.js Route Handler (`app/auth/google/route.ts`) that 302s to the backend — keeps the "browser never talks to backend directly" invariant (`AGENTS.md` §5) intact, the backend's own address never reaches client-rendered markup. `app/auth/callback/route.ts` is the landing point for the backend's post-login redirect — **had to be a Route Handler, not a `page.tsx`** (see 36.2, a real bug caught live).

### 36.2 A real bug, caught live, not by `npm run check`

First implementation put the exchange-and-cookie logic in `app/auth/callback/page.tsx`, calling the `'use server'`-marked `exchangeGoogleCode` directly from the page's render. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` both passed clean — TypeScript has no way to know that `cookies().set()` is only permitted inside a genuine Server Action *invocation* (a form submit, programmatic action dispatch) or a Route Handler, not inside a plain function call made during a Server Component's render, even when that function happens to live in a `'use server'` file. Only surfaced doing a real live end-to-end run through actual Google consent in the browser: the backend's own `/auth/exchange` call visibly succeeded in the container logs (a plain `LOG` line, not the `WARN` a real 401 produces — confirmed by deliberately reproducing both to compare), yet the browser still landed on `/auth?error=oauth_failed` with no session cookie set. Root cause: the cookie-write silently threw inside the try block, was swallowed by the local `catch { redirect(...) }`, and never surfaced as a visible server error anywhere `npm run check`/`build` would catch. Fixed by moving the whole exchange to `app/auth/callback/route.ts` (mirrors the project's own pre-existing `app/auth/clear/route.ts`, which already does the equivalent `response.cookies.delete()` — the working pattern was already in the codebase, just not followed for this new path). `SESSION_COOKIE_OPTIONS`/`SESSION_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` moved to `shared/config/session.ts` so both the Route Handler and the existing `loginToAccount`/`registerAccount` Server Actions share one definition instead of two copies drifting apart.

### 36.3 Verified

`backend`: 183 tests, lint/format/typecheck clean; live `curl` against the real running container — `GET /auth/google` returns a clean 503 before credentials are set (`GoogleOAuthConfiguredGuard` working), then a real 302 to a correctly-formed `accounts.google.com` URL once the owner supplied real credentials (env vars named `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`, not `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET` as first drafted — renamed everywhere to match what the owner had already set, rather than asking them to edit `.env` again); `POST /auth/exchange` correctly 401s on a bogus/reused code (`WARN`-level log, verified by deliberately calling it twice with the same code); existing password login re-verified live both for a real account (successful login + immediate session revoke to avoid leaving a stray token) and for a non-existent email (clean 401, not a 500 — confirms the `user?.passwordHash` null-safety change didn't regress the password path).

`frontend`: 52 tests, lint/format/typecheck clean, a real `npm run build` (not just `check`) both before AND after the Route Handler fix — the first build's clean pass is exactly why this needed a live browser run to catch at all. **Full live run through real Google consent** in the browser (`claude-in-chrome`), using the owner's own already-authenticated Google session (no password entered by the agent at any point — only an existing account-chooser click and a consent "Продолжить"): confirmed via Postgres directly afterward that this created exactly one `oauth_accounts` row (`provider='google'`, real Google `sub` as `providerAccountId`) linked to the owner's **existing** `users` row (same `id` as before, `passwordHash` still non-null) — auto-link worked as specified, no duplicate account. Verified the `?error=oauth_failed` banner renders correctly too (deliberately hit `/auth/callback` with no `code`). Session was cleared/re-authenticated multiple times during this verification pass; ended the pass logged back in as the owner, no lingering state changed beyond the one real `oauth_accounts` row (expected, permanent).

### 36.4 What's still open

Facebook and Apple were explicitly deferred by the owner until Google is proven out — `OAuthProvider` (the enum) already has `facebook`/`apple` values reserved, but no adapter/routes exist for either yet. Apple Sign-In in particular needs a paid Apple Developer account and a JWT-signed client secret (materially more setup than Google/Facebook) — flagged to the owner as the more involved of the two remaining providers before this session started. No account-settings UI yet to view/unlink a connected OAuth provider, or to set a password for an OAuth-only account after the fact — real, unscheduled future work, not attempted this slice.

A Facebook implementation (generic `OAuthAdapter`/`OAuthProviderRegistry`, generic `:provider` routes) was built and verified in this same session, then reverted at the owner's explicit request ("убираем значит facebook и apple") — back to the simple hardcoded-Google shape described in 36.1, on the reasoning that a provider-agnostic abstraction for a single remaining provider is exactly the premature-abstraction case `AGENTS.md` warns against. Reverted cleanly via `git revert` (not `reset`), so the generic version remains recoverable from history if a second provider is wanted again later.

### 36.5 Post-login redirect now returns to the origin the login started from (2026-09-02, continued)

The owner ran a real production build (`next start`) and hit it from their machine's LAN address (`http://192.168.x.x:3000`, visible alongside `http://localhost:3000` in Next's own startup banner), then asked: *"нужно чтобы после авторизации через гугл редирект происходил на origin"* — the post-login redirect always landed on the configured `FRONTEND_URL` (`http://localhost:3000` by default) regardless of which address the login actually started from.

**Why this needed care, not a one-line fix**: Google's callback only ever returns `code`/`state` — the original request's origin has to be carried through the whole round trip (browser → frontend → backend → Google → backend → frontend) some other way. The natural carrier is the same one-shot `state` value already used for CSRF protection (`OAuthStateService`) — its Redis value changed from a placeholder `'1'` to the actual resolved origin string, so `consume(state)` now returns the origin to redirect back to (or `null` if the state itself is invalid/expired, same as before).

**The real risk this creates, and how it's closed**: naively trusting a client-supplied `?origin=` query param and redirecting to it after login would be a textbook open-redirect — a crafted link (`/auth/google?origin=https://evil.example`) would hand the one-shot exchange code (which converts to a real session token via `POST /auth/exchange`) to an attacker's domain, a real account-takeover path, not just a cosmetic bug. `AuthService.resolveOAuthOrigin` never redirects to a client-supplied value directly: it's accepted only if it matches `FRONTEND_URL` exactly or appears in a new explicit allowlist (`ALLOWED_OAUTH_ORIGINS`, comma-separated, empty by default — unset means *only* `FRONTEND_URL` works, identical to the pre-existing behavior); anything else — an unlisted origin, a malformed string that fails `new URL()` parsing, no origin at all — silently falls back to `FRONTEND_URL`, the same safe default as before this change, never an error.

**What shipped**: `app/auth/google/route.ts` (frontend) now appends its own real `request.nextUrl.origin` as `?origin=` when proxying to the backend. `AuthController.googleLogin` reads it and passes it to `AuthService.buildOAuthAuthorizationUrl`... (named `buildGoogleAuthorizationUrl` in the current, post-Facebook-revert codebase — see 36.4's note above), which resolves and validates it before issuing `state`. `completeGoogleLogin` builds the final `/auth/callback` redirect against the origin recovered from `state`, not the fixed config value.

**Verified**: `backend` 183 tests, lint/format/typecheck clean; `frontend` 52 tests, lint/format/typecheck clean, a real `npm run build` showing `/auth/google` unchanged in the route list. Live against the real backend (not curl fakery for the security-relevant part — inspected the actual Redis value each `state` resolved to): no `origin` param → stored value is `FRONTEND_URL`; `origin` exactly equal to `FRONTEND_URL` → itself; `origin` set to the owner's real LAN address *before* adding it to `ALLOWED_OAUTH_ORIGINS` → safely fell back to `FRONTEND_URL` (proves the allowlist actually gates, not just documents); same LAN address *after* adding it to `.env` and restarting → correctly stored and would be used verbatim; a syntactically invalid `origin` string → safely fell back, no crash.

**Follow-up bug, same day**: the owner switched from a LAN IP to an ngrok tunnel (`FRONTEND_URL`/`ALLOWED_OAUTH_ORIGINS` both set to the tunnel's HTTPS URL) and reported the redirect still landing on `localhost:3000`. Root cause was in the piece this slice's own verification pass couldn't reach with curl alone: `app/auth/google/route.ts` read `request.nextUrl.origin` to determine where the request came from — that property reflects the address Next.js itself is literally listening on, **not** the incoming request's `Host` header, confirmed by curling the running frontend directly with a spoofed `Host: <tunnel>.ngrok-free.dev` header and observing the outgoing redirect still carry `origin=http://localhost:3000` regardless. This project had already hit and solved the identical class of bug once before for a different feature — `proxy.ts`'s own comment explains exactly why it reads `request.headers.get('host')` instead of `nextUrl` for the same reason, plus `next.config.ts`'s `allowedDevOrigins` and its long comment about ngrok tunnels breaking dev-mode hydration — three separate pieces of prior, on-disk evidence for the same underlying Next.js behavior that a fresh read of `next.config.ts`/`proxy.ts` before writing the first version would have surfaced. Fixed by building the origin from `request.headers.get('host')` + `x-forwarded-proto` (falling back to `nextUrl.protocol` when absent, i.e. no reverse proxy in front) instead of `nextUrl.origin`.

**Second follow-up, same session**: the first follow-up fix only touched `app/auth/google/route.ts`. Asked directly to check via the real tunnel, a live click-through (real browser, real Google consent, no shortcuts) still landed on `https://localhost:3000/` — confirmed via Redis that the *backend* had correctly resolved and stored the tunnel origin against that exact `state` before the browser was even sent to Google, so the remaining bug had to be entirely on the frontend's return leg. `app/auth/callback/route.ts` (the actual landing point after backend redirects back) had the identical `new URL('/', request.url)` pattern, missed when the first fix was written — reproduced directly with the same spoofed-`Host` curl trick (`location: https://localhost:3000/auth?error=oauth_failed`, matching the live symptom exactly) before touching any code. A third, pre-existing, unrelated file (`app/auth/clear/route.ts`, used when a session cookie is found invalid — not part of this OAuth work at all) had the exact same `new URL('/auth', request.url)` pattern and would have hit the same failure the next time a session expired under a tunnel/LAN address, so it was fixed alongside rather than left as a landmine now that the pattern was recognized. With three real occurrences of the identical fix, extracted a shared `getRequestOrigin()` helper (`shared/lib/request-origin.ts`) rather than leaving a fourth copy-pasted block; all three route handlers now call it. Re-verified the exact original failing scenario end to end for real this time: killed and relaunched the owner's own `next start` process (a rebuild on disk doesn't hot-swap an already-running `next start`), then drove a full live click-through against the actual `https://automatic-salutary-attest.ngrok-free.dev` tunnel in the browser — real Google account picker, real consent (already granted from earlier in the session, so no password entry involved), and the browser landed on `https://automatic-salutary-attest.ngrok-free.dev/`, logged in, no `localhost` anywhere in the final URL.

---

## 37. True device-preview emulation + style-control rethink (2026-09-02/03) — not part of the AI initiative

Owner's brief: redesign the builder to be clearer, make mobile show a real desktop preview, ship something "not embarrassing to show a user," and rethink the style dropdowns. Scoped into three bounded, independently-verified phases rather than attempted as one pass, matching this project's own established discipline (§31.3).

### 37.1 The actual bug behind "can't preview desktop on mobile"

`Canvas.tsx`'s and `PreviewModal.tsx`'s `desktop` viewport width was literally `'100%'` — "however wide the current screen is," not a real ~1280px desktop layout. On an actual phone this meant tapping "Десктоп" changed which `responsive`-typed prop values were being edited but had **zero visual effect** on layout width — multi-column content just rendered squashed into the phone's own width. Confirmed via `Canvas.module.scss`'s own `@include mobileOrTablet { width: 100% !important; ... }`, whose comment argued (reasonably, in isolation) that simulating a second narrow screen inside an already-narrow editor was pointless — correct for the *editing* case, wrong for *preview*, and the code didn't distinguish the two.

**Fix**: `VIEWPORT_FRAME_WIDTH` (`entities/website/model/types.ts`) — one real source of truth, `{desktop: 1280, tablet: 834, mobile: 390}` px. New `useFitZoom` hook (`shared/lib/use-fit-zoom.ts`) fits that fixed width into whatever container is actually available, via CSS `zoom` — **not** `transform: scale`, deliberately: `zoom` is layout-affecting, so `getBoundingClientRect`/pointer-event coordinates stay correct post-scale, which matters because the canvas has real `dnd-kit` drag-and-drop running inside it; `transform` only affects paint, which would have de-synced drag physics from what's visually rendered. Applied in both `Canvas.tsx` (editing) and `PreviewModal.tsx` (preview), with a small floating zoom-percent pill (`ZoomIndicator.tsx`) to toggle fit/100%.

**Live-verified, not just typed**: real Playwright session (headless Chromium, real login as the owner) at a 390px viewport — selecting "Десктоп" now renders the actual multi-column desktop layout scaled to ~27-30%, not a squashed mobile column. A second check dragged a block under active zoom < 100% and confirmed the block actually reordered (proof `zoom`, not `transform`, was the right call for drag-and-drop specifically) — see the block-order assertion in that session's log, not repeated here since it isn't committed as an automated test (a one-off manual Playwright script in scratch, not `frontend/`).

### 37.2 Style controls: `scale` (slider) and `segmented` (icon buttons) join `FieldSchema`

Every ordinal style property (padding, radius, shadow, border width, section spacing) was a plain `<select>` of named presets. New `FieldSchema` control kinds (`registry.ts`, rendered in `FieldControl.tsx`):

- **`'scale'`** — a slider stepping through the same named-preset arrays selects already used (`SPACING_SIZE_OPTIONS`/`RADIUS_OPTIONS`/etc., zero new option lists), with an optional `allowCustomPx` that adds a final "Свой" step revealing a number input — the same custom-px escape hatch the old `'spacing'` control had, now generalized. Applied to spacing, radius, shadow, border width, section spacing, grid columns, gap.
- **`'segmented'`** — icon/swatch button row for genuinely categorical (non-ordinal) choices with a small option count — applied to text alignment (3 icon buttons). Deliberately **not** applied to every categorical field: background/font/button-style kept as `<select>` once their option count or label length made a segmented row read worse than a dropdown (a judgment call made per-field, not a blanket conversion).

A real layout bug was caught and fixed during this slice's own first browser check, not left in: the slider + its value label, laid out side-by-side, overflowed a narrow inspector grid column and bled into the next field — root cause was a flex item (`<input type="range">`, a replaced element) refusing to shrink below its native intrinsic width inside a `minmax(180px, 1fr)` grid track. Fixed by stacking the slider and its label vertically instead of trying to force the shrink.

### 37.3 Universal block-layout system — closes §31.2's "composition system" gap

§31.1's own table entry for "§12 Composition System / primitives" said `section`/`container`/`columns`/`column` existed but weren't "a full arbitrary-primitive system (Stack/Flex/Grid as freely nestable primitives)." This slice closes most of that gap. New `BlockStyle` fields (`entities/website/model/types.ts`): `display` (`block`/`flex`/`grid`), `direction` (responsive — row on desktop, column on mobile is the single most common real "sidebar collapses" pattern, so this one field is `StyleValue<T>` unlike the rest), `wrap`, `justify`, `align`, `gap`, `gridColumns` — plus, on **any** block as a *child* of such a container: `grow`/`fixedWidth` (fixed-width sidebar vs. a flexible content column) and `sticky`/`stickyOffset` (`position: sticky`, with `align-self: flex-start` applied automatically so it doesn't get stretched flat inside a flex row). `computeBlockWrapperStyle` (`block-style.ts`) gained a third return value, `layout`, threaded through `BlockRendererProps` into `SectionRenderer`/`ContainerRenderer`/`ColumnsRenderer`/`ColumnRenderer` (`blocks/layout/index.tsx`) — applied on top of their existing SCSS class, so a document with none of these fields set renders byte-identical to before (confirmed live against Зёрна's existing site, unchanged).

Genuinely still not built (real, unscheduled follow-ups, not silently dropped): **bento-style asymmetric grids** — cell-level row/column *span* control, so `gridColumns` today only produces equal-width columns, not a "big tile + four small tiles" layout; a true bento grid needs per-child `gridColumn`/`gridRow` span fields this slice didn't add. **Responsive `display`** — only `direction` is `StyleValue<T>`; switching a block from `grid` to `block` per-viewport isn't possible today (workaround: leave `display` on and rely on `gridColumns`'s own responsive value collapsing to 1 on mobile, which covers the common case but isn't the same feature). **No visual snap-guides/drag-to-resize** for any of this — it's inspector-driven (sliders/toggles), not a direct-manipulation UI, matching §31.2's still-open "drag-and-drop snap lines/smart guides" item.

**Live-verified**: manually rebuilt the design brief's own reference sidebar pattern (fixed 260px sticky category column + flexible product-grid column) by hand in the browser via the new "Раскладка"/"Дополнительно" inspector tabs — screenshotted, rendered correctly side-by-side. `backend`/`frontend npm run check` and real `npm run build` clean at each of this section's three phases.

## 38. AI chat image/file attachments (2026-09-03) — not part of the curated-block-allowlist track

Owner's request: let the chat's AI-2 conversation accept an attached image (for "build a site like this design") or file, via a button and via pasting from the clipboard.

### 38.1 Design: a separate upload endpoint, not multipart inside `chat/stream`

`AiController.chatStream` is `@Sse()` — already documented as fragile around anything async happening before the handler returns its `Observable` (`AiOwnershipGuard`'s own comment: a real, previously-hit bug where `@Sse()` commits response headers on an internal timer, racing any `await` inside the handler itself). Rather than add `FilesInterceptor` to that exact handler and risk the same class of bug, attachments upload through a **new, plain (non-SSE) endpoint** first — `POST .../ai/chat/attachments`, reusing `createChatAttachmentMulterOptions()` (`common/lib/upload.ts`, already existed for the human-to-human messenger, just not wired to AI chat) — returning `{id, name, mimeType, url, sizeBytes}`. `ChatRequestDto` gained `attachmentIds?: string[]`; both proxy layers (`app/api/ai-chat/route.ts`, `stream-ai-chat.ts`) stay pure JSON, just pass the id array through — the delicate SSE-proxying code itself is untouched.

### 38.2 Backend: base64 inline data, read-once-then-delete

`LlmMessage` gained `attachments?: {mimeType, data}[]` (base64, `role: 'user'` only); `GeminiAdapter.toGeminiContent` adds one `inlineData` part per attachment — the standard Gemini multimodal `generateContent` shape, no SDK change needed (this project calls the REST API directly). `AiService.runToolLoop` resolves each `attachmentId` to a file (strict UUID-pattern validation + path-containment check, `resolveChatAttachmentPath`/`mimeTypeForChatAttachment` in `common/lib/upload.ts` — the id is client-echoed, so untrusted input even though the server minted it originally), reads + base64-encodes it into `messages[0]`, and — inside the loop's own `finally`, so it runs on success, on error, and on early client disconnect — deletes the file. Matches the pre-existing "no memory between HTTP calls" model this chat already had (every `chat`/`chatStream` call starts `messages` from zero): an attachment is relevant for exactly one turn, same as the rest of the conversation.

### 38.3 Frontend

`AiChatPanel.tsx` gained a paperclip button (hidden `<input type=file>`) and an `onPaste` handler on the message input that intercepts an image from `clipboardData.items` — both funnel into one `attachFile()` that uploads immediately (not deferred to send time), giving instant size/type-error feedback, then shows a thumbnail chip with a remove button before sending. Same 5MB/5-file limits as the existing human-messenger composer (`features/send-message/ui/MessageComposer.tsx`), copied deliberately, not reinvented.

### 38.4 Verified — and where verification stopped short

`backend`/`frontend npm run check` and real `npm run build` clean. **Live end-to-end through the real UI, real login, real upload** (Playwright, headless Chromium, the owner's actual account): attach `design.jpg` → thumbnail appears in the composer → send → thumbnail chip renders correctly in the sent message bubble in history. This proves the entire upload/inline-data/cleanup pipeline is wired correctly on the plumbing side.

**What was never verified, because it can't be yet**: an actual Gemini *response* to an attached image. The account's Gemini API key returned `429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` — not this project's own RPM/RPD limiter (that layer worked correctly and wasn't the blocker), but Google's own `"Your prepayment credits are depleted"` message, confirmed by hitting the real `generateContent` endpoint directly with `curl`, bypassing this app's code entirely. Three attempts across ~10 minutes of backoff all hit the identical message — this is a billing-account state, not a transient rate limit, and needs the owner to add credit at [ai.studio/projects](https://ai.studio/projects) before it can be re-tried. **The owner's original literal ask — "attach this design image, build me a site that matches it" — has never actually been observed working**, only every piece up to and including the point where a real Gemini call would happen.

---

## 39. `add_block` grows from 7 to all 48 registered block types (2026-09-03, continued)

Directly closes the gap §35.4 named explicitly: *"`productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid` and the rest of the `control: 'dataSource'` family remain outside both `add_block`'s and `CustomWidget`'s allowlists... extending the curated schema to cover them is a real, larger follow-up (a new `'dataSource'` `CuratedField` kind, plus capability-gating logic this file doesn't have yet)."* Also closes half of §34.3's named-but-unbuilt `getWidgetSchema(widgetType)`.

### 39.1 Scope: the full registry, confirmed by exact count, not "most of it"

`grep -c "^registerBlock<"` across every `entities/website/blocks/*/index.tsx` file: **48** real block types. `AllowedBlockType` in `add-block-schemas.ts` now lists exactly 48, one schema each, hand-transcribed field-by-field against the real frontend `FieldSchema[]`/`defaultProps` for every one (not generated) — the same "independent copy of one contract, backend doesn't import frontend code" pattern this file's own header already documented for the original 6.

### 39.2 Three new `CuratedField` kinds, needed for real coverage this time

Unlike AI-20's `web3wallet` (which only needed the already-existing `'number'` kind), most of the remaining 41 types needed genuinely new validation shapes:

- **`'list'`** — a bounded (`maxItems`, defaulting to 20) array of same-shaped items, `itemFields` recursively the same `CuratedField` system — covers every repeatable-cards/menu-links/FAQ-items pattern in the registry, validated by one recursive function (`validateOneField`), not copy-pasted per block.
- **`'dataSource'`** — exactly the `{limit: 1-24, sort: newest|price-asc|price-desc}` shape `productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid` need, rejecting any extra key (the model can't smuggle an `entity` override — which entity a grid queries is fixed by its block type, never a value the model chooses).
- **`'boolean'`** — `pricing.plans[].highlighted` and nothing else needed it, but it's now general.

### 39.3 Capability gating, real this time — not just documented as deferred

`productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid` require `commerce`/`booking`/`content` respectively (`CuratedBlockSchema.capability`, sverено 1:1 with the frontend's own `BlockDefinition.capability`). Unlike the frontend's `ComponentLibraryPanel` (which only *hides* the picker entry — a human sees fewer options, nothing stops them if they somehow triggered it another way), `AddBlockTool`'s handler now does a **real** `business.capabilities.includes(...)` check before creating the block, throwing a clear, actionable error instead. This asymmetry is deliberate, not an inconsistency to fix: a human missing an option in a picker just doesn't see it; a model given a `blockType` enum listing `productgrid` has no equivalent visual cue that it's currently meaningless for this specific business, and would otherwise confidently report success while leaving a permanently-empty block. Already-placed blocks of these types are unaffected if a capability is later disabled — same "gate on creation, not on every render" rule the frontend already followed.

### 39.4 `needsBlockRefs` is now derived, not a hardcoded list

Previously `blockType === 'image' || blockType === 'button'`, a literal two-item check — with 48 types and most of them containing `linkTarget`/`mediaAsset` fields somewhere (including nested inside `list.itemFields`), a hardcoded list would already be wrong and would silently drift further with every future block added. `schemaNeedsRefs`/`fieldsNeedRefs` (`add-block-schemas.ts`) instead walk the schema itself, recursively, so the answer can never disagree with what the validator actually checks.

### 39.5 New `get_block_schema` introspection tool — the other half of §34.3's `getWidgetSchema` gap

Inlining every field of 48 block types into `add_block`'s own tool description (the pattern the original 6-type version used) would have made that description unreadable and, worse, a second copy of the same contract `buildValidatedProps` already enforces, free to drift. Instead: a new `low`-risk, read-only tool, `get_block_schema` — no arguments returns a compact `{type, label, category, capability}` list of all 48 (for the model to pick a type), a `blockType` argument returns that one type's exact `fields`/`defaultProps` straight from `BLOCK_SCHEMAS` (so it is, by construction, never out of sync with validation). `add_block`'s own description was cut down to point at it instead of enumerating fields. `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` gained one line telling the model this tool exists and to prefer it over guessing from memory.

### 39.6 `add_block` gained `parentId` — without it, none of the above composes

A pre-existing, separate gap this slice also had to close to make the new block types useful at all: `add_block` could previously only append to a page's top-level `blocks` array — no way to put anything *inside* `section`/`columns`/`column`, even though those four types were already addable (§ earlier layout-track work). New optional `parentId` — when set, the block is appended to that parent's `children` instead, after checking the parent (a) exists on the same page, (b) is one of the four container types (`CONTAINER_BLOCK_TYPES`), and (c) accepts this child type (`ALLOWED_CHILDREN` — `columns` still only accepts `column`, mirroring the frontend's own `allowedChildren` restriction). Reuses `findBlockInPages`/`replaceBlockInPages` (already existed for `set_style`) — no new tree-walking logic.

### 39.7 `MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS`: 5 → 15

5 was sized for AI-1 (one read-only tool call per turn). Nesting via `parentId` is inherently serial in a way flat top-level `add_block` calls never were: to put a `column` inside a `columns` block, the model must first see that `columns` block's freshly-generated id come back from a previous round — no amount of "batch independent tool calls into one response" (the new `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` hint from §37... see below) can collapse a parent→child dependency into the same round. A realistic multi-section page with one sidebar-style nested section needs roughly 6-10 rounds, not 1-2. Left as a real, finite cap (not removed) — still a guard against a genuinely looping model, just sized for what composing a real page actually requires now. **Not live-load-tested** — see §41.

### 39.8 Verified

`backend/npm run check` — **250 tests** (+67 over §35's 183: full key-parity check across all 48 schemas' `defaultProps` vs. their own `fields`, deep-validation spot-checks on a representative sample, dedicated `list`/`dataSource`/`boolean`-kind coverage, `schemaNeedsRefs` recursive-detection cases, capability-metadata assertions), lint/format/typecheck clean, real `npm run build` clean. Two pre-existing tests that had hardcoded "`gallery`/`productgrid` are outside the allowlist" as their fixture were updated to assert the opposite (now correctly inside it) rather than left red or deleted.

**Live-verified against the real running app and real Postgres, not curl-mocked, but deliberately bypassing Gemini itself** (still credit-blocked, §38.4) — a one-off `NestFactory.createApplicationContext` script (not committed; run via `ts-node`, deleted after) grabbed the real `ToolRegistryService` from the fully-wired DI container and called each new tool's `.handler()` directly against Зёрна (has `commerce`) and "AI Демо Магазин" (doesn't): `get_block_schema` returned exactly 48 types and real `hero` fields; `add_block productgrid` correctly **rejected** on the business without `commerce` and correctly **succeeded** on the one with it; `add_block section` → `add_block columns` (`parentId`=section) → `add_block column` ×2 (`parentId`=columns) all succeeded, nested correctly; `add_block heading` with `parentId`=columns correctly **rejected** (`columns` only accepts `column`); `set_style` applied `display:flex`/`direction:row`/`gap:lg` to the columns block and `grow:fixed`/`fixedWidth:260`/`sticky:true` to the first column, both succeeded. Test blocks removed from Зёрна's real page afterward, confirmed via a before/after block-count check. This proves every piece of the new machinery works correctly through the exact same code path a real Gemini tool-call would take — the only thing not yet observed is the model itself choosing to call these tools in a real conversation, which needs §38.4's billing fix first.

## 41. Known open items — refreshed handoff (2026-09-03)

Supersedes §29 as the pointer for "picking this up fresh" (§29 itself is left unedited, as history — see its own items for anything not restated here, e.g. Custom Entities' public-read blocker, the conic/multi-stop-gradient and effects/animation/state-system gaps, which are all unchanged and not repeated below).

### 41.1 Closed since §29

Border/shadow, the spacing diagram, and the introspection API's first slice (§32-§34) were already closed before this batch. This batch additionally closes: real device-preview emulation on mobile (§37.1), the style-control rethink — slider + custom-px, segmented icon pickers (§37.2), a real cross-block-type layout/composition system covering most of §31.2's named gap (§37.3), AI chat image/file attachments (§38), and — the largest single item — `add_block`'s allowlist growing from 6 curated types to the full 48-type registry with real nesting and capability gating (§39), which was the exact, specifically-named next step §35.4 left open.

### 41.2 Genuinely new, real, open items from this batch

- **The owner's original ask — "attach a design image, build a matching site" — has never been observed working end-to-end.** Every piece up to the Gemini call itself is live-verified (§38.4); the model side is untested because the configured Gemini API key's prepayment credit is depleted (a billing-account state, confirmed by calling `generateContent` directly, not this project's own rate limiter). **Owner action needed**: add credit at ai.studio/projects. Nothing to build here — this is the single most valuable thing to re-test the moment credit is added, since it exercises §37 (layout), §38 (attachments), and §39 (block allowlist + nesting) all at once, together, for the first time.
- **`MAX_TOOL_ITERATIONS = 15` (§39.7) is reasoned, not load-tested** — sized from a paper estimate of how many rounds a realistic nested page needs, never observed against a real multi-round Gemini conversation (same blocker as above). Could be too low for an ambitious "build a 6-section site with two sidebars" request, or fine — genuinely unknown until the billing block clears.
- **No `delete_block`/`move_block`/`reorder_block` AI tool exists.** With the allowlist now covering the full registry and nesting available, the AI has real means to make a *structural* mistake (wrong nesting, wrong order) it previously couldn't — and no way to correct one itself short of overwriting props/style on what's already there. A human can always fix it via the canvas, but a fully AI-driven multi-turn build-and-refine session has no self-correction path for structure specifically. Not attempted this batch — a real, clearly-scoped next slice if agentic building turns out to need it in practice.
- **Orphaned chat-attachment files** — §38.2's cleanup only runs inside a completed `runToolLoop` call. A user who attaches a file via the new upload button and then closes the tab without ever sending the message leaves that file on `uploads/messages/` forever — no TTL sweep exists (mirrors §29.2's identical, already-known "no TTL on pending confirmations" gap, now with a second real instance of the same missing-cleanup-job pattern). Not a security issue (files are random-UUID-named, never listed publicly) — a slow disk-usage leak, low urgency, real.
- **Bento/asymmetric grid spans, and responsive `display`** — both named as explicit non-scope in §37.3 itself, restated here only so §41 stays a complete single list.
- **The port-3000 stray-process mystery (§29.3) recurred a fifth time this batch** — the owner asked directly to stop whatever was listening on `localhost:3000` mid-session; it turned out to be a `next start` (production) process, same signature as all four prior occurrences, again not attributable to any command run in that session. Still genuinely unidentified after five occurrences across multiple sessions — §29.3's suggestion to check this machine's WebStorm run configurations directly with the owner remains the most promising unexplored lead.
- **The dev backend container's `nodemon`/`tsc --watch` combo has a real, reproducible race** under several rapid consecutive source edits in a short window (hit ~4 times this batch alone): `nodemon` restarts on a file-change event before `tsc-alias`'s companion build step has finished writing `dist/main.js`, crashing with `Cannot find module '/app/dist/main.js'` and then sitting in "waiting for file changes" indefinitely (i.e. it does not self-recover on its own without a further edit or a manual restart). Workaround used every time: `docker restart tavern-backend-1` forces a full clean rebuild via the container's own `npm run dev` entrypoint. Not investigated further (root cause likely a missing `--watch`-file-completion signal between the two npm-concurrently'd processes in `backend/package.json`'s `dev` script) — a real, minor DX papercut worth a future session's 15 minutes if it keeps recurring, not urgent.

### 41.3 Suggested entry point for the next session

**If Gemini credit has been added**: re-run the owner's original request for real — attach a design image in the chat, ask for a matching site, watch what the model actually does with `get_block_schema`/`add_block`(+`parentId`)/`set_style` across a real multi-round conversation. This single test will surface whether §41.2's iteration-count concern is real, whether the model actually uses the new layout system well given only the `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` hints it has today, and whether `get_block_schema`'s two-tier (list-then-detail) shape is actually ergonomic for the model in practice — all currently unknown, all answered by one real conversation. **If not**: the `delete_block`/`move_block` gap (§41.2) is the most clearly-scoped, fully-unblocked next slice that doesn't need a live model to build or verify most of.

## 42. `delete_block`/`move_block` — the AI gets a self-correction path for structure (2026-09-03)

Closes the single most clearly-scoped item §41.2 named: with the full block registry and `parentId` nesting available since §39, the AI had real means to make a *structural* mistake (wrong nesting, wrong order) and no way to correct one itself short of a human fixing it on the canvas.

### 42.1 `delete_block` — `high` risk, deliberately, with a real reason

New tool, works on any block type anywhere in the document tree (`lib/block-tree.ts`'s `findBlockInPages`, same reach as `set_style`). `high` risk — the only other `high` tool in the project is `publish_website`, and this is arguably a step worse: this project has no version history or undo (confirmed again this batch — `WebsitesService` still has only the same forward-comment about it from before), so deleting a container takes every one of its `children` with it, permanently, with no "draft stays a draft" mitigation the way publishing has. New `countDescendants`/`removeBlockFromPages` in `lib/block-tree.ts` return both the removed subtree and an honest descendant count, which the tool surfaces in its output (`descendantsDeleted`) — the tool's own description tells the model to call `get_block` first and state that count in its own message before calling `delete_block`, because the confirmation card (`AiChatPanel.tsx`, AI-9) only ever shows the tool name and risk label, never the args — the same limitation `publish_website` has, just consequence-free there since it takes no arguments.

### 42.2 `move_block` — `medium` risk, mirrors the frontend's own `moveBlock` contract

New tool: reposition a block among its current siblings, and/or re-parent it into a different container on the same page — same `(blockId, parentId, index)` shape as the frontend's existing `moveBlock`/`insertBlock` (`entities/website/model/block-tree.ts`), just page-scoped rather than single-tree-scoped (the backend's `WebsitePage[]` needs to know which page a block's siblings live on; the frontend already gets a single page's tree handed to it by the caller). `parentId` omitted → keep the block's current parent, just reorder among the same siblings — deliberately the default, since "move this up" is the overwhelmingly common case and shouldn't require the model to already know a container id it was never given. `toTopLevel: true` is a separate explicit flag for "take it out of any container" rather than `parentId: null`, because `JsonSchemaProperty` (`ai.types.ts`) — the subset of JSON Schema this project's function-calling actually uses — has no clean way to distinguish "field omitted" from "field is null" the way TypeScript itself can.

### 42.3 A real, independently-found bug: moving a container into its own descendant silently deleted it — in both frontend and backend

Building the backend validation surfaced something neither `add_block`'s existing parent-checks nor the frontend's own `moveBlock` actually guarded against: moving a block into a target that is itself somewhere inside that block's own subtree. Both implementations share the identical two-step shape — remove the block from the tree first (which necessarily also removes everything nested under it, target included), *then* look up the target parent to insert into. If the target was inside the removed subtree, that lookup fails after the removal has already happened — and both `insertBlock` (frontend) and the equivalent backend logic were written to fail by silently returning the tree as it stood post-removal, i.e. **the moved block simply vanishes, no error, no signal**. This was a real, reachable, pre-existing latent bug in the frontend's `moveBlock`, not just a hypothetical concern for the new AI tool — whether dnd-kit's own collision/sortable-context constraints happen to prevent a human from ever triggering it in practice was not investigated (out of scope for this slice; a worthwhile 20-minute check for a future session if this bug is suspected to be reachable via drag-and-drop, not just via a hypothetical scripted `moveBlock` call). Fixed identically in both places with a new `isBlockOrDescendant`/`isBlockOrDescendant` guard (backend: `lib/block-tree.ts`; frontend: `entities/website/model/block-tree.ts`) checked *before* removal — an invalid move now returns the tree completely untouched (frontend) or throws a clear, explicit error the model can react to (backend), instead of quietly losing data either way. Both fixes have dedicated regression tests asserting the pre-fix behavior no longer reproduces.

### 42.4 `SYSTEM_INSTRUCTION` and tool-label gaps closed alongside

One new line added telling the model both tools exist and what they're for (structural self-correction, not another props/style editor). Also closed, while in the area, a small pre-existing gap: `get_page_blocks`/`get_block`/`get_block_schema` (all added in earlier sessions/batches) had never been given entries in the frontend's `TOOL_LABELS` map (`features/ai-chat/lib/tool-labels.ts`) — their tool-execution badges in the chat UI were silently falling back to the raw snake_case tool name. Added labels for those three plus the two new tools in the same edit, since it was a one-line-per-tool fix directly adjacent to what this slice was already touching.

### 42.5 Verified

`backend npm run check` — **265 tests** (+15 over §39's 250: `countDescendants`/`isBlockOrDescendant`/`findParentIdInBlocks`/`removeBlockFromPages`/`insertBlockIntoPage` unit coverage), lint/format/typecheck clean, real `npm run build` clean. `frontend npm run check` — 56 tests passing (+4 for the `moveBlock` cycle-rejection regression suite), lint/format/typecheck clean (the only lint output is a pre-existing `import/no-cycle` warning on two inspector files from an earlier, uncommitted batch of this session's work, unrelated to and untouched by this slice), real `npm run build` clean.

**Live-verified against the real app and real Postgres, same technique as §39.8** (a one-off `NestFactory.createApplicationContext` script, `ts-node` inside `tavern-backend-1`, deleted after) — against Зёрна's real site: built `section > columns > column×2`, one `heading` inside the first column; `move_block` correctly **rejected** moving the section into its own column (cycle guard) and correctly **rejected** moving the heading directly into `columns` (wrong child type, same `ALLOWED_CHILDREN` gate `add_block` already enforces); `move_block` correctly moved the heading from column 1 into column 2 (re-read the saved draft from Postgres afterward to confirm the move actually persisted, not just that the handler returned success); `move_block` with no `parentId` correctly reordered a second heading to the front of its existing siblings; `delete_block` on the section correctly reported `descendantsDeleted: 5` (columns + both columns + both headings) and the section was confirmed gone via a fresh re-read; `delete_block` on a nonexistent id correctly threw a clear not-found error. Test blocks were self-cleaning (the test's own final step deletes everything it created) — confirmed Зёрна's page is back to its pre-test 11 top-level blocks, same as after §39.8's cleanup.

**Not yet observed**: the model actually choosing to call either tool in a real conversation — same Gemini billing blocker as everything else since §38.4, unchanged this batch.

## 43. Orphaned chat-attachment files get a TTL sweep (2026-09-03)

Closes item #3 from `WEBSITE_BUILDER_GAPS.md` — a file uploaded via the AI chat's attach button (§38.3) is deleted after its chat turn completes, but a user who attaches a file and then just closes the tab left it on disk forever, with no cleanup path at all.

### 43.1 The actual shape of the gap — narrower than "AI chat leaks files", once traced through both callers

`uploads/messages/` (`createChatAttachmentMulterOptions`, `common/lib/upload.ts`) is shared by two callers, not one: the human-to-human messenger (`ThreadsController.sendMessage`) and the AI chat's attachment endpoint (`AiController.uploadAttachment`, §38.1). Tracing both: the messenger uploads files and sends the message text in the *same* HTTP request — either both commit or (on a mid-request crash) neither reliably does, a rare failure path, not the "user attaches then abandons" case. The AI chat's upload endpoint is deliberately separate and earlier (§38.1's own reasoning: instant preview in the composer before the message is sent) — which is exactly what makes an abandoned upload possible there and only there in the common case. So the real, common orphan source is narrower than "the AI chat leaks files" — it's specifically "an AI-chat upload whose chat turn never ran."

### 43.2 The fix generalizes past just the AI chat, for free

Rather than track "which uploads came from the AI chat" specifically, the sweep uses the one signal that's already true for *any* legitimately-kept file regardless of which caller wrote it: a sent message's attachment has a `MessageAttachment.url` row pointing at it. New `UploadsRetentionService` (`infrastructure/uploads/uploads-retention.service.ts`, new `UploadsRetentionModule`, registered once in `app.module.ts` next to `PrismaModule`/`RedisModule`/`RealtimeModule`) sweeps `uploads/messages/` on a timer (`setInterval` in `OnModuleInit`, the same pattern this project already uses everywhere for background jobs since there's no `@nestjs/schedule` dependency — see `AiRetentionService`/`AiCapacityService`) and deletes any file that is both older than a TTL (`UPLOAD_ORPHAN_TTL_HOURS`, default 24h — comfortably longer than any real AI chat turn, which completes in seconds-to-minutes) **and** whose url matches no `MessageAttachment` row. A file that's part of a real sent message is protected forever, however old; only genuinely-unreferenced files ever get touched. The pure selection logic (`selectOrphanedUploadUrls`, `uploads-retention.lib.ts`) is deliberately extracted from the `fs`/Prisma-touching service — the same `*.lib.ts` split this project already uses for `AiCapacityService` and friends, specifically so the actual selection rule has real unit tests without mocking the filesystem or database.

### 43.3 A side effect worth naming: this also catches an unrelated, smaller pre-existing gap

`ThreadsService.deleteMessage` already unlinks each attachment's file on disk when a message is explicitly deleted (`deleteUploadedFile`, confirmed by reading the existing code, not new this batch) — so that path was never the gap. But nothing before this sweep protected against the same file becoming orphaned by some other route (a failed/partial delete, a direct DB intervention, or any future code path that removes a `MessageAttachment` row without also calling `deleteUploadedFile`). The sweep's rule — "unreferenced + old = safe to delete" — closes that class of leak too, incidentally, not just the one it was built for.

### 43.4 Verified

`backend npm run check` — **271 tests** (+6, all in the new `uploads-retention.lib.test.ts`: fresh-unreferenced kept, stale-referenced kept, stale-unreferenced selected, a mixed-batch case, empty input, and an exact-cutoff boundary case confirming strict `<` not `<=`), lint/format/typecheck clean, real `npm run build` clean. New config (`UPLOAD_ORPHAN_TTL_HOURS`, `UPLOAD_ORPHAN_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`) added to both `configuration.ts` and `env.validation.ts` — kept in sync, same as every other config value in this file.

**Live-verified against the real container, real Postgres, real filesystem** (a one-off `NestFactory.createApplicationContext` script, deleted after): created three test files directly in the container's `uploads/messages/` with backdated `mtime` (48h-old unreferenced, 1h-old unreferenced, 48h-old but with a real `MessageAttachment` row created against a real thread/message pointing at it) and called `UploadsRetentionService.run()` directly. Result: the 48h-old unreferenced file was deleted, the 1h-old unreferenced file survived (too fresh for the TTL), and the 48h-old *referenced* file survived despite its age — confirming the protection is really keyed on the DB reference, not just age. Test message and any leftover test files cleaned up in the same run; `git status` confirmed no stray files left behind.

Hit the pre-existing nodemon/`tsc --watch` crash race (§29.3/§41.2) once more while wiring this in — same symptom, same fix (`docker restart tavern-backend-1`), nothing new to add to that item beyond one more occurrence.
