## 44. Nested-hover bug had a second, separate copy — plus a real flexbox squish (2026-09-03)

Owner reported (with a screenshot) that the hover/editing chrome still showed on a parent block after §37/§41's device-preview batch, and that the "+" insertion buttons at the edges of a block looked squished.

### 44.1 The screenshot showed the fix from §37 was real but incomplete

§37's device-preview work never touched hover chrome — the bug the owner originally meant then was width/scaling, not hover bubbling. This session's earlier `CanvasBlock.module.scss` fix (`:hover:not(:has(.block:hover))`, applied while building `delete_block`/`move_block`'s UI-adjacent QA) was correct but only covered `.block`'s own outline/toolbar. `BlockInsertCross.module.scss` — a **separate** component wrapping every block (including every nested one) to render its own "+" insertion cross — has the exact same nested-DOM-ancestor `:hover` bubbling shape, in its own independent stylesheet, and was never touched. The owner's screenshot showed exactly this: hovering the hero block's own area also lit up the outer section's insertion cross. Fixed with the identical `:not(:has(...))` guard, applied to both of `.cross`'s `:hover`-gated rules (the faint always-visible guide line's opacity, and the button's `display`).

### 44.2 The squished buttons were a real, independent flexbox bug

`.zone--vertical` (the left/right insertion strip) is `16px` wide; `.button` inside it is an unconstrained flex item with an explicit `width: 22px` and the browser's default `flex-shrink: 1` — wider than its own container, so the flexbox algorithm compressed it along the main (horizontal) axis to fit, while its height stayed at the full `22px` (the cross-axis isn't shrunk the same way) — a circle squeezed into a vertical oval. Confirmed by cropping the owner's actual screenshot: the top/bottom "+" buttons (in a `100%`-wide horizontal zone, never width-constrained) render as clean circles; the left/right ones are visibly taller than wide. Fixed with `flex-shrink: 0` on `.button` — spec-guaranteed to keep its own declared size regardless of a narrower container; it now overflows its 16px zone by a few px on each side rather than being compressed, which is the same "badge centered on a thin guide line" look the zone lines already implied.

### 44.3 Verified

`frontend npm run check` (56 tests, clean) and a real `npm run build`, both clean. Live-verified in a real Chromium session using the actual compiled SCSS output (compiled from the real edited `.scss` file, not hand-copied) against a markup shape mirroring the real nested `BlockInsertCross` structure: confirmed the pre-fix CSS reproduces the bubbling bug (8 buttons visible across two ancestor crosses when hovering the innermost block) and the post-fix CSS shows exactly the 4 buttons belonging to the innermost cross; separately confirmed the button renders as an exact 22×22 square post-fix. Not verified against the live dev server itself — port 3000 is currently occupied by the same recurring stray `next start` process named in §29.3/§41.2 (confirmed by inspecting the process directly: a `next start`/`next-server` pair, no `next dev` running at all), and the owner asked not to touch it this time, so this was verified through an isolated real-browser harness using the actual compiled CSS instead of through the running app.

---

## 45. Builder for a first-time user, phase 1/2: inline rich-text editing + visual block-library thumbnails (2026-09-04)

Owner's brief: make the builder approachable for someone who's never used a computer, informed by how the top competitors (Wix, Squarespace, Framer, Durable, Carrd) do it — remove nothing (presentation-only simplification, agreed explicitly with the owner), implement well. Scoped as a 5-part plan; owner asked to start with the two highest-leverage parts and check in before continuing. This section covers those two.

### 45.1 What the audit found before any code changed

A prior turn's fact-finding pass (dense file:line citations, not summarized here) confirmed two structural gaps against every competitor examined: **zero inline/in-place text editing anywhere** (`grep -rln "contentEditable"` across `entities/website`+`widgets/website-builder` — 0 matches; every text edit required opening the Inspector's side-panel form) and **exactly one "add block" UI reused verbatim in all three places a user hits it** (sidebar panel, "+" insertion modal, empty-state button) — a bare 26px icon + label, with structural primitives (`section`/`container`/`columns`) visually indistinguishable from content blocks like `hero`/`testimonials`, and literally empty (`fields: []`) once added, offering a first-timer nothing to configure in the block's own Inspector tab.

### 45.2 Phase 1 — real inline rich-text editing, not just click-and-type

The owner explicitly asked for *real* formatting (bold/italic/link), not just moving the cursor onto the canvas — meaning `text`/`richtext`/`quote`'s content field stops being a plain string and becomes HTML, with everything that implies for storage and security.

**Reused, not reinvented**: this project already has a working Tiptap editor + backend sanitizer pair for posts (`features/publish-post/ui/PostEditor.tsx`, `backend/src/common/lib/sanitize-post-content.ts`) — same libraries (`@tiptap/react`, `@tiptap/starter-kit`, `sanitize-html`, already installed, zero new dependencies), same defense-in-depth shape (client editor is schema-constrained so it structurally can't produce disallowed markup; server sanitizer is the real trust boundary for direct API calls that bypass the editor entirely).

**New frontend**: `entities/website/ui/EditableText.tsx` (plain single-line click-to-edit — `contentEditable` on the block's own existing DOM node, no format toolbar — used for `heading` and `quote.author`, mirroring the exact commit-on-blur/Enter, cancel-on-Escape pattern already proven in `PagesPanel.tsx`'s inline page-rename), `entities/website/ui/RichTextEditor.tsx` + `EditableRichText.tsx` (a minimal Tiptap instance — Document/Paragraph/Text/Bold/Italic/Link only, deliberately not the full `StarterKit` — mounted *only* while a block is actively being edited, with a `BubbleMenu` toolbar that appears on text selection rather than a permanent toolbar bar, since a permanent bar would be too heavy for a small canvas block). `BlockRendererProps` (`model/registry.ts`) gained an optional `onEditProp?: (key, value) => void`, wired once in `CanvasBlock.tsx` straight into the same `updateBlockProps` the Inspector form already uses — one write path, not two. The Inspector's own duplicate `text` fields were removed from `text`/`richtext`/`quote`'s `FieldSchema` (kept for `heading`/`quote.author`, which stay plain strings on both paths, so no format mismatch there) — editing that field now has exactly one place to do it, avoiding two independent editors of the same field disagreeing on storage format.

**New backend, the actual security-critical part**: `common/lib/sanitize-rich-block-text.ts` — a narrow sibling of `sanitize-post-content.ts`, allowlisting only `p/br/strong/em/a` (no images/tables/divs/classes — this field is formatting-only, not layout). `modules/websites/lib/sanitize-rich-blocks.ts` (pure, unit-tested tree-walker, same split-DI-from-logic pattern as `uploads-retention.lib.ts`/`block-tree.ts` earlier this session) recursively sanitizes `props.text` for exactly `text`/`richtext`/`quote` block types, wired into `WebsitesService.saveDraft` before every write. AI tools (`add_block`/`update_block_props`/`set_style`) were deliberately left untouched — the model still writes plain strings into these fields (which the sanitizer passes through unchanged, having no tags to strip), and a human can layer formatting on top afterward through the new editor.

### 45.3 Phase 2 — visual thumbnails replacing the bare icon grid

`entities/website/ui/BlockThumbnail.tsx` — a live, scaled-down render of the actual block using its own `definition.defaultProps` (the same single source of truth every block already has), not a static screenshot pipeline, so a preview never goes stale when a block's design changes. Uses the same `zoom`-based scaling technique already proven for the device-preview fix (§37.1) — real layout-affecting scale, not `transform`, so overflow clips predictably. Rendered with the *real* current site theme (falling back to `DEFAULT_THEME` if the document hasn't loaded yet) so a preview looks like a piece of this actual site, not a generic sample.

Two categories of block get a hand-drawn SVG diagram instead of a live render, both to avoid showing nothing useful: the 5 structural/empty primitives (`section`/`container`/`columns`/`spacer`/`divider` — `fields: []`, a live render of nothing shows nothing) via a new `BlockDefinition.previewIcon` field, and `dataSource`-driven blocks (`productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid`, auto-detected by `fields.some(f => f.control === 'dataSource')`) plus `web3wallet` (same self-fetch-by-`businessId` risk, but not expressed as a `dataSource` field, so it needed an explicit `previewIcon` rather than being auto-detected — a real, live-verified distinction, not a hypothetical one: opening the block library with `web3wallet` treated as a normal live-render candidate would have fired a real wallet-balance API call with a meaningless empty placeholder `businessId` every time a user opened the panel). Five new icon components (`Preview*Icon`, `shared/ui/icons`) actually draw the layout shape (a full-bleed band vs. an inset one vs. side-by-side columns), not just a repeat of the existing generic nav icon — this is the one piece of this batch that's genuinely hand-authored artwork rather than derived from existing data, kept deliberately simple (stroke rectangles) to stay within what's reasonable to author as code.

### 45.4 Two real bugs found during live QA, fixed before shipping

- **Invalid nested `<button>` markup, a real hydration-safety defect**: `LibraryItem`'s wrapper was itself a `<button>`; several live-rendered block previews (`faq`, for one) contain their own interactive `<button>` internally (an accordion toggle) — nesting one inside the other is invalid HTML and React flagged it as a hydration error the moment thumbnails went live. Fixed by changing `LibraryItem`'s wrapper from `<button>` to a `<div role="button" tabIndex={0}>` with matching `onKeyDown` (Enter/Space) — `useDraggable`'s own `attributes` already supplies `role`/`tabIndex`, so no attribute duplication. `.thumb__stage`'s existing `pointer-events: none` already prevented the *interaction* half of this problem (clicking a preview's internal button doesn't accidentally toggle it instead of adding the block) — this fix closes the *structural* half.
- **`BubbleMenu` with no explicit `z-index`**: could render behind `BlockInsertCross`'s "+" insertion zones (`z-index: 5`), which sit right next to any block being edited — caught live when a real click on the Link button in the bubble menu got intercepted by a "+" zone instead. Fixed with `z-index: 50` on `.bubbleMenu`.

### 45.5 Verified

`backend npm run check` — **295 tests** (+9 for `sanitizeRichBlockText`'s own allowlist behavior, script/img/onerror/javascript:-href stripping, forced link `rel`/`target`; +6 for `sanitizeRichBlocks`'s tree-walking: type-scoping, recursion into nested containers, non-mutation, blocks with no `props.text`), lint/format/typecheck clean, real `npm run build` clean. `frontend npm run check` — 56 tests, clean, real `npm run build` clean.

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running app and real Postgres** (Playwright, real login, real business "Зёрна" — same QA discipline as the rest of this session), after several iterations to get the test methodology itself right (documented here because the false negatives were instructive, not because they were product bugs): a synthetic mouse-drag text selection reliably failed to create a real ProseMirror selection when the target sat at the very edge of the viewport (a test-script artifact — fixed by using keyboard-based selection, `End` + `Shift+ArrowLeft`, which doesn't depend on screen coordinates at all) and an early version of the verification script hit the backend directly from *inside* the browser page context via `fetch`, which is subject to real browser CORS enforcement the moment the dev server isn't on the one whitelisted origin — fixed by moving all backend verification calls to plain Node-side `fetch` with a manually-attached `Authorization: Bearer` header (Node has no CORS concept at all; this also matches how the backend actually authenticates external API calls — a Bearer token, not a cookie, confirmed by reading `session-auth.guard.ts`). Once the methodology was sound: typed real text into a freshly-added `text` block by clicking directly on its canvas content, selected a word with the keyboard, applied Bold via the BubbleMenu button and a link via its own dialog, blurred, and confirmed via a direct backend re-read (bypassing the browser entirely) that the persisted `props.text` was real HTML — `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer" href="https://example.com"><strong>…</strong></a>` — not just a visual change. Separately sent a raw `PATCH` with `<script>`, `onerror=`, `onclick=`, and a `javascript:` href directly to the backend (bypassing the editor, the React app, and the browser entirely) and confirmed via a fresh re-read that every dangerous construct was stripped while the safe surrounding text and the (now-attribute-free) `<a>` tag survived. Confirmed the block library's 45 visible cards render with zero console errors and trigger zero product/service/blog/wallet network calls. Confirmed the public site (`/site/[businessId]/...`) has zero `contentEditable` elements — the new editing surface is structurally builder-only, gated by the same `isEditing`/`onEditProp` pair `BlockRenderer.tsx` (the public renderer) never receives. All test-added blocks were removed afterward via direct API calls, independently re-verified against a fresh session that Зёрна's real page is back to its exact known-good 19-block count with zero stray `text`/`richtext`/`quote` blocks remaining.

**Recurring environmental note, unrelated to this batch's own correctness**: the stray `next start` process on port 3000 (§29.3/§41.2) reappeared *twice* more during this session — once discovered already running (killed, confirmed clean), and a second time within seconds of that kill, forcing `npm run dev` onto port 3001 for the live QA above. Still unidentified; the earlier suspicion that a WebStorm run configuration is the source remains unconfirmed but is now the leading theory (a JetBrains process was observed listening on several other local ports at the same time).

### 45.6 Explicitly not done this batch (3 of the 5 planned parts, deferred pending owner check-in)

Ready-made composed layout patterns (e.g., a one-click "sidebar + grid" built from the existing `section`/`columns`/`column` universal layout system, §37.3) so a first-timer doesn't have to hand-assemble structural primitives for common layouts; simplifying the "Раскладка" tab's `display: flex/grid` CSS-model language into visual layout-shape presets, and the color control's always-visible raw hex input into a curated swatch palette first; mobile-specific polish (larger bottom-tab touch targets, insertion-cross discoverability). None of these are started — the owner asked specifically to stop after the two highest-leverage parts above and review before continuing.

---

## 46. Builder for a first-time user, phase 3/5: one-click ready-made layout patterns (2026-09-04)

Continuation of §45, same owner-approved plan. This slice: "ready-made composed layout patterns" — closes the exact gap named in §45.1's audit (a first-timer choosing "Секция"/"Колонки" from "Структура" gets an empty container and must hand-configure `display`/`direction`/`gap`/`grow`/`sticky` on the "Раскладка" tab themselves) without adding any new capability to the data model — purely a curated combination of what §37.3's universal layout system already supports.

### 46.1 Design

New `entities/website/model/layout-patterns.ts` — 3 patterns (`sidebar`, `two-columns`, `three-columns`), each a small factory function building a real `section > columns > column[]` tree using the exact same `BlockStyle` fields (`display`/`direction`/`gap`/`grow`/`fixedWidth`/`sticky`/`stickyOffset`/`wrap`) already exercised by `set_style` and the "Раскладка" inspector tab — no new fields, no new block types. Each column is pre-filled with a `heading`+`text` placeholder pair (real, instructive copy — "Список категорий, фильтры или навигация — замените этот текст своим," not lorem ipsum) so a newly-inserted pattern is immediately something to *edit*, not another empty container to figure out. The sidebar pattern's `direction` is responsive (`{ desktop: 'row', mobile: 'column' }`) by default — the single most common real "sidebar collapses on phone" pattern, something a first-timer would otherwise need to know to configure by hand.

Insertion reuses the *existing* `insertWidgetBlocks` store action verbatim — a "layout pattern" is structurally identical to a saved Custom Widget (§16.2: an array of `WebsiteBlock` trees with placeholder ids that get remapped fresh on insert), just sourced from this new static file instead of a per-business saved record. Wired into both places a user can add something: `ComponentLibraryPanel.tsx` (new `onAddPattern` override, mirroring the existing `onAddWidget` one) and `AddBlockModal.tsx` (new `handleAddPattern`, refactored alongside `handleAddWidget` into a shared `insertBlocksAtTarget` helper, since the only real difference between "insert a widget" and "insert a pattern" is where the block array comes from).

**Live preview, not a static icon**: new `entities/website/ui/LayoutPatternThumbnail.tsx` renders the *entire composed tree* via `BlockRenderer` (the same recursive dispatcher the public site and Preview use) inside the same `zoom`-scaled thumbnail frame §45.3 built for single-block previews — reusing its CSS module directly. This is a genuinely accurate preview: the sidebar pattern's card visibly shows a narrow fixed column next to a wide growing one, not just a generic "columns" icon, because it's the same rendering path a real page uses.

New section "Готовые макеты" in `ComponentLibraryPanel` — placed *first*, above "Структура" and every other category, so it's the first thing a first-time user sees on opening the library, while the raw primitives (`section`/`container`/`columns` on their own) stay available immediately below for anyone who wants to compose by hand.

### 46.2 Verified

`frontend npm run check` — 56 tests, clean, real `npm run build` clean. Live-verified against the real running app and real Postgres (same Playwright + direct-Bearer-token technique as §45.5): opened the library, confirmed the "Готовые макеты" section renders with zero hydration errors; clicked "Сайдбар + контент"; confirmed via a direct backend re-read (bypassing the browser) that exactly 8 new blocks landed in the expected shape — one `section` containing one `columns` containing exactly two `column`s, the first with `grow:'fixed', fixedWidth:260, sticky:true` and real placeholder `heading`/`text` children, the second with `grow:'grow'`. Cleaned up afterward; confirmed the real business is back to its exact known-good 19-block count.

Not re-litigated here: the same recurring stray-`next-start`-on-port-3000 environmental flakiness (§29.3/§41.2/§45.5) was present again at the start of this slice (found already running, unrelated to this slice's own changes) — noted in `WEBSITE_BUILDER_GAPS.md`, not repeated in full here.

### 46.3 Still not started (2 of 5 planned parts)

Simplifying the "Раскладка" tab's CSS-model language (`display: flex/grid`) into visual layout-shape presets, and the color control's always-visible raw hex input into a curated swatch palette shown first; mobile-specific polish (larger bottom-tab touch targets, insertion-cross discoverability on touch). (§45.6, carried forward)

## 47. Builder for a first-time user, phase 4/5: visual layout icons + curated color palette (2026-09-04)

Continuation of §45/§46, same owner-approved plan. This slice: the last of the two "simplify presentation, keep every capability" items — the "Раскладка" tab's `display`/`direction` choice, and the always-raw-hex color control.

### 47.1 Layout tab — segmented icons instead of dropdown text

`LAYOUT_DISPLAY_FIELD`/`LAYOUT_DIRECTION_FIELD` (`LayoutSection.tsx`) switched from `control: 'select'` to the already-existing `control: 'segmented'` — the exact same mechanism this file already uses for text alignment (`TEXT_ALIGN_SEGMENTED_OPTIONS`, from an earlier slice this session). New `LAYOUT_DISPLAY_SEGMENTED_OPTIONS`/`LAYOUT_DIRECTION_SEGMENTED_OPTIONS` map the existing `theme-tokens.ts` option arrays (left untouched — same "icons don't belong in the token-data file, only in this inspector-specific mapping" convention already established for text-align) onto icons, reusing `RowsIcon`/`ColumnsIcon`/`GridIcon` — the *same three icons* already sitting on the `section`/`columns`/`container` block-library cards, so "how do I stack my content" now uses one consistent visual vocabulary across the picker and the inspector instead of introducing a fourth. Zero new icon components needed. `display`'s three options (stacked/row/grid) and `direction`'s two (horizontal/vertical) are the two fields the plan named specifically as "CSS-model language" — `justify`/`align` were deliberately left as regular selects (already reasonably plain-language: "К началу"/"По центру"/"К концу"/etc, not literal CSS keywords, and 5 options each is more than comfortably fits a segmented row in the narrow inspector column).

### 47.2 Color control — curated theme palette first, raw hex demoted to "Свой"

New `ColorControl` (`FieldControl.tsx`, next to the existing `ScaleControl`) replaces the old always-visible native-swatch-plus-hex-input pair. Reads the site's own current theme colors directly from the store (`useWebsiteBuilderStore`, the same direct-store-read pattern already used by `BlockThumbnail`/`LayoutPatternThumbnail` earlier this session) and shows them as small round preset swatches — Акцентный/Дополнительный/Текст/Приглушённый/Карточки/Фон страницы/Границы, plus white/black — with a final rainbow-gradient "Свой цвет" swatch that reveals the original native-picker-plus-hex-input row on demand. Same principle as every other "preset-first, custom-as-an-explicit-extra-step" control already in this inspector (`'scale'`'s "Свой (px)", `'spacing'`'s custom option) — this was the one remaining control in the whole inspector still defaulting straight to a raw, always-visible technical input with no preset step at all. A field with no value set (meaning "inherit from the theme," a real, common, valid state — e.g. `textColor`/`borderColor` start empty) no longer auto-opens the custom-hex row pre-filled with an arbitrary fallback color that could be mistaken for the actual current value — it only opens automatically when the stored value is a real hex that doesn't match any preset.

### 47.3 Verified

`frontend npm run check` — 56 tests, clean (no new pure logic to unit-test here — both changes are presentational option-list/control-swap, same shape as the already-tested `segmented`/`color` control kinds), real `npm run build` clean. Live-verified against the real running app and Зёрна's real theme (same Playwright + direct-Bearer-token technique as §45–§46): selected a container block, confirmed the "Раскладка" tab's display field renders as three real icon buttons (not text) with the active one visibly highlighted, zero console/hydration errors; confirmed the "Цвет текста" field renders Зёрна's actual theme palette (its real coffee-shop browns and creams, not a generic placeholder set) as round swatches plus the rainbow custom option, with no swatch active and no confusing pre-filled hex value shown for the field's genuinely-empty "inherit from theme" state. Test blocks (added to reach a container to inspect) cleaned up afterward; confirmed the real business is back to its exact known-good 19-block, 11-top-level-block baseline.

Hit two of this session's own now-familiar rough edges while writing the verification script itself, not the product: a synthetic click landed on a nested child's toolbar instead of the intended parent container (fixed by clicking the flex-gap between two known sibling elements — genuinely empty space belonging to the parent, not covered by any child) and a stale-read-before-autosave-completes race identical in shape to the one diagnosed in §45.5 (fixed the same way — a longer wait before re-reading). Both are script-methodology notes, not product defects; recorded here only because they cost real time twice in a row and are worth remembering for the next live-QA script in this project.

### 47.4 Still not started (1 of 5 planned parts)

Mobile-specific polish — larger bottom-tab touch targets, insertion-cross discoverability on touch (§45.6, carried forward). This is the last item of the original 5-part plan.

## 48. Builder for a first-time user, phase 5/5: mobile polish — and a real dead-tap bug found (2026-09-04)

Final slice of the owner-approved plan from §45. Larger bottom-tab touch targets, plus insertion-cross discoverability on touch — which turned out to be a real functional bug, not just a cosmetic one.

### 48.1 Bottom tab bar — readability bump

`.bottomTab` (`WebsiteBuilderWidget.module.scss`) — the only persistent navigation element in the mobile builder (Страницы/Блоки/Настройки) — was noticeably smaller than touch targets already enlarged elsewhere this session (`CanvasBlock`'s 30px action buttons, `BlockInsertCross`'s own 26px buttons on the same breakpoint). Icon 19px→22px, label 11px→12px, padding/gap bumped slightly to match.

### 48.2 Insertion-cross discoverability — a real dead-tap bug, not just "hard to notice"

Tracing the actual CSS (not just the audit's summary) turned up something worse than "subtle": the always-visible faint guide line (`.zone::before`, opacity 0.22, meant as a passive "you can add something here" hint even without interaction) sits on top of a `.button` that was `display: none` until the block is explicitly selected — and touch has no hover step to reveal it first. A first-time user's most natural action, tapping directly on that inviting line, hit **nothing at all**: the only clickable element in that spot didn't exist in the DOM's rendered box yet. Not a discoverability nice-to-have — a broken-feeling dead tap, found by tracing the code, not by guessing.

Fixed by making `.button` render on touch unconditionally (`display: grid` inside `BlockInsertCross.module.scss`'s own `mobileOrTablet` block, same nesting convention already used by `.zone--horizontal`/`.zone--vertical`), but at a dim `opacity: 0.45` in the resting state — full opacity only for `.cross--selected`. This deliberately preserves the exact constraint the original comment already named (§37/earlier: showing full-brightness buttons on every nested block at once turns deeply-nested layouts into "a picket fence of identical circles") — the fix makes every insertion point genuinely present and tappable everywhere, all the time, without reintroducing that visual clutter, because the resting state reads as a quiet marker, not competing UI.

### 48.3 Verified

`frontend npm run check` — 56 tests, clean (pure CSS changes, nothing new to unit-test), real `npm run build` clean. Live-verified on a real iPhone 13 viewport (Playwright's device preset, not just a resized desktop window) against Зёрна's real, actual production content — not a synthetic test page: confirmed the bottom-tab icons measure 22px (up from 19px); confirmed **76 "+" buttons render visibly (`display` and `opacity` both real, not just present-but-invisible in the DOM) across the real page without selecting anything first** — direct proof the dead-tap bug is fixed, not just "should be" from reading the CSS; confirmed tapping one of those dim, pre-selection buttons genuinely opens the "Добавить компонент" modal (the ready-made-layout patterns from §46 rendering correctly inside it, on a real mobile viewport, for the first time); confirmed zero console or hydration errors throughout; confirmed no block was accidentally added by the test's own interactions. A full-page screenshot of Зёрна's real hero/story/features sections with every insertion point visible confirms the "quiet marker, not clutter" design goal — the dots read as small blue seams between sections, the real content stays the obvious focus.

### 48.4 The plan is complete

All 5 parts from §45's owner-approved plan are now shipped: inline rich-text editing (§45), visual block-library thumbnails (§45), one-click ready-made layout patterns (§46), visual layout icons + curated color palette (§47), and mobile polish (§48, this section). Nothing from the original "builder for a first-time user" brief remains unstarted. Real, live-verified next steps from here are either owner-driven (the still-untested "attach a design, get a matching site" Gemini flow, blocked on the owner's own billing action, §41.2/§45.5) or open-ended (further passes at the mobile experience, more layout patterns, extending inline editing to more of the 44 not-yet-covered block types, §45.2) rather than anything already promised and unbuilt.

## 49. Drag-and-drop drop-position indicator, settings-panel polish, and mobile fields go from a two-step dropdown to a plain list (2026-09-04)

Follow-up request after §45-48: drag-and-drop had no visual feedback about WHERE a block would land (only a floating cursor-following label), the block settings tab read as visually flat, and mobile field editing used a deliberate-but-now-reconsidered two-step "pick a property from a dropdown, then edit it" pattern instead of the plain scrolling list every major competitor (Wix/Framer/Webflow/Notion) uses. Owner confirmed reversing that earlier decision explicitly before work started.

### 49.1 Drop-position indicator — the one genuine gap in drag-and-drop

`EmptyDropZone.tsx` already highlighted empty containers correctly via `useDroppable`'s `isOver` — untouched. The real gap was between/around existing blocks: dragging a new block from the library, or reordering/moving an existing canvas block (including across container boundaries), gave no hint where it would actually land until the drop itself.

Added a new ephemeral store field, `dragOverTarget: { blockId, position: 'before' | 'after' } | null` (`website-store.ts`, plain `set()` like `selectedBlockId`/`viewport` — not part of undo/redo history). `BuilderDndProvider.tsx` computes it in a new `onDragOver` handler: compares the vertical centers of the dragged element (`active.rect.current.translated`) and the hovered target (`over.rect`) to decide before/after, skipping empty-zone targets (already handled) and self-hover. `CanvasBlock.tsx` reads it through a point-selector (`state.dragOverTarget?.blockId === block.id ? ... : null`) so `onDragOver`'s frequent firing during a drag re-renders only the one or two blocks whose answer actually changed, not the whole page — and renders a 3px blue line at its top or bottom edge.

### 49.2 Settings panel — a labeled section divider and tab icons

Purely visual, no field/logic changes. `BlockInspectorForm.tsx` gained a small icon-labeled divider ("Оформление и раскладка", `PaletteIcon`) directly above `LayoutSection` — previously that section started with no heading at all and read as an accidentally-appended second form rather than a deliberate second section of the same panel. `LayoutSection.tsx`'s three tabs (Просто/Раскладка/Дополнительно) each gained a matching icon (`PaletteIcon`/`GridIcon`/`MoreIcon`), consistent with the icon-over-word treatment already applied elsewhere in the builder this session.

### 49.3 Mobile field editing — dropdown-and-reopen replaced with a plain vertical list

`FieldGroup.tsx` previously branched on `useMediaQuery` into two real render paths: the desktop multi-column grid, and a mobile/tablet "compact" mode — a `<select>` to pick ONE property, its control appearing below, requiring the dropdown to be reopened to touch a different field. That branch, its `isCompact`/`selectedKey`/`resetKey` state, and the now-dead `.compact*` SCSS classes were all removed. What's left is a single render path: the same field grid at every width, with `FieldGroup.module.scss`'s `@include mobileOrTablet` collapsing it to one column — an ordinary scrolling list where every field is visible and labeled at once, matching the pattern used by every competitor checked. `resetKey` becoming dead code (it only ever existed to reset the old dropdown's selection) cascaded into removing it from all 10 call sites across `BlockInspectorForm.tsx`/`ThemePanel.tsx`/`LayoutSection.tsx`, and then removing the now-unused `blockId` prop from `LayoutSection` entirely, once `resetKey` was its only consumer.

### 49.4 Verified

`npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run check` (56 tests, only the 2 pre-existing unrelated `import/no-cycle` warnings), and a real `npm run build` all clean. Live-verified against Зёрна's real content (not a synthetic page), with genuine multi-step `dragover` cycles (not instant drops) via Playwright:

- Dragging a "Заголовок" block from the library onto the canvas: the drop-indicator line was confirmed **visibly rendered mid-drag** (`display`/`opacity` read directly off the live DOM, not just present-but-hidden), the block actually landed in the document (verified by re-fetching the saved document from the backend — it nested correctly inside the target section, before its sibling, matching the indicator's own "before" reading), and the block count increased by exactly 1.
- Reordering an existing (nested) canvas block via its drag handle showed the same live indicator mid-drag.
- Mobile settings (iPhone 13 Playwright preset): confirmed the old "which property" dropdown no longer exists in the DOM at all, and confirmed 14 field labels are visible simultaneously in a plain scrolling list for a real block.
- Confirmed the three `LayoutSection` tabs with their new icons render correctly for an actual container block ("Секция") — the "Раскладка" tab correctly stays hidden for non-container blocks, unchanged pre-existing behavior this work didn't touch.
- Zero console or hydration errors throughout; all test-added content was removed afterward and the document was confirmed byte-for-byte back to its original 19-block shape and order.

## 50. Competitor-widget-library batch 1/N: 10 new presentational blocks (timeline, tabs, countdown, logo cloud, comparison — 2 design variants each) (2026-09-04)

Owner's ask (2026-09-04): implement the widgets competitors (Wix/Squarespace/Framer-class builders) have, in multiple design variants, as an open-ended creative task with the owner's explicit "decide the scope yourself" latitude, plus a second, separate track — AI-driven creation of genuinely new (not-yet-registered) widget types/styles on demand — which the owner deliberately deferred to a later iteration (see "Not done this batch" below; per [[feedback_bounded_iterations]], asked the owner to pick a starting track via `AskUserQuestion` rather than attempting both — chose "widget library first").

### 50.1 What was built

Five new widget families, each as **two separate registered block types** sharing one `fields`/default-content shape but a different layout — the same convention the existing registry already uses for `team`/`teammember`/`testimonials` and `cards`/`articles` (one engine, one `type` per visual variant, not a user-facing "layout variant" selector inside a single type):

- **Timeline** — `timeline` (zigzag around a center line) / `timelinesimple` (single left rail).
- **Tabs** — `tabs` (horizontal) / `tabsvertical` (side list + panel).
- **Countdown** — `countdown` (large day/hour/min/sec boxes) / `countdownbar` (compact inline promo strip with a CTA link) — both a genuinely live, ticking timer, not a static number.
- **Logo cloud** — `logocloud` (static grayscale-on-hover grid) / `logocloudmarquee` (seamless infinite horizontal scroll, `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware).
- **Comparison** — `comparison` (per-plan cards with check/cross feature lists, same shape as `pricing` but for feature inclusion) / `comparisonsplit` (fixed two-column "us vs. them" table).

All ten registered in the existing `content` category (`frontend/src/entities/website/blocks/content/index.tsx` + `.module.scss`) — chosen over a new category per the existing convention (testimonials/stats/team-style generic widgets already live there). None are capability-gated (pure presentational/free-text — no new capability makes sense for any of them) and none need `richtext`/sanitizer wiring (plain `text`/`textarea` fields only, matching every comparable existing block). The whole file needed `'use client'` added (tabs/countdown need `useState`/`useEffect`) — same file-level-directive tradeoff already accepted for `blocks/business/index.tsx` (FAQ's accordion), converting the file's other 5 pre-existing presentational block types to Client Components too, not a new pattern.

New pure helper `frontend/src/entities/website/lib/countdown.ts` (`getCountdownParts`/`formatCountdownCompact`) — date-diff parsing kept out of the Renderer specifically so it's unit-testable per this repo's actual test convention (`vitest.config.mts`: only `src/**/*.test.ts` pure-function tests, no component tests) — `countdown.test.ts` added alongside it. The live-ticking hook (`useLiveCountdown`, shared by both countdown variants) went through two real iterations: first pass called `setState` synchronously inside the effect body and was rejected by `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`; fixed by moving the actual time computation into the render body (`getCountdownParts` called directly, not stored in state) with the effect doing nothing but subscribing an interval that forces a re-render — the textbook "subscribe to an external clock" shape the lint rule itself asks for.

No `control: 'date'` field type exists in either the frontend `FieldSchema` union or the backend `CuratedField.kind` vocabulary — added one just for these two blocks was judged out of proportion for this batch, so `targetDate` is a plain text field with a format hint (`ГГГГ-ММ-ДДTЧЧ:ММ`) on both sides, same tradeoff already accepted elsewhere in the registry for narrow one-off needs.

### 50.2 AI-tool parity (backend)

Per this repo's existing (independent, manually-curated) contract — new frontend block types are **not** automatically usable by the AI chat's `add_block`/`get_block_schema`/`update_block_props` tools; `backend/src/modules/ai/tools/lib/add-block-schemas.ts` keeps its own allowlist deliberately decoupled from the frontend registry. Chose to do this wiring as part of this same batch (not deferred) so all ten new widgets are AI-buildable from day one, consistent with every one of the other 48 types — added all ten to `AllowedBlockType`/`BLOCK_SCHEMAS`, 1:1 field-for-field with the frontend defaults, plus matching `add-block-schemas.test.ts` coverage (allowlist membership, `schemaNeedsRefs` for the two blocks with `linkTarget`/`mediaAsset` fields — `countdownbar`'s button link, `logocloud(marquee)`'s per-logo image/link — and the registry-count/key-parity assertions bumped 48 → 58).

### 50.3 Self-review found and fixed three real issues

Ran `/code-review medium` on the diff per [[feedback_self_review_each_iteration]] rather than stopping at green `npm run check`/`npm run build`. All three fixed and reverified (full check + build re-run clean on both projects):

1. **SCSS convention violation** (`frontend/AGENTS.md` §6: nesting via `&` is only for a selector's own state/modifier, never a cross-selector descendant like `.block .element`) — three spots used `.logo-cloud__item:hover & { ... }` / `.comparison-plan__feature--excluded & { ... }` inside a *different* element's own rule (parent-state-styles-child pattern). Fixed by moving the hover state onto the hovered element's own selector directly (`.logo-cloud__image:hover` — hovering the wrapper necessarily also hovers the image, since it fills the wrapper with no gap) and, for the excluded-feature icon, giving the icon itself an explicit modifier class (`comparison-plan__feature-icon--excluded`) applied directly in JSX instead of deriving it from an ancestor's class.
2. **Countdown interval never stopped after expiry** — `useLiveCountdown`'s `setInterval` had no dependency on `isPast` and kept forcing a re-render every second forever, even on a long-dead promo left open in a tab. Fixed: skip starting the interval at all if already expired, and self-`clearInterval` on the tick where it flips to expired.
3. **Dead code** — `LogoCloudLogo` had `item.url ? resolveLinkHref(...) : undefined`, but `LinkTarget` (and its `EMPTY_LINK_TARGET` default) is never falsy, so the ternary's else-branch was unreachable; `resolveLinkHref` already returns `'#'` for an empty target. Simplified to a direct call, matching the already-established fallback contract documented on `resolveLinkHref` itself.

### 50.4 Verified

`npm run check` (frontend: 63 tests, backend: 298 tests) and a real `npm run build` clean on **both** projects per [[feedback_verify_with_real_build]] — including recovering once from the exact pre-documented host/container build race (`backend`'s `nodemon`+`tsc --watch` crashed with "Cannot find module dist/main.js" after the host-side `npm run build`; fixed with `docker compose restart backend`, same known remedy, not a new bug).

Real (not curl-faked) end-to-end pass through the actual REST API, since `PublicSiteWidget` turned out to be fully client-rendered (`'use client'` + `useAsyncData`, no SSR data fetch) — a plain `curl` of the public page only ever returns the "Загружаем сайт…" loading shell, discovered while trying to grep rendered content out of it. Instead: registered a real user, created a real business, `PATCH`ed its website draft with all 10 new widgets (realistic props, including the nested list-of-lists shapes like `comparison.plans[].features[]`), published, and confirmed via the genuinely anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId` endpoint (not the owner-only `.../website/public` route, which 401'd as expected) that all 10 blocks and their full prop shapes round-tripped through draft → publish → public intact. Also confirmed the dev server boots and serves pages with zero Next.js error digest, proving `registerBlock()`'s duplicate-type guard and all ten `Renderer`s survive real module evaluation.

**Not verified — honest gap, not swept under the rug:** no Chromium/Playwright available in *this* session's environment (checked: not an installed dependency, no `chromium-cli`) — unlike §49's session, which had live Playwright access. So actual pixel rendering, click-driven tab switching, the countdown's visible tick, the logo marquee's CSS animation, and the mobile-breakpoint layout changes (timeline zigzag→single-column, comparison-split table) were **not** visually confirmed, only reasoned through from the CSS and confirmed structurally sound via the data round-trip above. Flagging for the owner rather than claiming full verification; offer to install Playwright and re-verify visually, or the owner can check via `npm run dev` directly.

### 50.5 Not done this batch (named, not silently dropped)

- **AI-driven creation of genuinely new widget types on demand** (owner's second ask this batch, e.g. "add a search-by-entity widget to the header") — deliberately deferred to its own bounded iteration per the owner's own choice when asked to prioritize; today the AI can only compose/style the ~58 *already-registered* types (via `add_block`/`set_style`), it cannot invent a 59th on the fly. This is a real architecture question (sandboxing AI-authored markup/logic safely, where such a block would live in the registry/schema system, how `get_block_schema` would even describe something that doesn't exist yet) that deserves its own scoped design pass, not a same-batch bolt-on.
- **"AI trains the constructor"** (owner's phrase) — not scoped or designed at all yet; noted here only so a future session doesn't have to re-derive that the owner raised it.
- Only 2 design variants per widget family (matching the existing registry's own convention, e.g. `team`/`teammember`/`testimonials` — 3 at most) — not the "N variants" a mature page builder might eventually have; more variants of any of these five is a small, bounded follow-up if wanted, not a redesign.

## 51. AI-driven widget creation, track 2/2: entity search widget, Custom Entities public exposure (owner opt-in), `insert_custom_widget` AI tool (2026-09-04)

Second bounded iteration of the owner's original two-part ask (§50/§50.5): §50 built a static widget library; this one makes real progress on "AI creates genuinely new functionality," scoped through two `AskUserQuestion` decisions the owner made explicitly before any code: (1) include Custom Entities in the search widget's scope now, accepting the new `isPublic` migration/security surface that entails, rather than deferring it again; (2) also close the pre-existing, separately-documented gap where `create_custom_widget` could save a widget but no AI tool could place it on a page.

Investigated first, before designing anything (per [[feedback_bounded_iterations]] — don't build blind): the codebase already has `CustomWidget` (saved compositions of existing blocks, owner CRUD + `create_custom_widget` AI tool, but no placement tool and no public rendering) and `CustomEntity` (generic EAV data store, owner CRUD + `create_entity`/`add_field`/`list_entities` AI tools, no record-level AI tools, no public exposure at all). Mission §2.4's original architecture recommendation explicitly argued *against* arbitrary AI-authored code/markup, for constrained declarative composition instead — this iteration follows that precedent rather than relitigating it: no new "AI writes code" capability, just (a) a new, genuinely interactive, parametrized "smart" block the AI can configure via the same `add_block` contract as any other block, and (b) letting the AI use what it can already create (`CustomWidget`) by finally being able to place one.

### 51.1 Custom Entities — safe, owner-gated public exposure

`CustomEntity.isPublic Boolean @default(false)` (migration `20260904180000_add_custom_entity_is_public` — applied via `prisma migrate deploy`, not `migrate dev`: the shadow database used by `migrate dev` failed to replay an unrelated pre-existing historical migration (`20260821000000_add_post_images_and_link_previews`, `P3006`, "column imageUrl does not exist") — a pre-existing environment issue, not caused by this change; `migrate deploy` doesn't use a shadow DB and applied cleanly. Worth a root-cause pass if `migrate dev` is needed again soon, not blocking this work). Default `false` because a `CustomEntity` can hold real PII (the dashboard's own example is "Клиенты CRM") — turning one public is a **deliberate, owner-only, UI-driven action** (`CustomEntitiesSection`'s new "Публично"/globe-icon toggle → `PATCH .../custom-entities/:entityId/visibility`, a narrow dedicated endpoint/DTO, not folded into the existing rename-only `UpdateCustomEntityDto`) — **no AI tool exists or should exist for this toggle**, stated explicitly in the service's own comment, not just an oversight to fix later.

New genuinely-anonymous endpoint `GET /sites/:businessId/custom-entities/:name/records` (`PublicSitesController`, same "no `SessionAuthGuard`" controller as products/services/blog posts) — resolved **by name**, not id (`CustomEntitiesService.listPublicRecords`, case-insensitive), because the AI/owner reference entities by the name shown in the dashboard, never an internal UUID; 404 identically whether the name doesn't exist or exists-but-private (same "both causes must look the same to an anonymous caller" discipline as `BlogPostsService.getPublicBySlug`). Named/documented simplification, not fixed this batch: entity names aren't unique per business today (no `@@unique` constraint, nothing enforces it at creation) — a same-named duplicate resolves to the oldest by `createdAt`, silently shadowing the newer one. A real gap, left as-is deliberately: the actual fix (either a uniqueness constraint + migration + validation error path, or switching public lookup to id-based with a friendlier picker UI) is its own decision, not a one-line fix bundled into an unrelated batch.

### 51.2 `entitysearch` — the 60th block type, and the first genuinely interactive/data-bound one in the competitor-widget-library lineage

Lives in `blocks/navigation` (header-search is a navigation aid, same category as `footer`/`breadcrumbs`) — configurable to search up to 4 sources at once (Product/Service/BlogPost — already public — and/or a business's own public `CustomEntity`, referenced by name). Unlike `productgrid`/`servicegrid`/`bloggrid`'s single-hardcoded-entity `dataSource` field kind, a source list needed its own shape (no new `FieldSchema` control invented for it — plain `list` of `{entity, entityName, label}`, matching the "don't add infrastructure for one block" discipline already applied to skipping a `control: 'date'` kind in §50). All matching is a plain client-side substring filter over data fetched once per mount (`entities/website/lib/entity-search.ts`, pure + unit-tested) — the same "catalogs are small, no real search infra needed yet" assumption already measured and accepted elsewhere (ROADMAP §8 Phase 14), not a new one. Product/service results reuse the *exact* `AddToCartButton`/`BookAppointmentButton` components already built for `button`/`cta`'s `addToCart`/`bookAppointment` `LinkTarget` variants (newly exported from `blocks/actions/index.tsx` for this reuse) rather than re-implementing cart/booking actions a second time; blog results link to the real `/blog/{slug}` route the same way `bloggrid` already does.

`useLiveCountdown`'s "compute in render, only tick via an effect-owned interval" shape from §50 generalized into `useEntitySearchIndex`: never calls `setState` synchronously in the effect body (`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`), and deliberately does *not* reset to `'loading'` on a source-set change — stays stale-while-revalidate, matching the countdown hook's own precedent rather than re-deriving a new pattern.

Backend AI-tool parity done the same way as every other type in the registry (`add-block-schemas.ts`, now 59 curated types, `entitysearch` included) — no capability gate (pure presentational + already-public data, like `cards`/`stats`).

### 51.3 `insert_custom_widget` — the other half of "AI creates a widget"

Backend-only AI tool (`insert-custom-widget.tool.ts`) mirroring the *frontend's own* human-driven path one-for-one: `ComponentLibraryPanel`'s "Мои виджеты" click → `insertWidgetBlocks`/`remapBlockIds` (`website-store.ts`) already does exactly this — copy a saved `CustomWidget.schema` into a page with fresh block ids, snapshot not live link. `remapBlockIds` ported to the backend (`lib/remap-block-ids.ts`, recursive on `children` for the same future-proofing reason as the frontend version, unit-tested) since the AI tool has no browser/Zustand store to call into. New `CustomWidgetsService.get()` (thin wrapper over the already-existing `findOwnedWidget`) — the service had create/update/remove/list but nothing to fetch one widget by id, which this tool needs and nothing else did yet.

### 51.4 Self-review (`/code-review medium`) — 8 findings, 4 fixed, 4 deliberately left

Per [[feedback_self_review_each_iteration]], run before declaring the iteration done, not skipped because mechanical checks were already green. Fixed:
1. `entitysearch`'s custom-entity source sent an **untrimmed** `entityName` to the lookup API while only trim-checking it for emptiness — a trailing space in the inspector field silently broke that source forever (caught, swallowed by the per-source try/catch, no error surfaced anywhere). Fixed: trim once, use the trimmed value everywhere.
2. `EntitySearchRenderer` had no render branch for `status === 'error'` — a failed fetch rendered identically to a legitimate "nothing found," masking real backend/network failures from visitors, a direct violation of `frontend/AGENTS.md` §4's explicit error/empty distinction. Fixed: a real error branch, gated on `index.length === 0` so a revalidation failure doesn't hide already-loaded stale-but-valid results (consistent with the hook's own stale-while-revalidate design, not a contradiction of it).
3. **Real duplication, not "three similar lines"**: `add-block.tool.ts` and `insert-custom-widget.tool.ts` each independently reimplemented "is this parent a container, does it accept this child type" and "does the business have the required capability" — a future rule change applied to one would silently not apply to the other. Extracted to `lib/block-placement.ts` (`assertIsContainer`/`findDisallowedChild`/`findMissingCapabilities`, pure + unit-tested, 12 new cases) and both tools refactored onto it; re-verified live afterward (see §51.5) that both the success path and the parent-validation error path still behave identically post-refactor.
4. Byte-identical duplicated SCSS between `.timeline-item__*` and `.timeline-simple__*` from §50 (missed in that batch's own review) — combined into shared comma-selector rules, one definition instead of two that could silently drift.

Deliberately **not** fixed, with reasoning (not oversights):
- Duplicate custom-entity names resolving unpredictably — real, but already named and reasoned through in §51.1 above as its own future decision, not a same-batch one-liner.
- `insertBlockIntoPage` called once per widget block (O(N × pageSize) instead of O(pageSize + N)) — matches the *frontend's own* `insertWidgetBlocks` loop shape exactly (§51.3); widgets and pages are small in practice (same measured assumption as everywhere else in this codebase). A real optimization if it ever matters, not a correctness issue.
- `CustomEntitiesSection`'s visibility toggle calls `refetch()` instead of applying the mutation's own response locally — true, but `handleDelete` in the exact same file already does the same "mutate then refetch" thing; introducing a local-optimistic-update pattern for only the new toggle would make the file *less* internally consistent, not more, for a marginal one-request saving on an infrequent manual action.
- `useEntitySearchIndex` hand-rolls load/error/cancelled state instead of the shared `useAsyncData` — deliberate: `useAsyncData` doesn't support (and shouldn't be bent to support just for this) staying on stale results during a background revalidation, which this widget specifically wants.

### 51.5 Verified

`npm run check` (frontend: 73 tests, +10 for `entity-search.test.ts`; backend: 318 tests, +12 for `block-placement.test.ts` — both re-run clean *after* the review-driven refactor, not just before it) and a real `npm run build` on both projects (backend build hit the same pre-documented host/container `nodemon`+`tsc --watch` race from §50 *twice* more this session — confirmed it happens even running the second build *inside* the container via `docker exec`, not just from the host as originally documented: the race is against the container's own live watcher, not about the host/container boundary — recovered both times with the same known `docker compose restart backend`).

Real end-to-end verification through the actual REST API and, for the two new AI tools specifically, through the actual `ToolRegistryService` (not a mock — the exact same lookup `AiService` uses when the model calls a function), via a one-off script (`NestFactory.createApplicationContext`, deleted after use, never committed):
- Registered a user, created a business, created a `CustomEntity` ("Клиенты") with a string field, added a record — confirmed `GET /sites/:id/custom-entities/Клиенты/records` 404s while private, confirmed the owner-only visibility toggle flips `isPublic`, confirmed the same endpoint then returns `200` with the correct field/record shape, including a case-insensitive name-match check (lowercase URL still resolves).
- Added an `entitysearch` block (two sources: `product` + the new custom entity) to a real page, published, confirmed via the genuinely anonymous `/sites/:businessId` endpoint that it round-trips intact.
- Created a real `CustomWidget` via the owner REST API, then invoked `insert_custom_widget` twice through `ToolRegistryService.get('insert_custom_widget').handler(...)` with a real `ToolContext` — confirmed the page's block count increased correctly both times, confirmed the two insertions produced **entirely different** fresh block ids each time (the snapshot-not-live-link contract), and confirmed the parent-validation error path (`assertIsContainer`) still throws the expected message after the §51.4 refactor.

**Honest gaps, not swept under the rug:** no real Gemini/AI-chat call was made — same pre-existing quota/billing blocker named in §38.4/§41.2, still not resolved by the owner. Tool correctness was verified by invoking the exact same registry/handler path a real model call would use, which is strong evidence but not identical to a live model actually choosing to call these tools with realistic arguments in a real conversation. No Chromium/Playwright in this session (same gap named in §50) — visual rendering of `entitysearch` (the search panel opening, result rows, the "Публично" dashboard toggle) was not seen in a browser, only reasoned through and verified structurally via the API round-trips above.

## 52. Competitor-widget-library batch 2/N: 10 more widgets — carousels, before/after, progress indicators, sticky promo, testimonial spotlight (2026-09-04)

Returned to the owner's original, still-open-ended primary ask ("все виджеты что есть у конкурентов") after two iterations on the AI-creation track (§50-§51) — batch 1 covered 10 patterns, competitors have far more, so this is batch 2/N of an intentionally unbounded series, scoped the same way each time: one bounded, fully-verified slice, not a promise to cover "all" in one sitting.

### 52.1 What was built

Five more families, ten types total, same "shared engine, separate `type` per visual variant" convention as batch 1:

- **Image carousel** (`blocks/media`) — `imagecarousel` (dot navigation) / `imagecarouselthumbs` (thumbnail strip) — arrow/dot/thumbnail navigation only, deliberately **no drag-swipe**: a robust pointer-based swipe that doesn't fight vertical page scroll is a real, separate piece of work, not worth folding into an already-large batch (named, not silently dropped).
- **Before/after comparison** (`blocks/media`) — `beforeafter` (real draggable divider, `clip-path` + `pointermove`/`pointerup` on `window`, keyboard-operable via arrow keys, `role="slider"`) / `beforeaftertabs` (simple labeled toggle, no drag — the accessible/simple fallback of the same idea).
- **Progress indicators** (`blocks/content`) — `progressbars` (horizontal, animates fill on mount) / `progresscircles` (SVG ring, `stroke-dashoffset`) — both share a new `useAnimateOnMount` hook (`requestAnimationFrame`-callback-based, same safe pattern as `useLiveCountdown`).
- **Sticky promo pair** (`blocks/content`) — `stickybar` (dismissible bottom bar, `sessionStorage`-remembered dismissal — deliberately session-scoped, not `localStorage`, so a promo the owner wants everyone to see doesn't silently vanish forever for a visitor who dismissed it out of curiosity once) / `popupoffer` (delayed modal, owner-configurable delay, no real form — a CTA button via the existing `LinkTarget`, not a second form-submission system).
- **Testimonial spotlight** (`blocks/business`) — `testimonialsslider` (one testimonial at a time, reuses the *exact* rating/avatar CSS classes from `primitives.module.scss` that `RepeatablePeopleCards` already uses, so stars/avatars look identical across every testimonial-shaped block on a site) / `quotespotlight` (one large static quote, no list, no interactivity — the simple end of the same family).

New shared `lib/use-carousel.ts` — the "current slide + optional autoplay" state machine was about to be written three times (both image-carousel variants and the testimonial slider); extracted once, matching `lib/countdown.ts`/`lib/entity-search.ts`'s own precedent of pulling out genuinely-repeated logic. No `control: 'date'`-style new field-schema kind needed this batch — plain `text`/`number`/`toggle`/`image`/`link` cover everything.

Backend AI-tool parity done the same batch, same way as always: all 10 added to `add-block-schemas.ts` (registry now 69 types on both sides), `add-block-schemas.test.ts` extended (allowlist membership, `schemaNeedsRefs` mixed-presence coverage, key-parity + spot-check validation for all ten).

### 52.2 Self-review — done manually, not via `/code-review`, and why

The `/code-review` skill (both a direct invocation and a background research fork feeding it) failed outright on a session-wide rate limit (`HTTP 429`, "You've hit your session limit"), not a transient single-call error. Per [[feedback_never_retry_rate_limit]] — a rate-limit failure is a hard stop, not something to retry — the review was **not** relaunched. Instead, ran the same checklist by hand (close, adversarial re-read of the diff specifically for what `npm run check`/`npm run build` structurally cannot catch), per [[feedback_self_review_each_iteration]]'s own fallback ("prefer `/code-review` … when available" implies a manual pass when it genuinely isn't). Found and fixed 3 real issues this way, plus one architecture discovery worth recording:

1. **`stickybar` broke the builder canvas while editing.** Confirmed first (`grep` — no `<iframe>` anywhere in `widgets/website-builder/ui/*.tsx`) that the canvas renders blocks inline in the same document, not in an isolated frame — meaning `position: fixed` inside `isEditing` would punch through the canvas and cover the *real* builder chrome (publish button, inspector), not just the page preview. `popupoffer` had already been built with the correct fix (a non-fixed, non-backdrop preview wrapper while `isEditing`) but `stickybar` hadn't gotten the same treatment — genuine inconsistency within the same batch, not just a missed edge case. Fixed with a `--preview` CSS modifier that drops `position: fixed` back to normal flow only in the builder.
2. **`progresscircles` positioned its percentage label with hand-tuned negative-margin magic numbers** (`margin-top: -56px` / `34px`) to fake centering it over the SVG ring — a direct violation of `frontend/AGENTS.md` §6's own "spacing/typography by tokens, not eyeballed values" rule, and fragile to any future font-size change. Replaced with a `position: relative` wrapper + `position: absolute; top/left: 50%; transform: translate(-50%, -50%)` — the same centering technique already used elsewhere in this same batch (the before/after handle-grip), not a new pattern.
3. **`beforeafter`'s draggable divider used `touch-action: none`**, which blocks *all* touch gestures on that element — including a visitor trying to scroll the page vertically with a finger that happens to land on the comparison image. Changed to `touch-action: pan-y` (native vertical scroll still works; the horizontal drag is handled by JS pointer events regardless of `touch-action`, since that CSS property only governs the browser's own native gesture handling, not JS event dispatch).

**Discovery, not a fix**: while investigating the `stickybar` iframe question, confirmed every single consumer of `WebsiteRenderer`/`BlockRenderer` in the whole app (`PublicSiteWidget`, `BlogPostWidget`, `BlogListWidget`, `BusinessPageWidget`, `PreviewModal`) is `'use client'` and gates real block rendering behind a `status === 'loading'` check fed by a client-only fetch — meaning block-level renderers are, today, **never actually invoked during a real SSR pass with real data** anywhere in this codebase (the SSR shell is always the loading spinner; real content only ever renders after client-side hydration). This retroactively means the hydration-safety precautions taken in §50/§51 (`suppressHydrationWarning` on the countdown digits, the `queueMicrotask`-wrapped `sessionStorage` read here) are defending against a risk that doesn't structurally occur *yet* — not wrong, not removed (harmless insurance against this changing later, e.g. if real SSR data-fetching is ever added), but worth recording plainly rather than silently letting the reasoning look more load-bearing than it currently is.

### 52.3 Verified

`npm run check` (frontend: still 73 tests — this batch added interactive hooks/components, not new pure-function logic, so no new test file, matching this project's own "Vitest only for pure functions" convention) clean both before and after the manual-review fixes; a real `npm run build` clean both times too. Backend `npm run check` clean (330 tests, +12 for the new type/ref coverage) — confirmed via the live dev container's own `tsc --watch` successfully recompiling and staying healthy after every edit, not a separate `nest build` invocation (avoided deliberately this time, having already hit the known host/container watcher race twice in §51 for the same marginal extra signal `tsc --noEmit` already gives).

Real end-to-end verification via the actual REST API on the same long-lived test business from §50/§51: fetched the current live draft (not a stale local snapshot — would have clobbered the blocks `insert_custom_widget` had already written to the real page), appended all 10 new widgets with representative props, published, and confirmed via the genuine anonymous `/sites/:businessId` endpoint that all 10 round-trip intact.

**Honest gap, unchanged from §50/§51**: no Chromium/Playwright in this session — the drag interaction on `beforeafter`, the carousel arrow/dot navigation, the progress-fill animation, and the sticky-bar/popup timing were reasoned through and verified structurally (props round-trip, no runtime import errors, dev container stays healthy), not seen operating in an actual browser.

## 53. Competitor-widget-library batch 3/N: floating contact bubble, animated counters, portfolio hover grids, gallery lightbox, cookie consent (2026-09-04)

Batch 3/N of the still-open-ended primary ask, same discipline as §50/§52: one bounded, fully-verified slice.

### 53.1 What was built

Five families, ten types:

- **Floating contact bubble** (`blocks/content`) — `contactbubble` (single channel — WhatsApp/Telegram/phone/email, real `wa.me`/`t.me`/`tel:`/`mailto:` link resolution) / `contactbubblemulti` (expandable multi-channel menu, `PlusIcon`↔`CloseIcon` toggle).
- **Animated stat counters** (`blocks/content`) — `statscounter` / `statscountericons` — genuinely different from §50's static `stats`: numbers count up from 0 via a new `useCountUp` hook (`requestAnimationFrame`-driven, same safe "setState only inside the rAF callback" shape as `useAnimateOnMount`/`useLiveCountdown`).
- **Portfolio hover grid** (`blocks/content`) — `portfoliogrid` (uniform grid) / `portfoliomasonry` (CSS-columns masonry, natural image aspect ratio instead of a forced crop) — both reveal title/category on hover **and** `:focus-visible` (keyboard users get the same reveal, not just mouse hover).
- **Gallery lightbox** (`blocks/media`) — `gallerylightbox` / `gallerylightboxmasonry` — the existing `gallery` block (§0-era) has never had a full-screen viewer; clicking a thumbnail now opens one with prev/next (click or arrow keys) and Escape-to-close. Disabled entirely in the builder (`isEditing`) rather than given a preview-safe variant — same reasoning as `FormBlock` not really submitting while edited.
- **Cookie consent** (`blocks/content`) — `cookiebar` (accept/decline) / `cookiebarminimal` (single acknowledgement) — the one pair in this batch using `localStorage`, not `sessionStorage`: a consent decision, unlike a promo dismissal, is supposed to persist indefinitely, not just for the session (see `stickybar`'s own comment in §52 for the contrasting case).

Backend AI-tool parity as always — all 10 added to `add-block-schemas.ts` (registry now **79** types on both sides), test coverage extended the same way (allowlist membership, `schemaNeedsRefs` mixed-presence, key-parity + spot-check validation).

### 53.2 Self-review — manual again, and a genuine new lint rule learned

`/code-review` was **not attempted this batch either** — the session-wide rate limit from §52.2 had no confirmed reset signal, and per [[feedback_never_retry_rate_limit]] the safer call was to keep doing the adversarial manual pass rather than risk a second failed subagent call. This time the manual pass caught issues *before* they shipped, not after, because the fixes from §52.2 were applied proactively going in (every new `position: fixed` widget — `contactbubble(multi)`, `cookiebar(minimal)` — got the `--preview` non-fixed treatment from the start; every grid/masonry pair used a modifier-on-the-element pattern from the start, not ancestor-descendant CSS).

One genuinely new thing surfaced by the mechanical checks themselves, not the manual read: ESLint's `react-hooks/static-components` rule (not hit by any earlier batch) fired on `const Icon = contactChannelIcon(...); <Icon />` and the same pattern in `statscounter`'s icon rendering — flagging that a variable holding a component, assigned during render, looks unstable to static analysis even though `contactChannelIcon`/`resolveIconChoice` both just index into a fixed map. This exact rule+justification already existed once in the codebase (`SiteButton`, `blocks/actions/index.tsx`) — applied the same established `eslint-disable-next-line` comment, not a new pattern.

The one thing manual review *did* still catch, and fixed: while re-reading `blocks/content/index.tsx` end to end for this batch, noticed `tabs`/`tabsvertical` (§50, not this batch) had shipped with exactly the same forbidden ancestor-descendant CSS (`.tabs--vertical .tabs__list`, `.tabs--vertical .tabs__tab`) that §51.4 and §52.2 already found and fixed twice elsewhere — missed in both of those passes because neither happened to re-read `tabs`'s own CSS. Fixed the same way a third time (`.tabs__list--vertical`/`.tabs__tab--vertical` modifiers on the elements themselves, JSX updated to apply them directly). Left `primitives.module.scss`'s two pre-existing instances (`.people-card--quote .people-card__text`, `.people-card--team .people-card__byline`) alone — that file predates this whole session, and `frontend/AGENTS.md` §12 is explicit that unrelated pre-existing violations get fixed only when a task's own diff already touches that code, not swept up opportunistically.

### 53.3 Verified

`npm run check` (frontend: still 73 tests, same "no new pure-function logic this batch" reasoning as §52; backend: 342 tests, +12) and a real `npm run build` on both projects, clean before and after the `tabs` fix. Backend container's own live `tsc --watch` recompiled successfully after every edit (confirmed via logs + `/health`), same non-`nest build` verification approach as §52 for the same reason.

Real end-to-end verification via the live REST API on the same long-running test business/page from §50-§52 — fetched the current draft fresh (not a stale snapshot), appended all 10 new widgets, published, confirmed all 10 round-trip intact through the genuinely anonymous `/sites/:businessId` endpoint.

**Named, not fixed**: floating overlay widgets (`contactbubble(multi)`, `stickybar`, `cookiebar(minimal)`, `popupoffer`) can visually collide if an owner adds several to the same page — no stacking/collision-avoidance system exists or was built (would be real, separate, speculative infrastructure for a combination not yet seen in practice — same category of deferred work as §52's "no drag-swipe on the image carousel").

**Honest gap, unchanged**: still no Chromium — the lightbox's keyboard navigation, the counters' count-up animation, the portfolio hover reveal, and the contact-bubble multi-menu expand/collapse were reasoned through and verified structurally, not seen in a browser.

## 54. Closing the no-Chromium gap for real — 2 genuine bugs found by an actual browser that no API-level test could have caught (2026-09-04)

Every single batch since §50 carried the same honest caveat: no Chromium in this session, visual/interactive behavior reasoned through but not seen. Rather than start a 4th widget batch on top of three unverified ones, stopped to actually close that gap — discovered `google-chrome` and a **pre-downloaded Playwright Chromium** already present in this environment (`~/.cache/ms-playwright`), just not wired up as a project dependency. Installed `playwright` in an isolated scratch directory (not touching the repo), confirmed a real headless browser launches, and drove the actual app: set the `tavern_session` cookie directly from an already-issued backend token (no need to re-drive the login form), navigated to the real public site and the real builder for the same long-running test business from §50-§53, screenshotted, and read `page.on('console'|'pageerror'|'response')` for real errors — not simulated, not mocked.

**What this immediately proved**: the timeline zigzag, tabs with active-state highlighting, the countdown showing genuinely live computed values ("118д 02ч 26м 25с", ticking for real), the logo marquee's duplicated track, comparison tables, and a fixed cookie-consent bar all render correctly, pixel for pixel, with zero console errors on first look. Also surfaced, incidentally, a real gap in earlier E2E coverage: the builder page (`/business/:id/edit`) sits behind a plan-selection gate for a `draft`-status business — every prior batch's curl-based verification went straight through the owner-only website-draft API and never hit this UI gate at all, so it was never seen until a real browser hit it.

### 54.1 Two real bugs found, both fixed and reverified

1. **`entitysearch`'s default `product` source 500'd the whole page's Server Action layer whenever rendered with an empty `businessId`** — exactly the situation `BlockThumbnail.tsx`'s own `PLACEHOLDER_BUSINESS` documents as intentional (`businessId: ''`, previews shouldn't need a real business). `loadSearchSource`'s error handling only wrapped the custom-entity branch (§51's own code), never the product/service/post branches — an uncaught rejection doesn't crash the page (React handles it), but the Server Action's HTTP response is still a genuine 500 by the time the exception leaves the server, logged as a real error regardless of what the client does with it afterward. Fixed with an explicit `if (!businessId) return [];` guard *before* attempting any request — the correct fix, not just a wider try/catch (a wider catch was tried first and, revealingly, did NOT stop the 500 from appearing in the server log, which is exactly how this got understood: catching client-side can't undo a server-side response that was already sent).
2. **`contactbubble`/`contactbubblemulti` rendered as completely empty (`return null`) in their own block-library thumbnails** — unlike every other widget shipped in §50-§53, these two were the only ones with genuinely **empty** default values (`value: ''`) rather than realistic placeholder content, so their own "nothing configured yet" guard fired on `defaultProps` itself. Fixed by giving them a plausible placeholder phone number (`380501234567`) as the default, matching the "every block ships with real-looking sample content" convention every other widget in this whole lineage already followed — this was the odd one out, not a new exception.

### 54.2 One more `BlockThumbnail.tsx` fix, and one confirmed non-issue

`BlockThumbnail.tsx` never passed `isEditing` to the live preview render at all — meaning every `position: fixed` widget from §52/§53 (`stickybar`, `popupoffer`, `contactbubble(multi)`, `cookiebar(minimal)`) rendered its real, viewport-anchored production form inside the tiny thumbnail stage, painting content outside the visible card entirely. Added `isEditing` (always `true`) to the `<Renderer>` call — safe for the other 47 pre-existing block types too (form/cart/booking blocks already treat `isEditing` as "suppress real side effects," so this can only make their thumbnails *more* correct, never less). Confirmed via DOM inspection (not just eyeballing screenshots) that `stickybar`/`progresscircles`/`portfoliogrid` were never actually broken — real content is present with non-zero dimensions, just visually subtle at thumbnail zoom scale (a thin bar, thin SVG strokes, a small overlay-on-hover card) — logged as a real finding, explicitly not "fixed" because there was nothing broken to fix, just small.

### 54.3 Verified

`npm run check` + real `npm run build` clean on both projects after the fixes (frontend: still 73 tests — no new pure-function logic; backend: 342 tests, defaultProps sync only). Re-verified live in the browser a second time after each fix: zero `Cannot GET /sites//products` errors in the dev server's own log on a genuinely fresh process start (confirmed by clearing `.next/cache` and restarting, not just relying on HMR), and a DOM-level check (not a screenshot guess) confirming `contactbubble`'s thumbnail now has real, non-empty rendered content.

**Named, not chased further**: one remaining harmless log line, `Тип блока "footer" уже зарегистрирован` — a pre-existing Turbopack dev-mode module-double-evaluation artifact on a block type from long before this session, unrelated to anything built in §50-§54, doesn't affect the rendered page in any observable way. Also recommending `/run-skill-generator` per the `run` skill's own guidance, since getting a working browser driver here took real setup (isolated `npm install playwright`, cookie-based session injection, port-conflict archaeology from this repo's own well-documented stray-dev-server history) that a future session shouldn't have to rediscover from scratch.

## 55. Real interaction testing, not just rendering — every non-trivial widget genuinely operated by a browser (2026-09-04)

§54 confirmed static rendering; this closes the deeper gap named repeatedly since — "reasoned through, not seen operating." Uploaded two real test images through the actual asset-upload endpoint (not placeholders) so the image-dependent widgets (`beforeafter`, `imagecarousel(thumbs)`, `gallerylightbox(masonry)`, `portfoliogrid/masonry`) had real content to interact with, then drove genuine pointer/keyboard input against the live public site — not just `.click()` calls, real `mouse.down()`/`mouse.move({steps})`/`mouse.up()` sequences for drag, real `keyboard.press()` for arrow-key/Escape navigation.
