**12 of 13 real interaction assertions passed outright; the 13th's underlying functionality was independently confirmed by a different assertion in the same run** — full list: `popupoffer` opens on its own after the real configured delay; `tabs` switches content on click; `imagecarousel`'s arrow advances the active dot; `beforeafter`'s divider genuinely moves under a dragged pointer (`left: 50%` → `left: 85%`) *and* responds to `ArrowRight` on the slider handle; `gallerylightbox` opens on thumbnail click, `ArrowRight` swaps to a **different real uploaded image** (asserted via distinct `src` URLs, not just "something changed"), `Escape` closes it; `contactbubblemulti`'s toggle expands its menu; `countdown`'s seconds digit visibly ticks down over real elapsed time; `statscounter` animates from a low value to its final target; `cookiebar`'s dismissal survives a real page reload (`localStorage`, confirmed working end to end).

### 55.1 Chasing three apparent failures to ground — all three were the test rig, not the product

Each "failure" was run to a definitive root cause via DOM/element inspection rather than accepted at face value or dismissed without checking:
- **`imagecarousel` dots/arrows looked broken on the first pass** — `[class*="carousel__dot"]` matched the *container* `.carousel__dots` too (a real substring collision: "carousel__dots" contains "carousel__dot"), corrupting the before/after index comparison. Fixed by switching to the `role="tab"`/`aria-label` attributes already built into the component for accessibility — which then doubled as reliable, unambiguous test hooks. Same fix applied to `beforeafter`'s container/handle lookup.
- **`contactbubblemulti`'s toggle click did nothing** — root-caused with `document.elementFromPoint`, not assumed: the element actually receiving the click was a `NEXTJS-PORTAL` — Next.js's own dev-mode indicator badge, which defaults to the same bottom-left corner this test's `contactbubblemulti` instance also defaulted to. Dev-only (`next dev` exclusive, never exists in a production build), and confirmed resolved outright by moving the test instance to `bottom-right`. Not a product bug; recorded so a future session doesn't re-chase the same false lead.
- **`cookiebar`'s "accept hides it" assertion stayed red even after several fixes** — root-caused by inspecting the live DOM: the test page had **two** independent cookie-consent widgets mounted simultaneously (`cookiebar` *and* `cookiebarminimal`, an artifact of this batch's own earlier test setup, not a realistic single-site scenario), each with its own local `answered` state — accepting one correctly hides only that one, which is *correct* per-widget behavior, not a bug; the test's `.first()` selector just weren't scoped to the specific instance it had clicked. The functionally meaningful assertion — dismissal surviving a real reload — passed cleanly throughout, which is what actually matters (both widgets independently read the same `localStorage` key on mount).

### 55.2 Verified

No production code changed in this pass — pure verification, confirming §54's fixes hold under real interaction, not just static render. Dev server processes cleaned up by exact PID afterward (including one more of this session's own orphaned instances on port 3000, found the same way the others were — not an external mystery, just this session's own leftover background launches never torn down; a pattern worth remembering for any future session driving a browser here).

## 56. Competitor-widget-library batch 4/N: process steps, icon checklists, testimonial wall, announcement bars, pricing toggle, trust bar, video hero (2026-09-04)

Ten more block types — registry now **89** on both frontend and backend. Chosen by first surveying the existing 79-type registry (`grep`-ing every category file's `type:`/`category:` pairs) to rule out duplicates before adding anything, not just brainstorming from scratch — e.g. dropped an initially-planned `map`/`mapsplit` pair once `location` turned out to already do exactly that (address + embeddable map iframe), and kept `iconlist`/`iconlistinline` deliberately distinct from the pre-existing `featuregrid` (flat checklist vs. bordered card grid, not a reskin).

- **Steps** (`steps`/`stepsicons`) — horizontal numbered-or-iconed process steps connected by a line (`::after` pseudo-element per item, own-selector, not an ancestor-descendant rule). One shared render function (`StepsBlock`) with a boolean `iconMode` param, same convention as `tabs`/`tabsvertical` — both variants store the same `{icon, title, description}` item shape, the numbered variant just doesn't read `icon`.
- **Icon list** (`iconlist`/`iconlistinline`) — vertical checklist vs. a horizontal wrap of short icon+text badges (typically placed under a hero button — "free shipping ✓ no-commitment ✓ money-back guarantee"). Reuses `resolveIconChoice`/`ICON_CHOICE_OPTIONS` from the existing icon-choice library, no new icon infrastructure.
- **Announcement bar** (`announcementbar`/`announcementbarmarquee`) — a dismissible top-of-page bar and a separate continuously-scrolling text ticker (same duplicated-track/`aria-hidden`-second-copy technique as `logocloudmarquee`, §50, reused for text instead of logos). Deliberately **in-flow, not `position: fixed`** — unlike `stickybar`/`popupoffer`/the floating contact bubbles, which all fight for the same viewport-anchored space with no coordination system between them (a gap already named and left open in §53). Sidesteps that entire class of collision by not entering it, rather than half-solving it for one more widget.
- **Testimonial wall** (`testimonialwall`) — reuses the existing `RepeatablePeopleCards` engine (already backing `team`/`testimonials`/`reviews`) rather than forking it: added an optional `layout?: 'grid' | 'masonry'` prop, defaulting to `'grid'` so all three existing callers are unaffected. Masonry uses CSS `column-count`, with the `break-inside: avoid`/`margin-bottom` modifier applied as its own `people-card--masonry` class on the card itself — **not** `.people-grid--masonry .people-card` — that exact ancestor-descendant shape has now caused a real bug three separate times this session (§51, §53 twice), so this time the compliant form was used from the start, not fixed after the fact. Real masonry (uneven card heights, visually confirmed via screenshot) reduces to a one-line CSS-var change on an existing, already-tested component instead of a new one.
- **Pricing with toggle** (`pricingtoggle`) — a separate type from the existing `pricing`, not a mode flag on it: `pricing.plan.price` is one free-text field (see its own preserved comment about staying outside the Currency System), monthly/yearly needs two, plus a genuinely new interactive element (an animated switch, `role="switch"`/`aria-checked`, local `useState`, no store — pure per-block UI state per `AGENTS.md` §4). Reuses the *existing* `.pricing`/`.pricing-plan*` CSS from `pricing` unchanged — only the switch itself is new markup/CSS.
- **Social proof bar** (`socialproofbar`) — a compact "avatar stack + star rating + one line of text" trust strip, meant to sit under a hero CTA — deliberately not a full section-with-heading like `reviews`/`testimonials`, a different weight class of widget entirely.
- **Video hero** (`herovideo`) — `hero`'s layered background/overlay/content structure, rebuilt for a real `<video autoPlay muted loop playsInline>` background instead of a `background-image` (a genuinely different mechanism, not a conditional field on the existing type — `hero`'s parallax effect operates on a background-image layer and has no video equivalent). Empty `videoUrl` default (owner pastes their own file link, plain `url` field — no new asset-upload plumbing), matching the same honest-empty-default convention as `hero.backgroundImage`/`video.embedUrl`.

Backend `add-block-schemas.ts` extended in lockstep, inserted at the same relative position as each type's frontend sibling (`herovideo` next to `hero`, `pricingtoggle` next to `pricing`, etc.) so the two files stay easy to diff against each other by eye, not just by count. `add-block-schemas.test.ts`: registry count assertion 79 → 89, new `isAllowedBlockType`/`schemaNeedsRefs` coverage for all ten (including the one deliberately-asymmetric pair — `announcementbar` needs refs for its `linkUrl`, `announcementbarmarquee` needs none, having no link field at all), all ten added to the "spot-check defaultProps against full validation" sample. The automatic `it.each` "defaultProps keys match fields keys" check already covers all ten without hand-listing them — real leverage from that test's existing design, not new test infrastructure.

**Self-review, done by hand again** (same session-wide `/code-review` rate-limit caution as §52/§53) **before** writing any code, not after: grepped the existing registry first (caught the `map` redundancy above), grepped for the ancestor-descendant CSS shape before writing masonry CSS (avoided a fourth repeat of that exact mistake instead of fixing it afterward), and grepped icon usage to avoid accidental UI collisions in the block-library picker. `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**73** unit tests) and a real `npm run build` clean on frontend; `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**354** unit tests, **143** in `add-block-schemas.test.ts` alone) clean on backend, verified against the **live** `nodemon`+`tsc --watch` dev container (not raced — confirmed healthy via `docker logs`, all routes remapped cleanly, no crash) rather than a competing host-side `nest build`, per the container-race lesson learned earlier this session.

**Real end-to-end verification, not just unit tests**: PATCHed all ten onto the same accumulated multi-batch verification page used since §50 via the live REST API, published, and re-fetched through the genuinely anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId` endpoint — all ten round-tripped with their exact props intact. Then, reusing the Playwright/Chromium setup wired up in §54 (no new install), drove a **real browser** against the live public site: confirmed all ten render with zero console errors, confirmed the `pricingtoggle` switch genuinely swaps the displayed price on click (`₴990/мес` → `₴9 500/год`), confirmed `announcementbar`'s close button genuinely dismisses it, and took real screenshots — visually confirmed the `steps` connector line, the `testimonialwall` masonry effect (uneven card heights, not a uniform grid), and `herovideo`'s honest dark-background fallback when `videoUrl` is empty. Dev server torn down by exact PID afterward.

## 57. Competitor-widget-library batch 5/N: real lead-capture (popup + split hero), image hotspots, FAQ tabs, rating summary, page sharing, video testimonial, single-plan pricing, labeled dividers — two real bugs found and fixed (2026-09-04)

Ten more block types — registry now **99** on both frontend and backend. Before designing anything, surveyed the existing 89-type registry (`grep`-ing every category's `type:`/`label:` pairs) the same way §56 did, specifically to rule out a `map`/`mapsplit` idea a second time (still redundant with `location`) and to confirm nothing already covered "share this page" (`sociallinks` only surfaces the *business's own* profiles) or an aggregate rating bar (`reviews` only lists individual review cards).

- **Newsletter popup** (`newsletterpopup`, `blocks/forms/index.tsx`) — the exact same delay/backdrop/`--preview` shell as `popupoffer` (§52), but with a **real** `FormBlock` submission inside instead of a link button — reuses the same real, already-working form-submission engine that already backs `contactform`/`newsletterform`/`simpleform`, not a fourth copy.
- **Hero with split form** (`herosplitform`, `blocks/business/index.tsx`) — the classic "heading left, lead form right" landing pattern, today only achievable by hand-assembling `columns` + `heading` + `contactform`. Collapses it into one type, `FormBlock` embedded directly.
- **Image hotspot** (`imagehotspot`, `blocks/media/index.tsx`) — a photo with clickable markers (percentage-positioned, real `shared/ui/Popover` tooltips) — genuinely new interaction pattern for this registry, not a variant of an existing type.
- **FAQ with category tabs** (`faqtabs`, `blocks/business/index.tsx`) — composes two engines already in the same file (the tab-strip pattern from `content`'s `TabsBlock`, reimplemented locally rather than cross-imported across category-CSS boundaries, + the exact `.faq`/`.faq-item*` accordion `FaqRenderer` already uses) rather than inventing a third UI pattern.
- **Rating summary** (`ratingsummary`) — an aggregate "4.8★, 230 reviews" breakdown bar, distinct from `reviews`' full list of individual quote cards. Reuses `primitives['people-card__star(--filled)']` directly — same star visual language as `reviews`/`testimonialsslider`, not a third star-rendering implementation.
- **Social share** (`socialshare`) — "share this page" buttons (Facebook/X/LinkedIn/Telegram intents + real clipboard copy) — NOT the same as `sociallinks` (that one links the business's own profiles). Reads `window.location.href` via the same effect-deferred pattern as `StickyBarRenderer`/`CookieBarRenderer` reading `sessionStorage`/`localStorage` earlier in the project (default `''` matches SSR, real value set only after mount); clicks are inert while `isEditing` (the builder's own URL isn't the future public one, so a real click there would be misleading, same reasoning as `FormBlock` not really submitting mid-edit).
- **Video testimonial** (`videotestimonial`) — `video`'s `embedUrl` iframe pattern + `quotespotlight`'s author byline, for the case where the testimonial itself is a video, not text.
- **Single-plan pricing** (`pricingsingle`) — one spotlighted tariff/package, for businesses with exactly one plan, where `pricing`/`pricingtoggle`'s `auto-fit` multi-column grid would visually orphan a single card. Reuses the same `.pricing-plan*` CSS as both.
- **Labeled dividers** (`dividerlabel`/`dividericon`) — a horizontal rule with centered text ("or") or a centered icon badge, separate types from the existing `divider` (different prop shape entirely, and `divider` is already live on real sites — changing its shape retroactively was the wrong move, same reasoning applied to `steps` vs `pricing` in §56).

**Two real, reproducible bugs found by actually driving a browser, not just reading the code — both fixed, not just documented:**
1. **`imagehotspot` had a genuine layout-shift bug**: `.hotspot__image` used `height: auto` with no reserved `aspect-ratio`, so before the uploaded photo finished loading, the container collapsed toward zero height — since the hotspot markers are positioned as *percentages of the container*, they briefly rendered on top of whatever page content came after the block, not on top of the photo. Caught concretely by a real Playwright click failing with "`<div class="...divider-label">` intercepts pointer events" (later, once a slower widget pushed the timing further, "`<div class="...form-popup__backdrop">` intercepts pointer events") — not a guess, the actual DOM element blocking the click named exactly what was on top. Fixed by giving `.hotspot` a fixed `aspect-ratio: 4/3` and making `.hotspot__image` an absolutely-positioned `object-fit: cover` layer (same technique as `.video__frame`/`.hero-video__bg` elsewhere in this same registry) — the container now has real dimensions before the image ever loads, so the percentage math is correct from the first paint.
2. **`herosplitform`/`newsletterpopup` submissions genuinely 400'd on the real backend** — traced via `docker logs` to an exact `Bad Request Exception` on `POST /sites/:id/form-submissions`, then to its cause: `CreateFormSubmissionDto.formType` is validated with `@IsIn(FORM_SUBMISSION_TYPES)`, a **second, independent** curated allowlist (`backend/src/modules/form-submissions/form-submissions.types.ts`) that this session had never touched before — completely separate from the `add-block-schemas.ts` allowlist that gates the AI's `add_block` tool. Registering a new block type there was not enough; the value passed as `formType` at actual submission time needed its own allowlist entry too. Fixed by adding `newsletterpopup`/`herosplitform` to `FORM_SUBMISSION_TYPES`, plus display labels in the dashboard's `FormsSection.tsx` (`FORM_TYPE_LABELS`, which already degraded gracefully to the raw string rather than crashing — a display-quality gap, not a functional one, fixed anyway since already there). **Named for the future**: this project now has at least two independent "list of block/form types" allowlists that must be updated in lockstep with any new form-embedding block type — worth a comment trail (added) rather than a shared constant, since the two lists genuinely serve different concerns (AI-tool prop validation vs. real-submission-type validation) and forcing them into one shared source would couple two unrelated modules.

Self-review done by hand again (same rate-limit caution as §52/§53/§56) — this time BEFORE writing code where possible (grepped for the ancestor-descendant CSS shape and the redundant-widget risk up front, per §56's lesson), but the two bugs above were only found by the Chromium pass *after* implementation, which is exactly why that pass stayed mandatory rather than being skipped as "probably fine, same pattern as before." `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**73** unit tests) and a real `npm run build` clean on frontend both before and after the two fixes; `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**366** unit tests, **155** in `add-block-schemas.test.ts` alone) clean on backend; confirmed the live dev container restarted cleanly (not raced) after every change via `docker logs`.

**Real end-to-end verification**: round-tripped all ten through the live REST API (patch → publish → anonymous `GET /sites/:id` fetch) on the same accumulated multi-batch page first, then — once the two bugs surfaced there — built a second, **clean, single-purpose** business+page containing only these ten blocks (plus one real uploaded test image) specifically to isolate root cause from the giant kitchen-sink page's own cross-widget noise (the same lesson §55 already drew about that page not being a realistic scenario). On that clean page, driven with real Chromium: `imagehotspot`'s marker click now lands correctly and opens its popover with the right label; `faqtabs`' category-tab click swaps to the right question (scoped past a real selector collision with batch 1's `tabs` block, which happens to reuse the exact same default category labels — same class of trap named in §55.1); `socialshare`'s copy-link button genuinely writes to the clipboard (`context.grantPermissions`) and shows the confirmation text; and — the most meaningful check in this batch — `herosplitform` and `newsletterpopup` both completed a **real end-to-end form submission** (fill → real `POST` → real success message replacing the form), the first time in this whole widget-library effort a Playwright pass has verified a *write*, not just a render or a client-side interaction.

## 58. Competitor-widget-library batch 6/N: photo timeline, scarcity/event/pricing-calculator widgets, team socials, generic accordion, branch list, scroll utilities — a real cross-cutting scroll-container bug found and fixed (2026-09-04)

Ten more block types — registry now **109** on both frontend and backend. Same survey-first discipline as §56/§57: grepped the full 99-type registry before designing anything, which killed a planned `bloggridfiltered` idea outright — `BlogPost` has no category/tag field at all (`entities/blog-post/model/types.ts`'s own comment: "сознательно узкий v1: без Author/Category/Tag/Comment"), so a filter-by-category widget would have had nothing real to filter by. Building it anyway would have meant either fabricating categories (banned by this project's own "no mock data" discipline) or silently doing nothing — dropped before a line of code was written.

- **Timeline with photo** (`timelinemedia`, `blocks/content/index.tsx`) — the `timeline`/`timelinesimple` zigzag pattern, extended with a per-milestone image. While touching this block's CSS, fixed a **pre-existing ancestor-descendant violation** missed by all three prior self-review passes (§51, §53, and again in §53's own retrospective on §50): `.timeline-item:nth-child(even) .timeline-item__content` — the exact forbidden shape (`AGENTS.md` §6), just never re-read since §50 shipped it. Replaced with a `--alt` modifier class applied from JSX (`index % 2 === 1`), the same fix pattern used three times already this session.
- **Scarcity bar** (`scarcitybar`) and **pros/cons** (`proscons`) — straightforward, low-risk additions; `scarcitybar`'s numbers are owner-entered, not a live order counter (same honest-free-text convention as `pricing.plan.price`), explicitly **not** a fake "someone just bought this" activity ticker — considered and rejected: this app has no real per-widget purchase-activity data source, and fabricating one would cross the same "no mock data" line the rest of the project holds to.
- **Event countdown** (`eventcountdown`) — reuses the existing `useLiveCountdown` hook and `formatCountdownCompact` (`lib/countdown.ts`) rather than inventing a third countdown-rendering path; adds a real "add to calendar" link built as a pure, deterministic function of `props` (`buildGoogleCalendarUrl` — a real Google Calendar deep link with correctly-formatted UTC timestamps), safe to compute directly in the render body with no effect needed since it never depends on "now".
- **Team with socials** (`teamsocial`) — not a fork of `RepeatablePeopleCards`: added an optional `socialLinks?` field to the shared `PersonCardItem` type (backward-compatible, `team`/`testimonials`/`reviews`/`testimonialwall` all unaffected), same extension technique as `layout` in §56.
- **Pricing calculator** (`pricingcalculator`) — the first real `<input type="range">` in this whole widget library; a plain client-`useState` slider (`units`) recomputes `basePrice + pricePerUnit × units` on every drag, no backend round-trip.
- **Generic accordion** (`accordionlist`) — literally reuses `FaqRenderer`'s own `.faq`/`.faq-item*` CSS classes, just with "title/content" field names instead of "question/answer" — same visual engine, different semantic framing (menu sections, product specs, anything collapsible that isn't a question).
- **Branch list** (`locationslist`) — deliberately **not** a multi-pin map: a real interactive multi-marker map needs the Maps JavaScript API and a paid API key this project doesn't have (the existing `location` block's static iframe embed only ever supports one address). Chose the honest, buildable version — a list of branch cards (name/address/phone/hours) — over either faking multi-pin support or blocking the whole idea on infra the project doesn't have.
- **Scroll progress bar** (`scrollprogress`) and **back-to-top button** (`backtotop`) — new `utility` category entries.

**One real, cross-cutting bug found by Chromium, not by reading the code**: both `scrollprogress` and `backtotop` were first written listening to `window`'s `scroll` event and reading `window.scrollY`/calling `window.scrollTo(...)` — the obvious, standard-web approach, and it silently did nothing on every real page of this site. Root cause, confirmed by walking the live DOM: the entire public site (and Preview) scrolls inside a custom `ScrollArea` component (`shared/ui/ScrollArea`, a JS scrollbar over a hidden native one — see `AGENTS.md` §6), whose `scroll` event does **not** bubble to `window`. This exact fact was already discovered and solved once before in this codebase, for a completely different reason: `useParallaxOffset`'s own comment spells it out almost verbatim, solving it there via `getBoundingClientRect()` polling (works for tracking one element's position, not applicable to "how far down the whole page is scrolled"). Fixed by writing a new shared hook, `entities/website/lib/use-scroll-position.ts` (`useScrollPosition`): walks up from a ref'd DOM node through `parentElement`, computed-`overflow-y`, and `scrollHeight > clientHeight` to find the *real* scrolling ancestor (falling back to `window` if none exists, so the hook degrades correctly on any future page that does use native document scrolling), then listens for `scroll` and exposes `scrollTop`/`scrollableHeight`/`scrollToTop()` against whichever element that turns out to be. `backtotop` needed a small structural fix to use it correctly: the button only renders while `visible`, but the hook can't find the scrollable ancestor until *some* DOM node from this block is mounted and ref'd — so the button is now wrapped in an always-mounted `display: contents` anchor div that carries the ref, with the actual `<button>` conditionally rendered inside it, not gating the ref itself.

Self-review done by hand again (session-wide `/code-review` rate-limit still in effect) — grepped for redundant-widget risk and the ancestor-descendant CSS shape before writing code (per §56/§57's now-established habit), which is exactly how the pre-existing `timeline` violation surfaced this time — reviewing code adjacent to what's being changed, not just the new code itself. `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**73** unit tests) and a real `npm run build` clean on frontend both before and after the scroll-container fix; `npm run check` (lint/format/typecheck/**378** unit tests, **167** in `add-block-schemas.test.ts` alone) clean on backend; confirmed the live dev container restarted cleanly via `docker logs` after every change.

**Real end-to-end verification**: round-tripped all ten through the live REST API on a clean, isolated single-purpose test page (continuing §57's "avoid the kitchen-sink page's own noise" practice) with one real uploaded test image for `timelinemedia`. Real Chromium pass, 15 assertions, all passing after the scroll-container fix and after chasing two more test-harness false leads to ground (not accepted at face value): dragging the pricing-calculator's slider genuinely recomputes the total (`490 + 90 × units`, verified exactly: 5 units → `940 ₴/мес`, 30 units → `3 190 ₴/мес`); the generic accordion's click genuinely toggles `aria-expanded`; the event-countdown's calendar link is a real, correctly-encoded Google Calendar URL; `teamsocial`'s social icon carries the real configured href; and a real dev-server HMR artifact (`"Тип блока \"embed\" уже зарегистрирован"` — the same class of module-double-evaluation quirk this session has already logged once before for `footer`, harmless and dev-only) was correctly diagnosed via the dev server's own log rather than mistaken for a product bug, and cleared with a full server restart + cache clear, not a code change.

## 59. Real production bug found and fixed: anonymous site visitors never saw real business contact info — plus competitor-widget-library batch 7/N (2026-09-05)

### 59.1 The bug, found while designing a new widget, not while looking for one

Building a genuine "open now" badge (`businesshours`) required real structured `Business.workingHours` data to be readable inside block renderers. Tracing how business data actually reaches a block renderer (`BlockBusinessContext`, `model/registry.ts`) surfaced something unrelated to the widget itself: **the truly anonymous public site (`PublicSiteWidget.tsx`, `/site/[businessId]`) has hardcoded `email: null, phone: null, address: null, socialLinks: []` since the day `contact`/`location`/`sociallinks` blocks shipped** — meaning any real business using those three block types has been showing **blank** contact information to every real, anonymous visitor of their actual public site, for the entire lifetime of this widget-library effort (§50 onward). The builder/owner preview looked correct (it reads a separate, fuller `Business` object), which is exactly why this went unnoticed — the one path that matters for real visitors was never exercised by any of this session's own verification passes, all of which used the builder/preview or the raw JSON response, never checked the *rendered* contact block against real data on the truly public route.

Root cause: `WebsitesService.getPublic()` — the single method backing both the fully-anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId` and the logged-in-but-still-limited `GET /businesses/:id/website/public` — builds a deliberately curated `WebsitePublicDto.business` object (with its own "why is this field safe to expose anonymously" comments per field) that simply never included `email`/`phone`/`address`/`socialLinks`/`workingHours`. Not a typo; a real gap in the curated list, silently compensated for by hardcoded nulls at both call sites.

**Fixed at the root, not patched at the symptom**: added the five fields to `WebsitePublicDto.business` (backend `websites.types.ts`, imports `SocialLinkDto`/`WorkingHours` from the `businesses` module) and populated them in `WebsitesService.getPublic()` from the already-fetched `Business` row (`Prisma`'s `include: { business: true }` already had this data in memory — the fix is purely "stop discarding it"). Extended the frontend's shared `PublicWebsite` type (`get-public-website.ts`) to match, then fixed both real consumers: `PublicSiteWidget.tsx` (the actual anonymous-visitor bug) and `BusinessPageWidget.tsx`'s non-owner branch (a Tavern user browsing someone else's `/business/[id]` page was, for the same root-cause reason, seeing less than an anonymous stranger already sees on the same business's real site — inconsistent and, per its own now-stale comment citing the exact backend limitation just fixed, not an intentional privacy boundary, just the same bug's second symptom). Also added `workingHours` to `BlockBusinessContext` itself (`registry.ts`) — required by every one of the 6 places across the app that construct this object, which TypeScript's own required-field check enumerated exactly (no manual hunting needed once the field was added).

**Verified as fixed, not just plausible**: real REST round-trip through the genuinely anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId` confirmed real `email`/`phone`/`address`/`socialLinks`/`workingHours` in the response; real Chromium pass against the live public site confirmed the `contact`/`sociallinks` blocks now render the actual configured email/phone/social link (address rendering was independently confirmed via `getdirections`'s own assertion plus a direct DOM query, after a same-page duplicate-text collision made the first, broader assertion throw Playwright's own strict-mode error — a test-script issue, chased to ground rather than accepted as a real failure, same discipline as every prior batch's false leads).

### 59.2 Competitor-widget-library batch 7/N: ten more block types, mostly capitalizing on the same real business data

Registry now **119** on both frontend and backend.

- **Live "open/closed" badge** (`businesshours`) — the widget that prompted §59.1, genuinely bound to `Business.workingHours`, computing state client-side from real wall-clock time (recomputed every 60s, first read deferred to a `queueMicrotask` per the project's established SSR-safety convention).
- **Get directions** (`getdirections`) — a real Google Maps directions link built purely from `business.address`, no embed URL to configure (unlike `location`).
- **Two audience paths** (`dualcta`) — for sites serving two distinct audiences from one hero moment, not a third `hero`/`herovideo`/`herosplitform` variant (those all lead one visitor to one goal; this deliberately forks).
- **Video testimonial slider** (`videotestimonialslider`) — composes two already-shipped pieces (the `useCarousel` engine from `testimonialsslider`, the iframe/placeholder/byline markup from `videotestimonial`, §57) rather than building a third carousel from scratch.
- **Coupon code box** (`couponcode`) — real clipboard copy, same pattern as `socialshare` (§57).
- **Sticky countdown bar** (`stickycountdownbar`) — the fixed-position sibling of the existing in-flow `countdownbar`, same relationship as `stickybar`/`announcementbar` (§56).
- **Callout box** (`calloutbox`) — a simple colored info/success/warning note box.
- **Video gallery** (`videogallery`) — a grid of multiple embedded players, reusing `video`'s own `.video__frame`/`.video__caption` CSS per cell rather than inventing new video-embed styling.
- **Anchor navigation dots** (`anchornav`) — floating side rail of real anchor links; deliberately **without** scroll-driven active-section highlighting, which would require either fragile cross-block DOM scanning or pixel-accurate manual configuration — shipped the version that's simply correct, not the version that's impressive but brittle.
- **Exit-intent popup** (`exitintentpopup`) — reuses `popupoffer`'s exact card/backdrop shell (§50/§52) with a different trigger: real cursor-to-top-of-viewport detection (`mouseleave` + `clientY <= 0`) instead of a delay timer, gated to once per session via the same `sessionStorage` convention already used for bar-dismissal elsewhere in this file.

Before writing any of the ten, surveyed the 109-type registry first (per §56/§57's established habit) and it earned its keep again: dropped a considered "team org chart," "instagram-style grid," and a fake "someone just bought this" activity popup — the last one rejected specifically because this project has no real per-widget purchase-activity data source, and fabricating one would be the exact "mock data" this project's own conventions forbid.

**Verified for real**: `npm run check` clean on both projects (frontend 73 tests, backend 390 tests incl. 179 in `add-block-schemas.test.ts`), real `npm run build` clean on frontend, all ten round-tripped through the live REST API on a clean isolated business seeded with genuinely real contact info and working hours (not placeholders), and a full real Chromium pass (18 assertions) confirmed every interaction — including the exit-intent popup genuinely triggering on a real dispatched `mouseleave` event, the coupon code's real clipboard write verified by reading the clipboard back, and the sticky countdown bar's live tick and dismissal — with one false lead (a duplicate-text selector collision) chased to ground rather than logged as a bug.

## 60. Competitor-widget-library batch 8/N: data table, mini-quiz, big-number stat, audio embed, captioned image, platform ratings, searchable FAQ, native share, team slider, scroll cue (2026-09-05)

Registry now **129** on both frontend and backend.

- **Data table** (`datatable`) — a genuinely generic `headers[]`/`rows[].cells[]` grid (spec comparison, price list, schedule), not a third `comparison`/`comparisonsplit` variant — those are fixed two/three-column plan comparisons with feature-check semantics; this is an arbitrary NxM table with plain text cells.
- **Mini-quiz** (`quizsingle`) — deliberately a single question with per-choice result text, not a multi-step branching quiz engine — that would need its own "current step" state model this project has no real request for yet (same "don't build it in advance" discipline as `anchornav`'s §59 note on skipping scroll-spy).
- **Big-number stat** (`statshero`) — one large animated number as a standalone section accent, reusing `statscounter`'s existing `useCountUp` hook rather than a third counting implementation; distinguished from `statscounter`/`statscountericons` (§55) by showing exactly one number, large, not a row of several.
- **Audio embed** (`audioembed`) — the audio sibling of `embed`/`video`, for a podcast/track/audio-testimonial iframe URL (Spotify/SoundCloud "embed" links), same XSS-safety reasoning as `embed` (§56): only a URL is stored, never raw HTML.
- **Captioned image** (`imagecaption`) — a single photo with an eyebrow/caption overlay anchored left/center/right, for a simple "our story"-style image moment; distinct from `beforeafter`/`imagehotspot`/`gallerylightbox` (no comparison, no hotspots, no lightbox — just one photo with text over it).
- **Platform ratings** (`platformratings`) — a small row of "Google 4.8 (230)"-style platform score cards; distinct from `ratingsummary` (§55, one aggregate score with a distribution bar) — this is several independent external platform scores side by side, real numbers the business enters, not scraped.
- **Searchable FAQ** (`faqsearch`) — client-side substring filter over question+answer text with a configurable empty-state message; the search variant of `faq`/`faqtabs`/`accordionlist` (§55/§58) for sites with a long FAQ list where scanning becomes the friction, not accordion depth.
- **Native share button** (`nativesharebutton`) — a single button invoking the device's native share sheet (Web Share API), distinct from `socialshare`'s (§55) fixed row of platform-specific share links — for a mobile-first "share this page" moment that lets the OS pick the destination instead of the site enumerating platforms.
- **Team slider** (`teamslider`) — one team member at a time, large, reusing `testimonialsslider`'s exact `useCarousel` engine and `.testimonial-slide__nav` CSS (§57) rather than a fourth carousel implementation; distinct from `team`/`teamsocial` (grids) the same way `testimonialsslider` is distinct from `testimonials`/`reviews`.
- **Scroll cue** (`scrollcue`) — a small bouncing-arrow anchor link inviting the visitor to keep scrolling, typically placed at the end of a `hero`-like section; a plain `LinkTarget`/`resolveLinkHref` link like any button in the registry, just styled as an animated hint rather than a labeled button.

Surveyed the 119-type registry first, per established habit: rejected a fourth generic carousel wrapper (already covered three separate ways), a "table of contents" widget (would need fragile cross-block DOM heading-scanning to build automatically — the same category of shortcut already rejected for `anchornav`'s active-state highlighting, §59), and a fake live-visitor-counter/"X people viewing now" widget (no real per-page-view data source exists in this project — would be exactly the fabricated data this project's conventions forbid).

**Verified for real**: `npm run check` clean on both projects (backend 402 tests incl. 191 in `add-block-schemas.test.ts`, count assertion 119→129), real `npm run build` clean on frontend, backend `tsc --noEmit` confirming the `AllowedBlockType` union and `BLOCK_SCHEMAS` map stay in exact lockstep (TypeScript's own completeness check), all ten round-tripped through the live REST API (including a real uploaded image for `imagecaption`/`teamslider`'s photo field), and a full real Chromium pass (16 assertions, zero console errors) confirmed every interaction — the quiz's choice-then-restart flow, the stats-hero count-up settling on the exact configured number, the FAQ search actually filtering by substring (both a real match and the configured no-match empty state), and the team slider's carousel nav genuinely advancing to the second member — with one false lead (a click landing on the non-interactive `.testimonial-slide__nav` wrapper div instead of the actual arrow `<button>` inside it) chased to ground and fixed in the test script, not logged as a product bug.

## 61. Inline canvas editing extended from 4 to 68 block types, via one shared lever (2026-09-05)

§45 shipped inline canvas text editing (`EditableText`/`EditableRichText`, click text on the canvas instead of only the inspector sidebar) for exactly 4 block types: `heading`/`text`/`richtext`/`quote`. §6 of `WEBSITE_BUILDER_GAPS.md` flagged this as a real, bounded next slice — this closes it, going far beyond the "card titles, button labels" scope originally sketched, by finding the actual highest-leverage lever instead of touching blocks one at a time.

**The lever**: `SectionHeading` (`blocks/shared/SectionHeading.tsx`) is the shared eyebrow/heading/description header used by roughly half the business/content registry — 45 direct call sites across 6 files, plus 4 shared repeatable-grid engines (`RepeatableIconCards`/`RepeatablePeopleCards`/`RepeatableSimpleCards`/`FormBlock`) that themselves render it once and get reused by another 12 block types on top. Making `SectionHeading` itself support inline editing — three new optional props, `editable`/`onEditEyebrow`/`onEditHeading`/`onEditDescription`, each field independently gated so a block missing e.g. `description` doesn't get an editable phantom field — and wiring it through every call site converts dozens of block types in one mechanical, low-risk pass, rather than bespoke work per block.

**Mechanical wiring, done with a script, verified with the compiler**: `onEditProp`'s type (`registry.ts`) widened from `(key: string, value: string) => void` to `(key: string, value: unknown) => void` (a safe widening — the one real sink, `CanvasBlock.tsx`'s `updateBlockProps`, already accepted `Record<string, unknown>`). Then a small Python script (kept in the session scratchpad, not committed) parsed each block file's renderer functions by brace-counting, found every `<SectionHeading eyebrow={props.X} heading={props.Y} .../>` call, and mechanically added `isEditing`/`onEditProp` to the enclosing function's destructuring plus the matching `editable`/`onEdit*` attributes — including correctly detecting the one call site (`quizsingle`) where the visual "heading" slot is actually backed by a differently-named prop (`question`), so the generated `onEditHeading` callback writes to the right key. Five call sites used a shared internal `XBlock` helper (`StepsBlock`/`IconListBlock`/`TabsBlock`/`StatsCounterBlock`/`PortfolioBlock`, each backing two registered types) with a different, non-`BlockRendererProps` signature the script couldn't safely rewrite — those five were fixed by hand, then re-running the script (now skip-aware — it detects an already-migrated tag via a JSON-safe `if ('editable' in attr_dict) continue` guard) confirmed no leftover unmigrated call sites. Every edit was verified against the real TypeScript compiler (`tsc --noEmit`) immediately, not just visually reviewed — the actual backstop against a mechanical, high-volume edit like this producing a subtly wrong prop-key mapping somewhere in 45+ call sites.

**Beyond the shared lever**: five high-traffic blocks with their own bespoke, non-`SectionHeading` markup — `hero`, `herovideo`, `herosplitform`, `about`, `banner` — were converted by hand to use `EditableText` directly against their own existing CSS classes (no visual change, just made the existing static `<span>`/`<h1>`/`<p>` interactive), since `hero` alone is likely the single most-used block in the entire registry and was worth the individual attention the mechanical pass couldn't give it.

**Deliberately out of scope this round**: nested list-item text — a card's own title inside `cards`/`services`/`featuregrid`, a team member's name inside `team`/`teamslider`, a testimonial's quote text — stays inspector-form-only. Editing one item's field requires patching one element of an array prop, not a flat string key; `onEditProp`'s value was widened to `unknown` specifically so a future slice can do this (a block computes a new `items` array and calls `onEditProp('items', updatedItems)`), but wiring per-item `EditableText` UI on top of that for every list-shaped block is real, separate work, not a trivial extension of this pass. Button/link labels were also deliberately skipped after inspection: `SiteButton` (`blocks/actions/index.tsx`) renders its label inside a real `<a href>`, and the canvas has no existing click-guard against real anchor navigation (confirmed by grep — no `preventDefault`/capture-phase handler anywhere in `widgets/website-builder`) — making that text clickable-to-edit would make the FIRST click on it navigate away from the builder instead, a real regression risk for a cosmetic win, not attempted.

**A real, narrow UX finding, verified and documented rather than papered over**: the pre-existing block-selection toolbar (`CanvasBlock.module.scss`, `.block__toolbar`, `top: 6px; left: 6px`, shown on hover/selection) fully occludes the SectionHeading `eyebrow` specifically for `align="left"` instances (`FormBlock` and several `align="left"` business/content blocks) when the block has no top padding — both sit at the same top-left corner, and the toolbar is a solid bar (drag grip + type label + delete button), not mostly-empty space a `pointer-events: none` passthrough could meaningfully fix. Confirmed via a dedicated Playwright reproduction (`debug-toolbar-overlap.js`) that this is a genuine pixel-level overlap, not a test artifact — a real click at that screen position hits the toolbar. Scoped as **not fixed here**: it's a pre-existing selection-toolbar design question (how a hover affordance and inline-editable content should share the same corner), not something this slice's mechanical SectionHeading rollout should redesign — and a fully working fallback already exists (the inspector sidebar form field for `eyebrow`, left untouched by this change on every block). Confirmed narrow: only the `eyebrow` line is affected (it sits flush at the corner); `heading`/`description` render below the toolbar's vertical span and are unaffected, verified directly.

**Verified for real**: real `npm run check` clean on frontend (lint/format/typecheck/73 tests), real `npm run build` clean, and a full real Chromium pass against the live builder (`/business/:id/edit`, cookie-authenticated) — 12 assertions across `hero`/`about`/`cta`/`services`/`team`/`cards`/`contactform`/`stats`, covering all three of the mechanical lever's paths (a direct `SectionHeading` call, a shared repeatable-grid engine, `FormBlock`) plus the two hand-converted bespoke blocks, zero console errors. Confirmed dual-editing (canvas click AND the inspector form field both write the same prop, matching the established `heading` convention rather than `text`/`richtext`'s canvas-only convention, since eyebrow/heading/description here are plain strings with no HTML-vs-form-field divergence risk). Confirmed `Escape` cancels an in-progress edit without committing. Two false leads chased to ground during verification, both Playwright strict-mode violations from an unqualified `getByText` matching both the canvas and the sidebar textarea simultaneously (same class of issue as prior batches' false leads) — fixed by scoping assertions to `[role="textbox"]` (the canvas element) or `.first()` where the plain-text sidebar echo was expected and harmless.

## 62. Inline canvas editing extended to nested list-item text — card titles, team member names, testimonial quotes (2026-09-05)

§61 deliberately left nested list-item text out of scope, since editing one field of one array element needs patching that one element, not writing a flat string key — closes that gap for the three shared repeatable-grid engines (`RepeatableIconCards`/`RepeatablePeopleCards`/`RepeatableSimpleCards`), which together back 12 registered block types.

**The mechanism**: a new shared helper, `patchListItem(items, index, field, value)` (`blocks/shared/patch-list-item.ts`), returns a new array with one element's one field replaced — pure, no mutation, since `updateBlockProps` relies on a new array reference to notice the change. Each shared component gained an `onEditItem?: (index, field, value) => void` prop alongside its existing `onEditEyebrow`/`onEditHeading`/`onEditDescription`, and each consumer renderer wires it as `onEditItem={(index, field, value) => onEditProp?.('items', patchListItem(props.items, index, field, value))}` — the shared component itself never needs to know the owning block's prop shape beyond `items`.

- **`RepeatableIconCards`** (`services`/`featuregrid`) — card `title`/`description` now editable; `icon` stays form-only (not text).
- **`RepeatablePeopleCards`** (`team`/`teamsocial`/`testimonials`/`reviews`/`testimonialwall`) — `name`/`role` always editable; quote `text` editable only for the `quote`/`review` variants (`team`/`teamsocial` don't display a text line at all, so no phantom editable field appears there). `photo`/`rating`/`socialLinks` stay form-only.
- **`RepeatableSimpleCards`** (`cards`/`articles`) — `description` always editable; `title` editable **only when the card has no real link** (`hasLink` false). When a card's `url` resolves to a real link, its title stays a plain `<a href>` (not wrapped in `EditableText`) — the canvas has no click-guard against real anchor navigation (confirmed again, same reasoning as §61's button-label decision), so making that text clickable-to-edit would make the first click navigate away instead of opening the editor. `bloggrid` (also built on this engine) was left with no `onEditItem` at all — its items are synthesized from real blog posts, not stored in the block's own props, so there is nothing to patch.

Deliberately still out of scope: `icon`/`photo`/`rating`/`socialLinks`/`url`/`meta`/`image` fields on any item — none are plain inline text, each needs its own control (icon picker, image upload, star rating, link editor) that the inspector form already provides.

**Verified for real**: real `npm run check` clean on frontend (lint/format/typecheck/73 tests), real `npm run build` clean, and a full real Chromium pass against the live builder — 8 assertions covering all three engines (`services`, `team`, `testimonials`, `cards`), including the specific link-guard check: a card with a real `url` renders its title as a genuine `<a>` with **zero** `[role="textbox"]` matches (confirmed not editable), while its sibling `description` — not wrapped in a link — remains editable; a second, untouched list item confirmed `patchListItem` only ever changes the one element addressed, zero console errors.

## 63. New ready-made layout pattern: "Промо (текст + фото)" split-hero (2026-09-05)

§46's "Готовые макеты" (ready-made layout patterns) shipped exactly 3: sidebar+content, two columns, three columns. §61's own "natural next candidates" note named a promo/split-hero pattern as discussed-but-not-built — this ships it, the 4th pattern.

**What it is**: a real `section > columns > column` tree (same universal layout system as the other 3, §37.3 — no new data-model fields), one column with a heading (`h2`)/paragraph/button stack, the other with a single centered image placeholder — `align: 'center'` on the `columns` style so the text and photo line up vertically regardless of which side is taller. Genuinely composable, unlike the pre-existing single-widget `hero`/`herovideo`/`herosplitform` block types: every piece (heading, text, button, image) is its own real block afterward, freely rearrangeable/deletable/duplicable, not one opaque widget with a fixed internal shape.

**Purely additive, zero backend changes**: the pattern expands into `section`/`columns`/`column`/`heading`/`text`/`button`/`image` — block types already in both the frontend registry and the backend's curated `add-block-schemas.ts` allowlist. `LayoutPatternThumbnail` already renders any pattern generically via the real `BlockRenderer` (same dispatcher as the public site), so a new entry in `LAYOUT_PATTERNS` (`model/layout-patterns.ts`) needed no thumbnail-specific code. New `button`/`image` builder helpers added alongside the existing `column`/`heading`/`paragraph` ones; `heading`'s helper gained an optional size level (`h2` vs the other patterns' `h3`) since a promo headline reads larger than a two-column section title. New `PreviewSplitHeroIcon` (`shared/ui/icons/icons.tsx`) follows the existing preview-icon convention exactly (outer frame + semi-transparent content rects) — three stacked bars (heading/text/button) on one side, one solid block (photo) on the other.

**Verified for real**: real `npm run check` clean on frontend (lint/format/typecheck/73 tests), real `npm run build` clean, and a full real Chromium pass against the live builder — confirmed the pattern card is genuinely listed in the "Готовые макеты" tab, and clicking it inserts a real, editable section showing the actual heading/paragraph/button text and the real (unconfigured) image placeholder, zero console errors. Incidentally rediscovered and worked around a pre-existing onboarding feature (`StarterTemplatePicker`, a "С чего начнём?" business-type template picker shown whenever a page has zero blocks) that isn't documented anywhere in this roadmap — noted here so a future session isn't surprised by it the way this verification pass briefly was.

### 63.1 Move-into-own-descendant: reachability confirmed live, then the misleading indicator fixed (closes §42.3's open question)

§42.3 fixed a real bug (moving a block into its own descendant used to silently delete it) but left one question open: was this ever actually reachable by a real human through drag-and-drop, or only by a scripted `move_block` call? Root-caused with a live Playwright drag test rather than assumed: built a real `section > columns > column` tree with the section containing its own nested child, then performed a genuine multi-step pointer drag of the section's own toolbar grip over that nested child's position. The drop-position indicator (`CanvasBlock.module.scss`'s drop line, driven by `BuilderDndProvider.tsx`'s `onDragOver`) **did light up**, visually inviting the drop — confirmed the reason: the dragged block only gets `opacity: 0.5` while remaining fully mounted and interactive (not hidden), so its own descendants stay registered as valid `closestCenter` drop targets throughout the drag. `moveBlock`'s existing data-layer guard (`isBlockOrDescendant`) already silently no-op'd the actual drop, so no corruption ever occurred — but a real user releasing the mouse there would see nothing happen, with no indication why, after the UI had just told them it would work.

Fixed by exporting `isBlockOrDescendant` (`entities/website/model/block-tree.ts`) and checking it in `onDragOver` before computing the indicator: dragging a canvas block over any target that is itself or one of its own descendants now suppresses the indicator entirely, matching what `moveBlock` would actually do. Verified both directions live: the false-positive indicator is gone for the invalid case (re-ran the exact same drag, confirmed `false`), and a second live drag test confirmed the indicator still correctly lights up for a legitimate, unrelated reorder target — no regression to normal drag-and-drop feedback. `npm run check` clean on frontend (73 tests), zero console errors in either Playwright run.

## 64. Custom Widget Engine gains genuine parametrization — the AI can now define a reusable widget "kind", not just a frozen snapshot (2026-09-05)

The owner's own priority for this slice, stated explicitly: "I want item 7 to work flawlessly" and "make it maximally safe for our server, database, and codebase — nobody should be able to get it." Both drove every design decision below, not just the functional goal.

### 64.1 The gap being closed

§50.5/§16.2/§1605 all named the same open question: `create_custom_widget`/`insert_custom_widget` let the AI save and re-place a **frozen** composition of blocks — instantiating "the same widget again" always produces byte-identical content, because there was no way to parametrize it. The owner's own phrase, "AI trains the constructor," was logged as raised but entirely undesigned. This closes it — **without** opening the door mission §2.4 explicitly closed ("declarative composition, not AI-authored arbitrary code").

### 64.2 The design: parametrized templates, not executable code

`CustomWidget` gains an optional `fields: WidgetFieldSchema[]` column (`fields JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'`, migration `20260905190000_add_custom_widget_fields`) — each entry `{ key, label, kind, ... }`, `kind` a **closed** six-value enum: `string | number | boolean | enum | mediaAsset | linkTarget` (deliberately excludes `list`/`dataSource` — a widget parameter is one value, not a nested structure; parametrizing individual list items is named, explicit non-scope, not a silent gap). When a widget has fields, its `schema`'s string prop values may contain `{{fieldKey}}` placeholders — whole-value (`props.src = "{{photo}}"`, any field kind) or embedded inside a longer string (`"Цена: {{price}} грн"`, primitive kinds only).

**Nothing new is ever executed.** A widget "kind" is authored once (`create_custom_widget`, extended with the optional `fields` param) and instantiated any number of times (`insert_custom_widget`, extended with a `values` param) — each instantiation:
1. Validates the caller's `values` against the widget's own `fields` via `buildValidatedWidgetValues` (`custom-widgets/widget-fields.ts`) — which calls **`validateOneField`**, exported from `add-block-schemas.ts` for exactly this reuse, the *same already-audited function* that validates every ordinary block prop today. No new validation logic was written for value-checking; the widget's `fields` are just a narrower, block-agnostic reuse of `CuratedField`.
2. Substitutes `{{fieldKey}}` tokens with those validated values (`resolveWidgetTemplate`, `custom-widgets/resolve-widget-template.ts`) — pure, recursive `string.replace`/object-walk, no interpretation of the substituted text as code, markup, or a template language beyond literal token replacement.
3. **Re-validates every resulting block against the owning block type's own full curated schema** (`buildValidatedProps`, with real `refs` for this business) before insertion. This is the load-bearing safety invariant, stated plainly: *a parametrized widget instance can never produce a prop value that a hand-placed `add_block` call or manual edit of the same block type couldn't already produce* — because it is validated by the exact same gate. A malformed substitution (e.g. a value that violates the target prop's `maxLength`) is rejected with a clear error, not silently truncated or stored invalid — confirmed live (§64.4).
4. Only then does the already-existing, unchanged insert pipeline run (`remapBlockIds` → `insertBlockIntoPage` → `saveDraft`) — identical to the pre-parametrization code path.

Widgets with an empty `fields` array (the default — every widget created before this change) behave **byte-for-byte identically** to before: `parseWidgetSchema`'s new second parameter (`declaredFieldKeys`, a `Set`, defaulting to empty) only changes behavior for a whole-value placeholder whose key is in that set — with an empty set, nothing changes.

### 64.3 Why this is safe — addressed point by point

- **No code execution surface at all**: grepped the entire new surface (`custom-widgets/*.ts`, both modified AI tools) for `eval`, `new Function`, `child_process`, `exec`, `require(` — zero matches beyond `RegExp.exec()` (a plain string match, not code execution). The AI's only two inputs are (a) a closed-enum field *description* and (b) per-instance field *values* — both pass through the same typed validator as any other block prop; there is no path from AI input to `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, a `<script>` tag, or anything the existing `richtext` sanitizer (`sanitizeRichBlockText`) doesn't already gate.
- **No new trust boundary**: `CustomWidgetsService`'s ownership checks (`assertOwnership` — `Business.ownerId === callerId`, on every create/update/get/insert) are completely unchanged; the AI tool's `ctx.businessId` still comes exclusively from the authenticated request context, never from a model-supplied parameter (§0.1's tenant-isolation invariant, untouched).
- **No new DB/query risk**: one additive `JSONB` column via an ordinary Prisma migration; every read/write still goes through Prisma's parameterized query builder, nothing hand-assembles SQL.
- **Indistinguishable final output**: by the time a resolved widget instance's blocks reach `WebsitePage.blocks`, they are ordinary `WebsiteBlock` records — the public site renderer, the anonymous `GET /sites/:businessId` endpoint, and everything else downstream needed **zero changes** and gained **zero new trust boundary**, because the content was fully validated *before* it ever reached storage.

### 64.4 Verified for real, not just reasoned through

Backend `npm run check` clean (lint/format/typecheck/**431 tests**, 37 new across `widget-fields.test.ts`/`resolve-widget-template.test.ts`/extended `custom-widgets.types.test.ts`), backend dev container restarted cleanly with the new `MediaAssetsService` dependency wired into `InsertCustomWidgetTool`. Then, matching this project's own established precedent for AI-tool verification (§51) — **not a REST-only check** — booted a real Nest DI application context (`NestFactory.createApplicationContext(AppModule)`) and called `create_custom_widget`/`insert_custom_widget` through `ToolRegistryService`, the *exact* code path a live Gemini function call goes through (parseInput → handler), end to end:

- Created one parametrized widget kind (`heading` + `button`, 3 fields: `headline`/`buttonLabel`/`buttonUrl`).
- Instantiated it **twice** with different real values in the same call sequence — confirmed via a direct draft fetch that both instances hold distinct, correctly-substituted text and links (`"Первый экземпляр — скидка 20%"` / `"Купить сейчас"` → `https://example.com/sale`, vs. `"Второй экземпляр — новинка сезона"` / `"Смотреть"` → an anchor link) — this is the actual capability being proven: one definition, many genuinely different real instances, not copy-paste.
- Published and re-confirmed both instances via the **genuinely anonymous** `GET /sites/:businessId` endpoint — real end users see the correct, distinct content.
- Deliberately tried to break it: an out-of-bounds value (`headline` 200 chars against a 60-char field) was **rejected** with a clear error, not silently truncated or stored — confirms the re-validation safety net actually fires, not just exists in theory. A call with `values` entirely missing was also correctly rejected ("не хватает значения параметра").

**Honest scope boundaries, named rather than silently dropped**: parametrizing individual items *within* a `list`-kind prop (e.g., one card's title inside a multi-card widget) is not supported — a widget parameter is one value, substituted into one or more top-level props, not a per-item slot; this is a closed design decision (see `WidgetFieldKind`'s own comment), not an oversight. The frontend owner-dashboard UI (`WidgetFormModal.tsx`) was **not** touched in this slice — this closes the AI-facing capability the owner asked about; giving a human owner the same field-definition/instantiate-with-values UI is a real, separate, front-end-only follow-up (the AI tools are already fully usable today independent of it, and the existing dashboard UI was already narrower than the backend allowlist before this change, per its own `WIDGET_BLOCK_SCHEMAS` comment — not a regression introduced here).

## 65. Backend dev-container race ("Cannot find module dist/main.js") — mitigated

§41.2 named a reproducible race: the backend dev container runs two concurrent watchers on the same `dist/` output (`tsc -w` recompiling, `nodemon` — `legacyWatch: true` polling, needed because Docker Desktop on Windows doesn't always forward inotify events into the container — restarting on change), and under rapid consecutive edits `nodemon` could fire `node dist/main.js` in the brief window while `tsc`/`tsc-alias` were still mid-rewrite of that same file, crashing with "Cannot find module dist/main.js" and not self-recovering.

Root cause is a genuine race between two independent file watchers on one output directory, not something a single-process fix can eliminate outright — and `legacyWatch`/polling can't be turned off without risking silently breaking change-detection for whichever developer's Docker Desktop setup needed it in the first place, so that mechanism was left untouched. Mitigated at the actual failure point instead: `nodemon.json`'s `exec` now retries the `dist/main.js` existence check (up to 10×200ms) before invoking `node`, so a transiently-missing file during a rewrite gets a real chance to reappear instead of crashing the process outright. Verified: a full container restart with the new config still boots clean in the normal case (no added latency worth noticing), `npm run check` clean (431 tests). Not root-caused down to the exact byte-level timing (that would need instrumenting `tsc`'s own emit, disproportionate to this item's "not urgent" priority) — this is a mitigation of the observed symptom, documented as such, not a claim that the underlying two-watchers-one-directory race is structurally impossible now.

## 66. Universal layout system: bento/asymmetric grids + responsive `display` (closes §37.3's two named non-scope items)

Both items were explicitly named non-scope back in §37.3: "no per-child grid-column/row span control yet — `gridColumns` only produces equal-width tracks" and "only `direction` is per-viewport; can't switch a block from `grid` to `block` per breakpoint today." Closed together since both live in the same universal layout system (`BlockStyle`, `model/block-style.ts`, `LayoutSection.tsx`).

- **Responsive `display`**: `BlockStyle.display` widened from a flat `LayoutDisplay` to `StyleValue<LayoutDisplay>` — the same `ResponsiveValue<T>` wrapper every other layout field (`direction`/`gap`/`gridColumns`) already uses. The rendering path (`layoutStyle` in `block-style.ts`) already called `readResponsiveProp` on `display` before this change — it turned out only the *type* and the *inspector* were flat; **the inspector's own local logic was a real, latent bug**, not just a missing type: `LayoutSection.tsx` read `style?.display ?? 'block'` directly (bypassing `readResponsiveProp` entirely) and wrote `onChange({ display: next })` directly (bypassing `writeResponsiveProp`) — had a user ever gotten a responsive `display` value into storage by some other path, this code would have received the whole `{desktop, mobile}` object as `displayValue` and every `displayValue === 'grid'`/`'flex'` check below it would have silently failed. Fixed to match `direction`'s existing pattern exactly. Now a card grid can be "Сеткой" on desktop and plain "Стопкой" on mobile without duplicating the section.
- **Bento/asymmetric grids**: two new child-positioning `BlockStyle` fields, `gridColumnSpan`/`gridRowSpan` (`StyleValue<number>`, both responsive) — the same family as `grow`/`fixedWidth` (a child's own positioning within its parent's row/grid), computed in `childLayoutStyle` (now also `viewport`-aware) as `grid-column: span N`/`grid-row: span N`, a safe no-op on any parent that isn't itself `display: 'grid'`. Exposed in the inspector's "Дополнительно" position section, next to `grow`, with an explicit hint that they only do anything under a grid parent.

**Verified for real**: real `npm run check` clean on frontend (lint/format/typecheck/73 tests), real `npm run build` clean. Live Chromium pass against the real public site (not just the builder) at two real viewport widths: desktop confirmed the parent container is a genuine CSS grid and the big tile's *computed* style (not just the raw inline attribute — the browser canonicalizes `grid-column`+`grid-row` into the `grid-area` shorthand when serializing, a real detail chased down rather than accepted as a false failure) resolves to `span 2` on both axes; mobile confirmed `display` genuinely switched off `grid` at the real breakpoint. One false lead during verification (an inline-style-attribute string selector that didn't account for the browser's shorthand serialization) chased to ground and fixed in the test script, not logged as a product bug — same discipline as every prior batch.

## 67. Button/link label inline editing — the nav-guard §61 was waiting on, now built

§61 deliberately skipped this: `SiteButton` (`button`/`buttongroup`/`cta`) and the standalone `link` block render their label inside a real `<a href>`, and the canvas had no guard against real anchor navigation — making the label clickable-to-edit would have made the first click navigate away from the builder. Closed by adding exactly that guard.

**The guard**: `CanvasBlock.tsx`'s content wrapper (`.block__content`) gained an `onClickCapture` that calls `event.preventDefault()` (deliberately **not** `stopPropagation()`) whenever the click target is inside an `<a>`. Capture-phase `preventDefault` stops the browser's real default action (navigation, including `target="_blank"`) without stopping the click event itself from continuing to bubble — so `EditableText`'s own bubble-phase `onClick` (which only needs the click to happen, not a real navigation) still fires normally. This is canvas-only (`CanvasBlock.tsx`, never `BlockRenderer.tsx`) — the real public site is completely untouched, its buttons/links navigate exactly as before.

**Label editing, wired through every path that renders a button/link**: `SiteButton` (shared by `button`, `buttongroup`'s list items via the existing `patchListItem` helper from §62, and `cta`'s `buttonLabel`), the standalone `link` block, and — since neither actually needs the new guard, rendering as a real `<button>` not an `<a>` — `AddToCartButton`/`BookAppointmentButton` (the `addToCart`/`bookAppointment` `url.type` variants) too, for completeness and consistency.

**A real, wider-than-expected instance of §61's already-documented toolbar-occlusion finding**: verified live that `button`/`link` blocks (no `defaultStyle` padding, unlike `cta`) can have their ENTIRE visible label sitting under the block-selection toolbar's action-button zone, not just the `eyebrow` corner case §61 found — confirmed by chasing what looked initially like a broken feature down to real pixel geometry (toolbar `x:[82,268]`, label `x:[170,327]` in the reproduction), not accepting it as a silent failure. Applied one real, safe, zero-visual-risk mitigation while investigating: `.block__toolbar` gained `pointer-events: none` with `pointer-events: auto` restored explicitly on its actual interactive children (`.block__grip`, `.block__actions`) — clicks on the toolbar's non-button chrome (background, the block-type label span) now pass through to content beneath, verified via a live regression check that the toolbar's own buttons (grip drag, duplicate) still work correctly. This measurably helps (any content not directly under a button/grip icon is now reachable) but does not fully close the gap for a short label whose click-center coincides exactly with an action icon — confirmed the underlying edit mechanism itself is entirely correct by clicking a safe (non-overlapping) part of the same label live. Same resolution as §61: the inspector form's own `label` field remains a fully working, unaffected fallback; a full redesign of how the selection toolbar and inline-editable content share one corner remains real, deliberately out-of-scope design work, now affecting a demonstrably wider set of blocks than previously known.

**Verified for real**: real `npm run check` clean on frontend (lint/format/typecheck/73 tests), real `npm run build` clean, full real Chromium pass against the live builder confirming (a) clicking a button/link/CTA-button label never navigates the builder away (URL unchanged in all three cases) and (b) the edit mechanism itself commits correctly when clicked at a reachable position, plus a dedicated regression check that the toolbar's own action buttons still function after the `pointer-events` change.

## 68. Advertising Infrastructure — bounded first slice (Ad Slots, self-service campaigns, Ad Engine)

The owner's ask was explicit and large (a full inventory/targeting/analytics advertising system), with one binding constraint stated up front: build it **strictly on top of the existing tariff/payment/category/analytics architecture, never a second parallel one**. Three research passes confirmed there was genuinely nothing to reuse-by-collision (no existing advertising code at all — only a static marketing-copy comparison table) but confirmed five real systems to build directly on top of: `PlanTier`/`BusinessSubscription` (the tariff), the generic `PaymentProvider` abstraction (already backing `Order`/`Appointment` one-time payments through one Stripe webhook), `BusinessCategory` (previously cosmetic-only), the free-form `AnalyticsEvent` model, and the Builder's `registerBlock`/curated-allowlist pattern. Three decisions were locked in with the owner before writing code: slot counts per tier are an editable draft (`free:0, starter:1, business:3, scale:6, enterprise:10`), campaigns are **self-service for any Business** (not admin-only), and this is a deliberately **bounded first slice**, not the full spec at once — closed with a named "explicitly deferred" list, not silently.

### 68.1 What was built, and which existing system each piece reuses

- **Entitlements** (`backend/src/modules/billing/ad-entitlements.ts`): `AD_SLOT_ENTITLEMENTS: Record<PlanTier, number>`, sibling to the existing `plan-catalog.ts`. `getAdSlotLimit(tier: PlanTier | null)` treats a missing subscription the same as `free` (0 slots) — matches `BillingService.getStatus`'s own existing convention that `tier: null` means "no plan ever selected," not an error.
- **`AdvertisingInventoryService`** (new `backend/src/modules/advertising/`): the **one** new read the whole feature hangs off — `getInventory(businessId)` returns `{tier, limit, occupied, available}`. `occupied` is **computed**, not stored: walks the business's real `WebsitePage.content` JSON counting `adslot`-type blocks (`lib/count-ad-slot-blocks.ts`), the same "derive, don't duplicate" principle the codebase already uses for `ALLOWED_BLOCK_TYPES = Object.keys(BLOCK_SCHEMAS)`. **A deliberate, stated design deviation**: this does *not* fold into `Business.capabilities` — that array is owner-toggleable and explicitly documented as independent of plan tier; mixing a plan-*derived*, non-toggleable value into it would let an owner defeat the gate via the existing `PATCH /business`. Instead it's a parallel, dedicated gate wired into the exact same two call sites `findMissingCapabilities` already uses (`ComponentLibraryPanel.tsx` frontend, `AddBlockTool`/`InsertCustomWidgetTool` backend) — same pattern, independent check.
- **Data model** (one additive migration, `20260905200000_add_advertising`): new `AdCampaign`/`AdCreative` models plus three new enums (`AdCampaignStatus`/`AdFormat`/`AdPlacement`) — reuses the existing `PaymentStatus` enum (already shared by `Order`/`Appointment`, now a third user) and the existing `BusinessCategory` enum for targeting/competitor exclusion, rather than inventing a new taxonomy. No new slot-inventory or impression/click table at all.
- **Self-service campaign payment** (`AdCampaignsService.submitForReview`): calls the **existing** `PaymentProvider.createPaymentIntent` — the exact same call shape `OrdersService.createFromCart` already uses — making this a *third* caller of a payment abstraction that already backs two other flows, with **zero new Stripe integration code**. `StripeWebhookController` (the single webhook endpoint in the whole app) got a 3-line third dispatch branch (`orderId` xor `appointmentId` xor `campaignId`). Unlike an `Order` (an unpaid order is still a valid, manually-fulfillable state), a campaign with no payment has no honest next state, so `submitForReview` throws rather than degrading gracefully — a deliberate difference from `OrdersService`'s pattern, not an oversight.
- **Ad Engine** (`AdEngineService.selectCreative`): targeting (placement + optional category allowlist) → **competitor exclusion**, stated as an explicit default-deny rule (any campaign whose advertiser's own `BusinessCategory` equals the publisher's is excluded, no opt-in in this slice) → format compatibility (`AD_PLACEMENT_ALLOWED_FORMATS`, an independent backend copy of the same contract the frontend's `AD_PLACEMENT_CONFIG` encodes, same "two independent copies" convention as `add-block-schemas.ts` vs. the frontend registry) → round-robin among eligible creatives (a small Redis counter — explicitly *not* real auction/bidding logic, named as a v2 concern). The eligible-candidate pool itself is Redis-cached per `(publisher category, placement)` for 60s — the expensive Postgres query runs at most once a minute per targeting signature platform-wide, not once per page view, addressing the owner's explicit performance requirement.
- **Delivery**: new anonymous endpoints on the existing `PublicSitesController` (not a new controller — mirrors `getPublicProducts`'s exact anonymous pattern): `GET :businessId/ads/select` and fire-and-forget `POST :businessId/ads/impression|click`, the latter two recording through the **existing** `AnalyticsService.record()` with new `ad_impression`/`ad_click` event types — no new analytics model, campaign/creative ids ride in the existing free-form `metadata` JSON column.
- **Moderation**: minimal and campaign-level only (`pending_review → active|rejected`), on a new `AdminAdvertisingController` at `admin/advertising` — same guard pair and same "one composite `overview` endpoint" convention as the existing `admin/ai-infrastructure` controller, not a new generic moderation subsystem (none existed before, and a full rule-engine was explicitly out of scope for this slice).
- **Builder integration** (`frontend/src/entities/website/blocks/advertising/index.tsx`, new `BlockCategory: 'advertising'`): the `adslot` block's *only* owner-editable field is `placement` — no creative fields at all, which is what "Platform managed" concretely means here. Canvas rendering never fetches or shows a real creative (the fetcher itself resolves to `null` without a network call whenever `isEditing` is true, satisfying Rules of Hooks without a conditional hook call). **CLS fix**: `AD_PLACEMENT_CONFIG` reserves a *fixed* width/height per placement (not a min/max range) — loading, real-creative, and empty-result states all render into the exact same box size, so the owner's site layout never shifts regardless of which state resolves. Gated in `ComponentLibraryPanel`/`AddBlockModal` by a new `adSlotsAvailable` prop (parallel to, never merged with, the existing `capabilities` prop) fed by a new `getAdInventory` call in `WebsiteBuilderWidget`'s existing data fetch.

### 68.2 A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not just reasoned through

The public `ads/select` endpoint originally returned a bare `SelectedAdDto | null` from the controller. Live curl verification (not just `tsc`) caught that Nest/Express's Express adapter treats a controller returning `null` the same as `undefined` — it sends a genuinely **empty** response body (`Content-Length: 0`), not the JSON text `"null"`. The frontend's `backendFetch` always calls `response.json()` on a non-204 response, which throws `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of JSON input` on an empty body — meaning the single most common real-world case (no matching campaign) would have thrown on every anonymous visitor's page load. Fixed by wrapping the response in `{ creative: SelectedAdDto | null }` (matching what the plan had actually specified) — verified live afterward that the endpoint returns valid `{"creative":null}` JSON.

### 68.3 Verified for real, end to end, against live data — not just unit tests

Backend `npm run check` clean (lint/format/typecheck/**443 tests**, new suites for `getAdSlotLimit`/`countBlocksOfType`), frontend `npm run check` clean (73 tests) plus a real `npm run build`. Then a full live REST pass against the actual running dev backend + real Postgres/Redis, using real pre-existing test businesses already in the dev database (not just freshly-created ones):

- Confirmed inventory math against a genuinely un-subscribed business (`tier: null → limit 0, available 0`) and against real existing `scale`-tier businesses in the dev DB (`limit: 6`).
