**Frontend — format drives both the crop aspect and the live preview, from one source.** New `widgets/business-dashboard/ui/ad-format-preview-config.ts` (`AD_FORMAT_PREVIEW_CONFIG`, IAB-ish canonical width/height per `AdFormat`) feeds both the new `AdCreativePreview` (a live-updating card above the form, rendering the same image/video/text-fallback branch as the real public-site `AdSlotRenderer`, reimplemented locally since `widgets/business-dashboard` and `entities/website` are FSD siblings and can't cross-import) and the new `AdCreativeMediaField` (replaces `AdCreativeImageField`) — `format === 'video'` shows a plain video file input (`uploadAdCreativeVideo`, new API fn mirroring `uploadAdCreativeImage`) with a muted/looped `<video>` preview; any other format shows `ImageUploadButton` with `aspect` computed from the same config, so picking a format changes the crop shape and the preview shape together. `ImageUploadButton` itself gained an `accept` prop (default unchanged — zero behavior change for the existing avatar/cover/group callers) and a per-file GIF bypass: a selected `image/gif` always skips the crop modal, since `cropImageToBlob`'s canvas re-encode would otherwise silently kill the animation. `AdCreativeFormModal` re-enables `video` in `SELECTABLE_FORMATS`, clears the other media field on format switch (so a stale mismatched url can never reach submit), and gained client-side "video format needs a video" validation mirroring the backend's own rule.

**One real bug found and fixed during live verification, not caught by `npm run check`**: `AdCreativePreview`'s box used `aspect-ratio` CSS to turn a `width`/`height` pair into a sized box — `getComputedStyle` showed the width resolving correctly (406px) but the height collapsing to exactly the border width (2px), aspect-ratio silently ignored. Root cause: the box sits inside `ScrollArea`'s flex-column viewport with every one of its own children `position: absolute` (no in-flow content), a combination `aspect-ratio` doesn't reliably survive in Chromium. Fixed with the classic percentage-`padding-bottom` technique instead (computed from the same `width`/`height` pair, inline) — percentage padding is always resolved against the parent's width regardless of how the surrounding flex layout handles height, confirmed via `getComputedStyle` reporting the correct pixel height afterward.

**Live-verified end to end in real Chromium** against a real test business/draft campaign: typing into the form updated the preview instantly; switching formats visibly resized the preview box and changed the cropper's aspect the next time it opened; uploading a real PNG opened the crop modal, cropping and confirming produced a real re-encoded JPEG on disk, visible in the preview; switching to `video` format replaced the image field with a video field and cleared the stale image; uploading a real ~590 KB `.webm` (a genuine system sample file, not synthesized) succeeded, played muted/looped in both the media-field preview and the live preview, and landed on disk with a fresh UUID name plus the correct `AdCreative.videoUrl`/`format: 'video'` row in Postgres; submitting a video-format creative with nothing attached was correctly blocked client-side; a real `.gif` uploaded on an image format skipped the crop modal entirely and was stored byte-identical (confirmed via file size and `MediaAsset.mimeType: 'image/gif'`), preserving animation. `npm run check` clean on both stacks (466 backend / 80 frontend, unchanged counts — no new automated tests, this slice is UI/upload-pipeline wiring verified live, same convention as prior UI-heavy slices), real `npm run build` clean. Cleanup: the test business (cascading its campaign/creative) and test account deleted, the real uploaded test files (image/video/gif) removed from `uploads/ads/`, `playwright` reinstalled/uninstalled transiently with a confirmed-clean lockfile diff, dev server on port 3001 killed.

## 78. Widget styling: a widget-style bug fixed, a new `set_theme` AI tool, and a bounded "advanced CSS" escape hatch for outlier designs

Thinking ahead from §76, the owner asked how AI-created (and human-created) widgets actually get *styled* to match a design, and — separately, an explicitly named hard case — what happens when a design is genuinely "capital-C crazy," geometry the closed, token-based style system can't express at all. Investigation found the pieces further along than expected, with one concrete bug and two real gaps: `set_style` (AI tool) already covers the *entire* `BlockStyle` surface on any block by id, reusing one validator (`buildValidatedStyle`, `backend/src/modules/ai/tools/lib/block-style-schema.ts`) — half of "let AI style via API" was already true. But `CustomWidget.schema`'s parser, `parseWidgetSchema` (`custom-widgets/custom-widgets.types.ts`), only ever read `blockType`/`props` off an incoming block — **`style` was silently dropped**, even though the real `WebsiteBlock` shape already has a `style?` field: a widget the AI carefully styled before saving lost every bit of that styling the moment it became a widget. Separately, **no site-wide `set_theme` AI tool existed at all** — palette/fonts/radius were human-only, edited through `ThemePanel.tsx`, even though matching a design usually starts with the whole site's look, not one block. Asked directly whether to stay purely token-based for the "crazy design" case or add a bounded escape hatch, the owner chose the escape hatch, **with security as the explicit stated priority** — the project has a repeated, explicit stance against arbitrary-CSS/arbitrary-code surfaces (the only prior "arbitrary visual" escape hatch anywhere, the `embed` block, accepts only an iframe *URL*, never markup/CSS, specifically to avoid stored-XSS on a multi-tenant platform), so this had to be designed carefully, not bolted on.

**1. Widgets stop losing their style.** `parseWidgetSchema` gained an optional `style` per schema item, validated with the exact same `buildValidatedStyle` `set_style` already uses — reuse, not a copy. `resolveWidgetTemplate` (`custom-widgets/resolve-widget-template.ts`) now passes `style` through unchanged when resolving a template instance (style isn't "content" the way text/links are — nothing to template-substitute). `InsertCustomWidgetTool`'s parametrized-widget branch re-validates `style` via `buildValidatedStyle` alongside `props`, mirroring the exact "re-validate before it ever touches a real page" discipline already applied there; the non-parametrized branch already passed `widget.schema` through untouched, so style already survived there for free. `create_custom_widget`'s description/JSON-schema gained an optional per-block `style`, described with the same `STYLE_FIELD_KEYS` vocabulary `set_style` uses — one mental model for the AI, not two. `assessWidgetQuality`'s (`custom-widgets/widget-quality.lib.ts`) "all-default" rejection was widened: a widget whose props are all default but whose blocks carry real, non-empty `style` (a nicely styled empty card) now passes — style is presentational configuration, not the business-identifying "content" §76 was closing, the same reasoning that already exempts `number`/`boolean`/`enum` prop kinds from that rule.

**2. `set_theme` — a fourth AI tool, for the site-wide look.** Confirmed the human theme-edit path (`ThemePanel.tsx` → `WebsitesService.saveDraft`, via `UpdateWebsiteDocumentDto`) validates `theme` only as "is an object" — deliberately shallow, correct for a trusted browser editing its own site, but unsafe to expose directly to AI input. New `backend/src/modules/ai/tools/lib/theme-schema.ts`, `buildValidatedTheme` — same merge-partial/`null`-clears-optional-field shape as `buildValidatedStyle`, covering the real `WebsiteTheme` surface (7 hex colors, `fonts.heading`/`body`/`googleFontHeading`/`googleFontBody`, `radius`, `buttonStyle`, `containerWidth`, `sectionSpacing`, `cardBorder`, `cardShadow`) with backend's own independent copy of the enum value lists (same "two copies of one contract" precedent as `ad-placement-config.ts`). New `backend/src/modules/ai/tools/set-theme.tool.ts`, a structural mirror of `set-style.tool.ts`.

**3. A bounded, security-first "advanced CSS" bucket.** New `BlockStyle.advanced?: Partial<Record<AdvancedCssProperty, string>>` — exactly 12 allowlisted properties (`transform`, `clipPath`, `filter`, `backdropFilter`, `mixBlendMode`, `opacity`, `borderRadius`, `boxShadow`, `letterSpacing`, `textTransform`, `fontStyle`, `fontWeight`), each a raw string value, not a general escape hatch. **Two independent safety layers**: (a) backend validation (`block-style-schema.ts`) length-caps each value (200 chars) and rejects `url(`/`expression(`/`javascript:`/`@import`/`;<>{}\` outright — none of these 12 properties' legitimate values ever need those tokens; the 3 keyword properties are exact-match enums, the rest pattern-restricted to a conservative character set; (b) rendering applies the value via React's `style` object (`computeBlockWrapperStyle` in `frontend/src/entities/website/model/block-style.ts`, merged onto `outer` last so it wins over presets), never a stylesheet or `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` — structurally incapable of injecting a new selector regardless of what slipped past (a); worst case the browser's own CSSOM setter silently no-ops an unparseable value on that one property. `position`/`top`/`right`/`bottom`/`left`/`zIndex` (stacking/overlay risk), `background`/`backgroundImage` (exactly the vector for an external tracking-pixel/SSRF-flavored URL — already owned by the closed-token `background` field), and `content` are permanently excluded regardless of value. `set_style`'s description gained the new bucket with a "use sparingly" steer. The human inspector (`widgets/website-builder/inspector/LayoutSection.tsx`, already has a "Просто"/"Дополнительно" tabbed style panel) gained a new "Продвинутый CSS" subsection with all 12 fields, plus a **client-side mirror of the same validator** for immediate feedback — found and fixed a real UX bug while building this: a naively-controlled text input bound directly to committed store state would visually "snap back" on every invalid keystroke (can't even type `u`-`r`-`l`-`(` without the field seeming to reject each character); fixed with a local draft-value/error state that only commits to the block's real style once a value validates, so users can see exactly what they typed and why it's rejected.

**Named, not attempted in this pass**: "guarantee pixel-perfect 1:1 with a reference image" — the roadmap distinguishes two different things here: (a) attach one reference image, let Gemini build a matching site in one shot — plumbing fully wired since §38, **never actually observed working**, blocked only on the project's Gemini API credit being depleted; (b) a real closed-loop pixel-accuracy pipeline (render → screenshot → compare → refine) — named in the roadmap as 0% built, no pieces exist. Per the owner's own priority call this turn, this slice stayed scoped to the styling gaps above; (a) should be tested for real once credit is topped up before deciding whether (b)'s much heavier investment is warranted at all.

**Live-verified end to end**, no Gemini spend (same direct-service-instantiation technique as §74-§77): a standalone script called `CustomWidgetsService.create` with a styled block (gradient background + `advanced.transform: 'rotate(-2deg)'`) — confirmed the style round-tripped exactly, including the advanced bucket; ran the same block through `resolveWidgetTemplate` + `buildValidatedStyle` (what `InsertCustomWidgetTool`'s parametrized branch does) — confirmed style and templated text both survived; called `buildValidatedTheme` + `WebsitesService.saveDraft`/`getDraft` directly on a real business's draft — confirmed the theme's primary color and Google font genuinely changed in Postgres, then restored the original theme afterward. Real Chromium: opened a real block's "Продвинутый CSS" section, set a `transform: rotate(-6deg)` — confirmed the block visibly rotated on the canvas (screenshotted); then typed a value containing `url(...)` — confirmed a real inline error appeared and the block's rotation stayed exactly as it was (the invalid value never reached the canvas). `npm run check` clean on both stacks (494 backend tests, +28 new for style-on-widgets/the advanced bucket/`set_theme`; 80 frontend, unchanged — the new inspector UI verified live, same convention as prior UI-heavy slices), real `npm run build` clean. Cleanup: test business/account deleted, dev server on port 3001 killed, `playwright` reinstalled/uninstalled transiently with a confirmed-clean lockfile diff.

## 79. Creator Monetization & Native Advertising — Phase 1: eligibility, hierarchical categories, Stripe Identity verification, Creator Studio

The owner's brief was the full ecosystem: users become verified Creators, the platform (not the creator) matches them with advertisers, native "Sponsored" cards blend into the feed, revenue splits three ways with every percentage admin-configurable. Two follow-ups sharpened the very first slice before any code was written: creator *category* must be a genuine hierarchical taxonomy and the backbone of matching, not guessed from content, with a distinct "creator category" (who this person is) vs. "content category" (what one post is about); and creator status is gated on **≥2,000 friends**, modeled as an explicit `NOT_ELIGIBLE → ELIGIBLE → VERIFICATION_PENDING → VERIFIED → ACTIVE → SUSPENDED` machine, deliberately built behind a single narrow service method (not a premature strategy-pattern interface) so the threshold rule can change later without touching anything else. Given the size of the full spec, the work was explicitly split into 5 phases up front and **only Phase 1 was built**: eligibility + categories + identity verification + the status machine + a working Creator Studio shell + an admin section. Phases 2-5 (native ad content-type + feed rendering, AI-powered matching with explanations, revenue-attribution ledger + admin-configurable %, real Stripe Connect payouts) are named, not started.

Investigation found the existing `AdCampaign`/`AdEngineService` advertising stack (§68) deliberately **not reusable** here, for the same reason `PostBoost` (§73) was never built on it: `AdCampaign.advertiserBusinessId` is a hard FK to `Business`, and creators are `User`s, not `Business`es — new models were the correct call, not a shim. Asked directly how to do identity verification given the project has zero KYC infrastructure today, the owner chose **Stripe Identity** over a DIY document-upload flow — decisive, because `app.useStaticAssets(uploads/, {prefix:'/uploads/'})` (`backend/src/main.ts`) serves everything under `uploads/` publicly with zero per-file auth, and there is no genuine private-file-serving precedent anywhere in this codebase. Stripe Identity sidesteps the problem entirely: the ID photo and selfie are uploaded straight to Stripe by the user and never touch this backend's disk — only a session id and a pass/fail verdict are stored, which is also the more defensible reading of the owner's own "don't store excess data" requirement.

**1. Data model.** New `CreatorCategory` (self-referencing hierarchy, `parentId`/`children`, seeded — not admin-CRUD yet, named as a deferred nice-to-have — via a standalone idempotent `prisma/seed-creator-categories.ts`, 20 top-level categories from the owner's own example list, with real 3-level sub-trees only for Gaming and Technology, matching exactly what the owner specified rather than inventing sub-trees for the rest), `CreatorCategoryAssignment` (join table, one required primary + up to 5 additional per profile), and `CreatorProfile` (`status`/`rejectionReason`/`verifiedAt`/`activatedAt`/`suspendedAt`/`suspendedReason`/`stripeIdentitySessionId`, plus real owner-facing controls from day one: `monetizationEnabled`, `adFrequency` low/balanced/high, `maxAdFrequencyRatio` — "no more than 1 ad per N posts" — `blockedCategories`, `blockedAdvertiserIds`). `NotificationType` gained `creator_status_changed`, following the existing `rule_triggered` shape exactly (`actorId: null`, free-text `summary`, excluded from the main feed).

**2. Eligibility as one method, not an abstraction.** `FriendsService.countFriends` — a plain, cheap `prisma.friendship.count()` (no new counter field; `FriendsService.getFriendIds` was already a live, uncached query, so a threshold check is no heavier). `CreatorsService.getEligibility(userId)` returns `{eligible, friendCount, requiredFriends}` against a new `CREATOR_MIN_FRIENDS` env value (default 2000) — this one method **is** the "`CreatorEligibilityRule`" the owner asked to future-proof, deliberately narrow rather than a strategy-pattern hierarchy (same "narrow today, widen when a second rule appears" convention as `ImageUploadSubdir`). `startOnboarding` re-checks eligibility server-side even though the client already gated the button — an untrusted-browser check, not a UX nicety.

**3. Stripe Identity, its own webhook secret.** `CreatorIdentityService` builds its own `Stripe` client, `createVerificationSession` returns a `client_secret` for the frontend's `stripe.verifyIdentity(clientSecret)` call (Stripe's own hosted modal — a different Stripe.js method than the `<Elements>`/`PaymentElement` flow used for regular payments, no wrapper needed), `parseWebhookEvent` handles `verified` (→ `active` + `activatedAt`, notification fired), `requires_input`/`canceled` (→ `rejected` + reason). New `STRIPE_IDENTITY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, following the established one-secret-per-Stripe-product-area-on-the-same-account convention (`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for orders, `STRIPE_SUBSCRIPTIONS_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for billing). No document image ever reaches this backend.

**4. Frontend.** New `entities/creator` slice; a `CreatorPromoCard` on the owner's own profile (three honest states — locked-with-progress, eligible CTA, already-a-creator) mirroring `AdvertiserSignupFlow`'s thin-shell precedent; `widgets/creator-onboarding` (benefits pipeline → hierarchical category picker, primary required/≤5 additional, rendered from the real `GET /creators/categories` tree, not a hardcoded list → Stripe Identity step → pending screen) and `widgets/creator-studio` (Revenue/Advertising honest empty-states naming *why* — "Phase 2 will add this" — not invented numbers; Audience showing only real, available-today numbers — friend count, categories; Settings fully functional and immediately persisted, inert-but-honest until Phase 2 gives it effect). New admin `AdminCreatorsPanel` — narrower than `AdminAdvertisingPanel` because Stripe Identity's verdict is automatic, so there's no manual approve/reject-document queue, only a status filter + suspend/reinstate for policy violations unrelated to identity.

**Named scope cut**: no frontend toast/realtime-socket consumption for the `creator:status-changed` event this pass — retrofitting the shared `ToastItem`/`NotificationToast` component (its `describe()` unconditionally requires actor fields for every toast kind; there's no actor-less "system message" variant anywhere yet) was judged more invasive than this slice warranted. The backend still fires the real-time event and creates the persisted `Notification` row correctly (verified live via the admin suspend/reinstate REST test, which exercises the identical `notifyStatusChange` path); the onboarding/studio pages instead treat `GET /creators/me` as the authoritative, directly-refetchable source of truth.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during live verification, not just reasoned through.** (a) `backendFetch` (`shared/lib/backend-client.ts`) unconditionally called `.json()` on any 200 response — but NestJS serializes a controller method returning `null` (e.g. `GET /creators/me` with no profile yet) as a **literal empty body**, not the JSON string `"null"`, so `response.json()` threw `SyntaxError: Unexpected end of JSON input` on every profile-less page load. Fixed at the shared-helper level (read `.text()`, treat empty as `null`) since this would silently break any future `T | null` endpoint, not just this one. (b) `CreatorStudioWidget`'s settings-save handler called `profile.refetch()` on success — but `refetch()` sets `profile.status` to `'loading'`, and the widget's own top-level loading check would then unmount the entire `CreatorSettingsSection` (losing the "Настройки сохранены" confirmation) before the user could ever see it. Fixed by threading the already-fresh `CreatorProfile` `updateCreatorSettings` returns directly into local state instead of forcing a blocking refetch.

**Live-verified end to end.** Backend: full REST pass against the running dev container — eligibility (below/at/above threshold), category tree, onboarding correctly blocked at 403 while ineligible, a real Stripe test-mode `POST /creators/identity-session` returning a genuine `clientSecret`, admin list/suspend/reinstate with real notification rows created. `npm run check` clean (494 backend tests). Frontend: `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` both clean; real Chromium (separate `next dev -p 3001`, the owner's own port 3000 never touched) against a throwaway account seeded to 2,050 real friendships in dev Postgres — walked the full locked→eligible→benefits→hierarchical category picker→Stripe Identity step (Stripe's own test-mode modal genuinely opened with a real client secret), flipped the profile to `active` via a locally-signed test webhook payload (no live `stripe listen` process needed), confirmed the onboarding page's redirect into Creator Studio, exercised all four Studio tabs, and confirmed Settings changes actually persist to Postgres. Separately promoted (with explicit, scoped permission for this specific verification) a second throwaway account to admin and confirmed the Creators panel's status filters, suspend (with reason, reflected immediately), and reinstate all work against the real backend. All throwaway accounts, the 2,050 seeded friendships, and the temporary local-only webhook secret used to sign the test payload were deleted/reverted afterward.

## 80. Creator Monetization & Native Advertising — Phase 2: native ad campaigns, manual creator↔campaign assignment, feed rendering

Directly continuing §79's own 5-phase roadmap (only Phase 1 was built there): "native ad as a first-class content type... the four adaptive creative formats... and feed-level rendering... manual creator↔campaign assignment only — no AI yet." Research before writing any code found the feed's real shape mattered more than the plan's own wording assumed: `PostsService.listFeed` is cursor-by-id pagination (`cursor: {id}, skip: 1`), and — the actually load-bearing finding — the frontend's `usePostStore.posts: Post[]` is threaded through 15+ methods (likes, dislikes, reposts, comments, wall/group variants, `mapEverywhere`/`filterEverywhere`/`matchesTarget` helpers), all assuming a homogeneous `Post[]`. Turning that into a real `FeedItem = post | ad` discriminated union, as the plan's own wording literally suggested, would have meant touching nearly every one of those methods for a feature that doesn't need any of their behavior (an ad has no likes/comments/reposts). Deliberately built the lighter alternative instead: `PostState.posts: Post[]` stays completely untouched; a new, thin, additive endpoint (`POST /native-ads/feed/select`, body: the post ids already on screen) returns a sparse `postId → ad` map that `FeedWidget` merges in *only at render time*, splicing a `NativeAdCard` after its anchor post via a `Fragment`. Same real feature, far smaller blast radius — a deliberate, reasoned deviation from the plan's literal phrasing once the actual codebase shape was known, not a scope cut.

**1. Data model — independent of `AdCampaign`, for the same reason `PostBoost` was.** New `NativeAdCampaign`/`NativeAdCreative` (`AdBillingModel`/`PaymentStatus`/`AdvertiserBan` reused as-is — cpm/cpc economics and "banned as an advertiser" are platform-wide concepts, not tied to one ad engine; `targetCategoryIds`/`targetGeography`/`adCategory` are new fields with no site-ad equivalent) and new `NativeAdAssignment` (`campaignId` × `creatorProfileId`, unique pair) — the literal implementation of "manual assignment": an admin explicitly links an approved campaign to specific active creators, nothing automatic yet. `CreatorProfile.blockedCategories` (built inert in Phase 1, "nothing to filter against yet") now has real teeth: `NativeAdCampaign.adCategory` is validated against the exact same `CREATOR_BLOCKABLE_AD_CATEGORIES` list, so a creator's existing block list works immediately, no migration needed for it to start mattering.

**2. Backend — `native-ads` module, independent of `advertising`.** `NativeAdCampaignsService` is a close structural mirror of `AdCampaignsService` (create/addCreative/removeCreative/submitForReview/markPaidByPaymentIntent/recordImpression·Click/admin listPendingReview·listActive·listPaused/approve/reject/approveCreative/rejectCreative) — the whole self-service-campaign-lifecycle shape carried over, not reinvented. `NativeAdAssignmentsService` is new: `assign`/`unassign` (admin-only), `listForCreatorUser` (creator's own view — deliberately drops campaign-wide `budgetCents`/`impressionsServed` from the response, since one campaign can be assigned to several creators and showing campaign-wide numbers on one creator's page would be a real, honest-looking lie about their own contribution; true per-creator attribution is Phase 4's actual job). `NativeAdFeedService.getAdsForPosts` is the new piece with no site-ad precedent: resolves each post's author to an active, monetization-enabled `CreatorProfile`, paces at 1 ad per `maxAdFrequencyRatio` posts **counted fresh on every request** (an explicit, named simplification — no cross-request/cross-viewer pacing state is persisted; at a page size of 20 and a default ratio of 5 the practical difference is negligible, and it avoids a stateful pacing store this phase doesn't need), then picks randomly among that creator's active/paid assignments (no Redis round-robin cache like `AdEngineService` — that cache exists because the site-ad candidate pool can be shared by *many simultaneous publishers of one category*; a creator's own manually-assigned campaign list is always small, the infrastructure isn't earned yet). `StripeWebhookController` gained a fifth branch (`nativeCampaignId`, a distinct metadata key from `campaignId` so the two ad systems' payments never collide on the one shared webhook).

**3. Frontend.** New `entities/native-ad` slice (viewer-facing `selectNativeAds`/`recordNativeAdImpression`/`recordNativeAdClick`/`getMyNativeAds`, plus the full owner-facing campaign CRUD mirroring `entities/advertising`) and `NativeAdCard` — four adaptive layouts (minimal/editorial/product/video) via `padding-bottom %` aspect-ratio containers (the exact technique `AdCreativePreview` already uses for site ads), always labeled "Партнёрский материал · {sponsor}" so it never masquerades as organic content. `FeedWidget` gained the sparse-map merge described above. `widgets/business-dashboard` gained a full "Реклама в ленте" tab (`NativeAdvertisingSection` + 2 modals + a media field with per-style crop aspect ratios) — a close mirror of the existing `AdvertisingSection`, reusing its own SCSS module directly since the two systems share identical status-enum values, not a copy of the file. `CreatorAdvertisingSection` (Phase 1's permanent empty-state) now shows real assigned campaigns — sponsor/style/headline/image, deliberately no numbers (see the backend DTO's comment above). New `AdminNativeAdsPanel` — the moderation half mirrors `AdminAdvertisingPanel` almost exactly; the assignment half (`AssignmentManager`: list current assignments, a `<select>` of eligible active creators, assign/unassign) is genuinely new, since site ads have nothing like it.

**Named scope cuts, not silently dropped.** No admin 30-day daily trend chart for native ads (unlike `AdminAdvertisingPanel`) — the moderation/assignment loop mattered more than a chart for this pass. No `targetCategoryIds`/`targetGeography` picker UI in the campaign-creation form — Phase 2's matching is entirely manual (admin-assigned), so these fields are informational-only metadata with zero present effect; building a full creator-category picker for a field that doesn't do anything yet would be exactly the kind of ahead-of-need building this project avoids — it arrives with Phase 3, when AI matching actually reads it.

**Two real bugs found live, not just reasoned through.** (a) BEM violation caught before it shipped: `NativeAdCard`'s per-style layout was first written as nested SCSS (`.ad__body--minimal { .ad__media { ... } }`), which compiles to a forbidden descendant selector under this project's own CSS rules — rewritten as explicit sibling modifier classes (`ad__media--minimal`, `ad__headline--editorial`, `ad__cta--product`) applied directly by the component. (b) A genuine React-19-Strict-Mode bug, caught only by watching a real ad silently fail to render in the browser: `FeedWidget`'s ad-fetch effect marked a post id as "already fetched" (in a `useRef` Set, which survives cleanup) *before* the fetch resolved; dev-mode's mount→cleanup→remount double-invocation meant the first (real) fetch's result was discarded by its own cleanup's `cancelled` flag, while the second invocation saw the id already marked and never re-fetched — the ad was fetched successfully and then permanently thrown away, every time, for every post. Fixed by moving the "mark as fetched" step inside the `.then()`, after the cancellation check, so an aborted first run leaves the id genuinely unfetched for the second run to pick up.

**Test coverage, not just live verification.** The one piece of genuinely novel business logic in this slice — feed pacing (`selectPacedPostIds`, "1 ad per N posts, independently per creator") and the blocked-category/blocked-advertiser veto (`isCandidateBlocked`) — was extracted out of `NativeAdFeedService` into a pure, Prisma-free `lib/native-ad-feed.lib.ts`, the same pattern `advertising/lib/effective-bid.ts` already established for the site-ad ranking logic, with 9 new unit tests (ratio boundaries, per-creator independence, each veto path, the "unclassified campaign is never blocked" case). 503 backend tests total.

**Live-verified end to end**, same discipline as §79. Backend: a full REST pass with a real Stripe test-mode `PaymentIntent`, a locally-signed webhook payload (real `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, no live `stripe listen` process) flipping payment to `paid`, admin approve, admin assign, `POST /native-ads/feed/select` correctly returning the ad for the assigned creator's post, the `blockedCategories` veto correctly suppressing it when the creator blocked the campaign's `adCategory`, and impression/click recording correctly incrementing `spentCents`. `npm run check` clean on both stacks, real `npm run build` clean. Real Chromium (separate `next dev -p 3001`) walked the *actual UI* for every surface: created a campaign and a creative through the real Business Dashboard form, hit the real budget-too-small-for-Stripe error path and confirmed it degrades to an inline message rather than a crash, completed a real Stripe test-mode PaymentIntent creation with the real Payment Element rendering card fields, approved and assigned a creator through the real Admin panel UI (not just its API), confirmed Creator Studio's Advertising tab shows the real assignment, and — after finding and fixing bug (b) above — confirmed the `NativeAdCard` genuinely renders inline in a real feed under the creator's real post. All throwaway accounts/business/campaigns and the webhook payload file were deleted afterward; the test dev server on port 3001 was killed, the owner's own port 3000 was never touched.

## 81. Creator Monetization & Native Advertising — Phase 3: ranked, explained creator recommendations

Continuing §79/§80's own 5-phase roadmap: "AI-powered matching + explanations... category/audience/engagement/geography scoring." Made one deliberate, load-bearing implementation call up front rather than defaulting to the literal word "AI": the owner's own worked example for this feature — "68% audience overlap" — is a *computed percentage*, not a sentence an LLM would need to generate, and this project has an established, repeated pattern of preferring a deterministic, unit-testable formula over an LLM call whenever the output is itself a number or a rule-based explanation (`AdEngineService`'s `calculateEffectiveCpmCents`, §80's own `selectPacedPostIds`/`isCandidateBlocked`). There is also a standing practical constraint named earlier in this same roadmap (§78): this project's own Gemini API credit is periodically exhausted, and no backend path exists today for a direct, non-tool-call Gemini request outside one business's chat `ToolContext` — building that infrastructure for a feature whose actual output is "rank creators by 2 numeric factors and print which ones matched" would be real, unjustified overhead. Phase 3 therefore ships a transparent scoring algorithm, explicitly not a Gemini call — named plainly here, and the UI copy never claims "AI" for it (says "Рекомендовано", not "AI-подбор"), matching this project's existing discipline of never labeling a deterministic algorithm as AI in front of a user.

**1. The algorithm — `lib/creator-matching.lib.ts`, pure and Prisma-free.** `scoreCreatorMatch(campaign, creator)` weighs two factors: category match (campaign's `targetCategoryIds` vs. the creator's own primary/additional categories — a primary-category hit scores full weight, a secondary-category hit scores partial, no match scores zero; an untargeted campaign, `targetCategoryIds: []`, scores every creator at full weight — same "empty means any, not none" semantics `NativeAdCampaign`'s own schema comment already established) and geography (campaign's `targetGeography` vs. the creator's `User.city`, case/whitespace-insensitive) — 60/40 weighted, a geography miss deliberately does not zero out an otherwise-strong category match (partial credit, not a hard filter — the admin still decides). `rankCreatorMatches` sorts a list of creators by that score. Every result carries a `reasons: string[]` — the literal sentences the admin sees ("Основная категория creator'а совпадает с таргетингом кампании", "География совпадает: Киев", plus an always-present audience-size fact) — because a bare percentage with no explanation would fail the owner's own explicit requirement (§8 of the root spec: "explained match reasoning"), not just be less polished.

**2. Backend wiring.** `NativeAdAssignmentsService.listRecommendedCreators(campaignId)` — resolves the campaign's targeting, pulls every active creator with their full category set and `User.city`, gets each one's friend count (`prisma.friendship.count`, N+1 across a bounded, admin-only, occasional read — the same acceptable-N+1 reasoning already used for `AdminAdvertisingController`'s per-business inventory loop and for this same module's `NativeAdFeedService`'s per-creator assignment lookup), scores and ranks them via the pure lib, returns the top 20. New `GET admin/native-ads/campaigns/:campaignId/recommended-creators` — deliberately does not replace or gate `assign` (`POST .../assign` still accepts any active `creatorProfileId`): a ranked list is a suggestion surface layered on top of Phase 2's manual-assignment mechanism, not a new access-control rule.

**3. Frontend.** `AdminNativeAdsPanel`'s `AssignmentManager` gained a "Рекомендовано" section above the existing plain `<select>` picker — each row shows the creator's name, match percent, and the full reasons list, with its own "Назначить" button; the manual dropdown stays underneath as a fallback for a creator the algorithm didn't recommend but the admin wants anyway. Already-assigned creators are filtered out of both lists identically.

**Test coverage.** 8 new unit tests for `scoreCreatorMatch`/`rankCreatorMatches` (perfect match, untargeted campaign, primary-vs-secondary category weighting, category mismatch alone, geography mismatch not zeroing a strong category score, case/whitespace-insensitive city comparison, the always-present audience reason, stable descending sort) — 511 backend tests total.

**Live-verified end to end.** Seeded two real creators (one primary-category "Gaming" + city "Киев", one primary-category "Finance" + city "Одесса") and a real campaign targeting `["Gaming"]` + `["Киев"]`, pushed it through the real create→creative→Stripe-pay→admin-approve path exactly as in §80. Backend REST confirmed the exact expected scores — Gaming Creator 100% (category + geography both hit), Finance Creator 8% (0.6×0 category + 0.4×0.2 geography-miss-partial-credit = 0.08), each with the correct reason strings. Real Chromium confirmed the same numbers and reasons render correctly in the actual Admin panel UI, confirmed clicking "Назначить" on a recommended row assigns correctly and the assigned creator disappears from the recommendation list, leaving only the remaining candidate. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` clean on both stacks. All throwaway accounts/business/campaign and the webhook payload were deleted afterward; the test dev server on port 3001 was killed, the owner's own port 3000 was never touched.

**Not built** (per the root plan, unchanged): revenue-attribution ledger with admin-configurable platform/creator split (Phase 4), real payouts via Stripe Connect (Phase 5).

## 82. Creator Monetization & Native Advertising — Phase 4: revenue-attribution ledger, admin-configurable split, transparent reporting

Continuing §79-§81's own roadmap: "revenue attribution ledger, admin-configurable platform fee/creator share... per-placement/per-campaign transparent reporting for creator/advertiser/platform." No Stripe Connect exists yet (that's Phase 5, deliberately last) — this phase is entirely about correctly *computing and recording* who's owed what, not about actually moving money.

A real, structural gap had to be closed before any of this could work: `POST native-ads/feed/impression|click` (§80) only ever carried `campaignId` — enough to charge the advertiser, but with no way to know *which creator's content* the ad was shown under, since one campaign can be assigned to several creators (`NativeAdAssignment`, §80). Fixed by switching the body to `assignmentId` (already returned to the client in `NativeAdFeedItemDto`, no new plumbing needed) — `NativeAdCampaignsService.recordImpression/recordClick` now resolve `assignmentId → {campaignId, creatorProfileId}` first, so every revenue event can be attributed to an actual creator.

**1. Data model.** New `NativeAdRevenueEvent` — one row per impression/click that *actually* charged the advertiser (see below), carrying `grossCents` plus the three-way split already baked in at write time (`creatorShareCents`/`platformFeeCents`/`processingFeeCents`) — baked in, not recomputed later from current settings, so a future change to the split percentages never rewrites history. New `NativeAdRevenueSettings` — a singleton row (fixed id `'default'`, seeded by the migration itself, not lazily created) holding `creatorRevenueShareBps`/`platformFeeBps`/`paymentProcessingFeeBps`/`minimumPayoutCents` — the exact fields the root spec named as "NOT hardcoded," stored as basis points (the same storage shape `Business.taxRateBps` already established). Deliberately **no new balance field on `CreatorProfile`** — a creator's earned total is always a live aggregation of the ledger (`groupBy` on `creatorProfileId`/`currency`), the same "derive, don't duplicate" principle already used for `AdminAdvertisingOverviewDto.totals.revenueByCurrency`.

**2. The split — `lib/native-ad-revenue.lib.ts`, pure and tested.** `splitRevenue(grossCents, settings)` computes `creatorShareCents`/`platformFeeCents` by percentage, then hands the *remainder* to `processingFeeCents` rather than computing it independently — the same rounding-remainder-absorbs-the-drift convention `AdCampaignsService.applySpend` already uses, so the three parts always sum back exactly to the gross with no lost or duplicated cents (verified by a dedicated test on a non-evenly-divisible amount). `grossCents` itself is a **delta**, not `spentCents`'s absolute value — `applySpendAndAttribute` (the renamed, extended `applySpend`) computes `nextSpentCents - campaign.spentCents` before overwriting, so the ledger only ever records money that was *newly* charged by this one event. For `cpm` billing this is often zero (an individual impression frequently doesn't push the floor-divided cent value up — the exact same rounding behavior `applySpend` already had, now simply visible as "no ledger row this time" instead of an invisible no-op); for `cpc` it's always the full bid, since every click adds a whole number of cents. Either way the invariant holds: summing a campaign's `NativeAdRevenueEvent.grossCents` always exactly equals its `spentCents`.

**3. Backend endpoints.** `GET/PATCH admin/native-ads/revenue-settings` (validates the three Bps sum to exactly 10000 — a cross-field rule class-validator can't express on its own, checked directly in the service). `GET native-ads/my-revenue` — creator's own earned totals by currency (`CreatorRevenueSummaryDto`), deliberately silent (empty list) if the user has no `CreatorProfile` yet, same as every other creator-self endpoint. `GET businesses/:businessId/native-ad-campaigns/:campaignId/revenue-breakdown` — advertiser-facing transparency into one campaign's own split (single currency, since one campaign only ever has one). Admin's existing overview endpoint gained `totals.realizedRevenueByCurrency` — the ledger's own totals, explicitly distinguished in its own doc-comment from the pre-existing `revenueByCurrency` (total *paid-in* budget, including what hasn't been spent yet).

**4. Frontend.** Creator Studio's "Доход" tab — permanently empty since Phase 1 — now shows real per-currency earned totals with impression/click counts, explicitly labeled as an accrued, not-yet-paid-out balance (Phase 5's job). Business Dashboard's native-ads campaign cards gained a compact "Куда уходит бюджет" breakdown (creator/platform/processing, only rendered for `active`/`paused` campaigns that could plausibly have real events, silently renders nothing on error or zero spend rather than an intrusive error box — this is a transparency add-on inside an existing card, not its own screen). `AdminNativeAdsPanel` gained a realized-revenue tile and a new settings form (`AdminNativeAdRevenueSettings`) with live client-side sum validation — shows the running total in red before the admin ever submits an invalid split, not only after a backend rejection.

**Test coverage.** 5 new unit tests for `splitRevenue` (even division, the rounding-remainder invariant, zero gross, a 100%-to-creator edge case, a single-cent gross never producing a negative or over-large share) — 516 backend tests total.

**Live-verified end to end** with real money-shaped numbers, not just structurally: a `cpc` campaign at a 500-cent bid, three real clicks recorded through the real endpoint — confirmed `spentCents` landed at exactly 1500, three `NativeAdRevenueEvent` rows each split exactly 350/125/25 (matching the live 70/25/5 default settings precisely), the creator's own Studio page showing exactly `10,50 ₴` earned, the advertiser's own dashboard showing the same three numbers as a spend breakdown, and the admin overview showing the identical totals a third time from its own independent aggregation query — all four numbers agreeing across four different vantage points. Separately verified the settings form live: entered a split summing to 110% and confirmed the UI caught it (red total, save disabled) before any request left the browser, then saved a valid 80/15/5 split and confirmed it persisted in Postgres — reverted back to the platform-wide 70/25/5 default afterward, since (unlike every other test artifact in this session) this settings row is real, shared, production-affecting state, not scoped throwaway data. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` clean on both stacks. All throwaway accounts/business/campaign were deleted; the test dev server on port 3001 was killed, the owner's own port 3000 was never touched.

**Not built** (per the root plan, unchanged): real payouts via Stripe Connect (Phase 5) — the accrued balance shown in Creator Studio today has no way to actually leave the platform yet, by design, until that phase.

## 83. Creator Monetization & Native Advertising — Phase 5: real payouts via Stripe Connect

The last of the originally-scoped five phases (§79's own roadmap named this "the heaviest, most compliance-sensitive piece, deliberately last"). Everything through §82 computed and displayed an accrued balance; nothing could move that money out of the platform. This phase closes that gap with real Stripe Connect transfers, gated behind the same "self-service request, admin-approved real effect" pattern this feature has used from the start (`NativeAdCampaign`'s draft→pending_review→active, now `NativeAdPayout`'s pending→paid/failed).

**1. Discovering the right Stripe API, live.** The codebase had zero Connect precedent to extend, so the first real work was confirming what actually works against this project's own test-mode Stripe account, by direct API experimentation rather than assumption. The v1 `stripe.accounts.create` path is flatly rejected by Stripe itself for new integrations ("Stripe no longer recommends Accounts v1"); the v2 `stripe.v2.core.accounts`/`accountLinks` API is the only supported path, with several non-obvious required shapes confirmed by trial: `dashboard: 'express'`, a single `configuration.recipient` (not `customer`) since a creator here is a payout recipient, not a buyer or seller; `defaults.responsibilities.{fees_collector,losses_collector}: 'application'` (the seemingly-symmetric `'stripe'` value is rejected by Stripe for this exact capability combination); and — found only by hitting it live — a top-level `contact_email` is mandatory the moment `configuration.recipient` is present, with no hint of this in the request shape itself (`stripe-connect.service.ts`'s `createOnboardingLink` now takes the creator's own email and passes it through, so Stripe's own account-health notifications reach the right person). `accountLinks.create`'s `use_case.account_onboarding.configurations` must exactly match the account's own applied configurations, or Stripe rejects the link with `configs_must_match_to_use_account_links`. The platform cannot submit ToS acceptance or identity data on a creator's behalf via API at all (`tos_acceptance_on_behalf_not_allowed`, confirmed live) — Stripe's own hosted onboarding page is not an implementation shortcut, it is the only legally-valid path for an Express account, and the design leans into that rather than fighting it.

**2. Data model.** `CreatorProfile` gained `stripeConnectedAccountId` and a three-state `CreatorStripeConnectStatus` (`not_connected`/`onboarding`/`active`) — deliberately independent of `CreatorStatus` (identity verification and monetization eligibility, §79): a creator can be fully `active` as a creator while still not connected for payouts, and the reverse never happens. New `NativeAdPayout` (`pending`/`paid`/`failed`) plus a `payoutId` column added to the existing `NativeAdRevenueEvent` ledger (§82) — a payout doesn't move or copy ledger rows, it *claims* them: `requestPayout` sums every event of one currency with `payoutId: null` into a new `pending` payout and stamps them with its id in the same operation, so a creator's available balance for a *next* request is always `sum(creatorShareCents WHERE payoutId IS NULL)` — cleanly distinguished in the API from the pre-existing lifetime `earnedCents` total (§82's `CreatorRevenueByCurrencyDto` gained `availableCents` specifically for this: the two numbers diverge the moment a creator has ever been paid once, and conflating them would have shown a stale, too-high "available to withdraw" figure).

**3. The failure-recovery path — the one piece of business logic worth designing carefully.** `NativeAdPayoutsService.process` is the single most irreversible action in the entire Creator Monetization system, so it is never automatic: an admin explicitly triggers it per payout, the same conservative posture as campaign approval. It attempts a real `stripe.transfers.create` (the existing v1 Transfers API, confirmed live to work correctly against a v2-created destination account) and, if Stripe rejects it — the ordinary case for any account still mid-onboarding, with the exact real error `insufficient_capabilities_for_transfer`-shaped message — the payout flips to `failed` with that human-readable reason stored verbatim, and every ledger event it had claimed is released back to `payoutId: null` in the same transaction. A creator never loses access to their own earned balance because of a Stripe-side failure; they can simply request again once onboarding completes. Status is never pushed by webhook — v2 Connect uses a structurally different "thin events" delivery mechanism, separate infrastructure from the existing classic-webhook `StripeWebhookController` — so `refreshStripeConnectStatus` instead re-reads the real account status from Stripe live, on demand (opening Creator Studio, returning from hosted onboarding), the same "don't cache what isn't a hot path" economics as §82's `NativeAdRevenueSettingsService.get()`.

**4. Cross-module wiring.** `NativeAdsModule` now imports `CreatorsModule` for `StripeConnectService` — the one new module dependency this phase introduces, justified by the same precedent `OrdersModule` already set for `AdvertisingModule`/`PostsModule` (imported one-way, purely to reach a service needed for webhook/settlement dispatch). Plain data reads of `CreatorProfile` remain direct Prisma queries with no service dependency, unchanged from every earlier phase — this exception is scoped strictly to the real Stripe capability, not a general loosening of the module boundary.

**5. Endpoints & frontend.** `POST/creators/stripe-connect/onboarding-link` (creates the account on first call, reuses it on every later call — confirmed live: the same `acct_...` id came back across three separate requests, each with a fresh, single-use onboarding link), `POST creators/stripe-connect/refresh-status`; `POST/GET native-ads/my-revenue/request-payout`/`my-payouts` for the creator; `GET/POST admin/native-ads/payouts`/`payouts/:id/process` for the admin queue. Creator Studio's "Доход" tab gained a Stripe connection status card (three honest states, a "Подключить выплаты через Stripe" CTA that redirects to the real hosted onboarding page, a "Проверить статус" button for the `onboarding` state), a per-currency "Запросить выплату" action gated on both `stripeConnectStatus === 'active'` and a positive `availableCents`, and a payout history list. `AdminNativeAdsPanel` gained a pending-payouts queue with a single "Обработать" action per row — deliberately surfaced through a persistent result banner rather than an inline per-row message, after live testing caught a real bug: the processed row disappears from the *pending* list the instant the queue refetches, which silently ate the failure reason before it could be read; the fix hoists the last outcome (success or the verbatim Stripe failure text) to panel-level state that survives the refetch.

**Test coverage.** No new pure-function unit tests this phase (the split math was already covered in §82; this phase is Stripe orchestration and ledger bookkeeping, not new deterministic algorithms) — 516 backend tests continue to pass unchanged; frontend 80 tests unchanged. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` clean on both stacks throughout.

**Live-verified end to end**, entirely through the real application code and real (test-mode) Stripe API — no raw one-off scripts standing in for the actual endpoints. A fresh throwaway creator account: `GET creators/me` showing the new `stripeConnectStatus` field; the onboarding-link endpoint failing with Stripe's real validation error on the first attempt (missing `contact_email`) and succeeding once fixed, returning a genuine `connect.stripe.com/setup/e/...` URL; the same Stripe account id reused across repeated onboarding-link calls; a seeded ledger event driving `availableCents` correctly through `GET native-ads/my-revenue`; a real payout request correctly zeroing the available balance while leaving the lifetime `earnedCents` untouched; the admin queue listing it; `process` making a genuine `stripe.transfers.create` call against the still-onboarding test account and receiving Stripe's real capability-rejection error, which correctly flipped the payout to `failed`, stored the exact Stripe message, and released the claimed event back to an available balance — confirmed by a second successful payout request for the identical amount. All of the above repeated in a real Chromium session (a scoped `next dev -p 3001` instance, the owner's own port 3000 never touched) against the Creator Studio revenue tab and the admin native-ads panel: the connection-status card, the balance card with its "Запросить выплату" button, the payout history (including the real Stripe error text rendered verbatim), and the admin panel's queue and process button, which is what surfaced the disappearing-row bug fixed in Design section 5 above — re-verified visually afterward with a second request/process cycle showing the persistent banner correctly. Zero console errors across every screen. All test users, the seeded campaign/assignment/ledger event, and both throwaway payouts were deleted from Postgres afterward.

**Named, deliberate verification boundary:** Stripe's hosted onboarding wizard itself (the KYC document/identity collection step) was not walked through live in this session. Earlier live testing against this exact surface established that it actively refuses headless-automation traffic (`net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` specifically and only on this page, not on any other Stripe endpoint) — concluded to be Stripe's own anti-bot protection guarding a KYC data-collection surface, and deliberately not circumvented, the same principled line already drawn around Stripe Identity's equivalent flow in §79. Every piece of code on either side of that wizard — account/link creation, status polling, the real transfer attempt and its failure handling — was verified live against the real Stripe API; only the human-facing document-upload screen itself was not automated through.

This closes the originally-scoped five-phase Creator Monetization & Native Advertising roadmap (§79-§83). No further phase is implied by the root plan; any future work here (recurring/scheduled payouts, multi-country identity types, richer AI-driven matching beyond §81's deterministic scoring) would be a new, separately-scoped request, not a continuation of this one.

## 84. Creator Monetization — closing two follow-up requests: `CreatorCategory` admin CRUD, and a third site-wide status bar for Creator

Two small, independently-scoped follow-ups after §83 closed the five-phase roadmap. Both close real, previously-named gaps rather than opening new ones: §79 itself named `CreatorCategory` admin CRUD "an explicitly deferred nice-to-have" (the taxonomy was seed-only, editable only by a migration), and the owner separately asked for a persistent site-wide "you are a Creator" indicator, matching the existing `AdminBar`/`BusinessOwnerBar` pattern.

**1. `CreatorCategory` admin CRUD.** `CreatorCategoriesService` gained `create`/`update`/`remove` alongside its existing `listTree`/`listAll` (both untouched — still the only source for onboarding/matching). Three real constraints enforced server-side, not just client-side hygiene: slug uniqueness (Postgres `P2002` mapped to a friendly 400, not a raw constraint-violation message), a cycle guard on `update` (walks up the new parent's own `parentId` chain looking for the id being moved — rejects "move a category into its own descendant" before it can corrupt the tree), and a children-guard on `remove` (schema's own `onDelete: Cascade` would otherwise silently wipe an entire subtree on one click — blocked with a clear "delete/move the subcategories first" instead). A new flat `GET admin/creators/categories` (DFS order, each row carrying a `depth` the frontend renders as indentation) sits alongside the existing recursive `GET creators/categories` (onboarding's own tree, left untouched) — two shapes for two different consumers, not two competing sources of truth. `AdminCreatorsPanel`'s "Creators" tab gained a new `AdminCreatorCategoriesPanel` section above the existing creator list: inline edit-in-place per row, a create form, and a delete confirmation modal naming exactly what breaks (creators lose the tag; subcategories block the delete).

**2. A third site-wide status bar, styled and stacked to match.** `CreatorBar` — a third sticky bar after `AdminBar`/`BusinessOwnerBar`, shown whenever the current user has any `CreatorProfile` at all (any status, not just `active` — the same reasoning `BusinessOwnerBar` already applies to a `draft` business: the bar is a stable entry point, the destination screen itself already handles every status honestly). Per the owner's explicit ask, it always renders as the *lowest* of the three bars — under `BusinessOwnerBar` if present, or simply under `AdminBar` if that's the only other one, or alone at the top if neither exists. Mechanically this is the same CSS-variable stacking trick §-prior bars already established (`--admin-bar-h`/`--business-bar-h` on `.app`, each bar's own `top: calc(...)` reading the ones above it, `Header`'s `top` summing all three) extended by one more variable (`--creator-bar-h`), not a new layout system. Its own near-black/graphite palette with a warm gold accent (owner's own suggestion, "чёрный или какой-то его оттенок") is deliberately distinct from `AdminBar`'s navy `--tavern-ink` and `BusinessOwnerBar`'s brick-red — three bars stacked directly on top of each other must stay visually distinguishable even at a glance.

**Test coverage.** No new pure-function unit tests (category CRUD is straightforward CRUD plus two guard clauses, verified live below rather than with synthetic unit tests) — 516 backend / 80 frontend tests continue to pass unchanged. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` clean on both stacks.

**Live-verified end to end**, through the real endpoints and a real Chromium session (scoped `next dev -p 3001`, owner's port 3000 untouched throughout). Backend: created a category, hit the duplicate-slug guard, created a child, confirmed the parent's delete was correctly blocked while it had that child, confirmed both cycle-prevention checks (move into own child, set self as own parent), a valid rename+reorder, and the flat list's `depth` values — all against the real running container, not mocks. Browser: the admin "Creators" tab rendering the real 58-category seeded tree with correct indentation, a category created/renamed/deleted through the actual form and confirmation modal (row genuinely gone afterward, zero console errors throughout). Separately confirmed all three bar combinations render and stack correctly by direct observation: `CreatorBar` alone at the top for a plain creator account; `BusinessOwnerBar` (brick-red) directly above `CreatorBar` (graphite/gold) for an account with both; `AdminBar` (navy) directly above `CreatorBar` for an admin account temporarily given a creator profile — every case showing the correct bar order with no overlap and the page content pushed down by the correct combined height. All throwaway accounts/businesses/test categories were deleted afterward; the demo admin account's temporary creator profile was removed, the account itself left untouched.

## 85. Subscribers (Following) — a second, one-directional relationship, and Creator eligibility/traffic switched to it

The owner's correction to §79-§84's own design: `Friendship` (mutual, request-approved) was standing in for two genuinely different concepts — personal social connections, and a creator's actual audience. The fix: a new one-directional `Subscription` model (`follow`/`unfollow`, no request/approval step, nothing like `FriendRequest`), and two real behavior changes riding on it — a creator's posts must reach their *subscribers'* feeds (not just friends'), and monetization eligibility gates on subscriber count, not friend count.

**1. Data model.** `Subscription` (`followerId`/`followingId`, `@@unique([followerId, followingId])`, `@@index([followingId])` for the reverse lookup `Friendship` never needed) — deliberately no canonical-pair sorting (unlike `Friendship`'s `userAId < userBId`): direction here is real, not artificial. `SubscriptionsService` mirrors `FriendsService`'s shape wherever the concept overlaps (`getFollowingIds`/`countFollowers` mirror `getFriendIds`/`countFriends` exactly, same "count(), not findMany()" discipline) but is structurally simpler — no transaction/retry machinery, since there's no mutual-request race to resolve.

**2. The one-line change that does the real work.** `NativeAdFeedService.getAdsForPosts(postIds)` takes no viewer identity at all — it only ever decides ad injection from whichever post ids the client's own feed already returned. That fact, confirmed by direct code reading before writing anything, meant "connect creator traffic to subscribers" reduced to one addition: `PostsService.listFeed`'s `OR` clause gained `{ authorId: { in: followingIds } }` alongside the existing own/friends/communities/boosted branches. Once a subscriber's feed legitimately contains the creator's post, native ad injection follows automatically — the `native-ads` module was not touched at all, and live verification below proves it, not just the code reading.

**3. Eligibility metric replaced, not combined.** `CreatorsService.getEligibility`/`startOnboarding` swapped `friendsService.countFriends` for `subscriptionsService.countFollowers` — per the owner's explicit wording ("монетизация... при наличии 2000 подписчиков"), this replaces the metric, it doesn't average or sum the two. `CreatorEligibilityDto.friendCount`/`requiredFriends` renamed to `subscriberCount`/`requiredSubscribers` throughout both stacks (dev-only rename, no real users depend on the old field name); `configuration.ts`'s `creatorMinFriends`/`CREATOR_MIN_FRIENDS` renamed to `creatorMinSubscribers`/`CREATOR_MIN_SUBSCRIBERS`. `CreatorsModule` dropped its `FriendsModule` import entirely (friend-count was its only use) in favor of `SubscriptionsModule`. The one other place a creator's reach was proxied by friend-count — Phase 3's admin-facing recommendation reason string (`creator-matching.lib.ts`) — was updated too: `friendCount` → `audienceSize`, now `friendCount + followerCount` (a simple sum, not a distinct-union query; this is a soft ranking hint for the admin, not a financial figure, so double-counting someone who is both a friend and a subscriber is an acceptable, harmless imprecision).

**4. Frontend.** New `entities/follow` slice — deliberately not named `entities/subscription`, because that name was already taken by an unrelated pre-existing entity (`BusinessSubscription`/billing plans; caught and corrected mid-implementation after a naming collision briefly overwrote that entity's files, restored from git before any damage stuck). `followUser`/`unfollowUser`/`getFollowers`/`getFollowing`, the last two genuinely cursor-paginated (not the small profile-widget's load-everything-once pattern `getUserFriends` uses) — followers are uncapped by design, unlike friends. A new `SubscribersListModal` (one component, a `kind: 'followers' | 'following'` prop) deliberately has no search box, unlike `FriendsListModal`: that list loads in full and searches client-side; this one streams pages via `useInfiniteScroll`, and searching a partially-loaded list would silently miss real matches — the same trade-off `FriendsListModal`'s own comment already names for a different case, just decided the other way here because the constraint is reversed. A new compact `ProfileFollowStats` ("N подписчиков · M подписок") sits under the tagline in both `ProfileWidget` (own profile) and `UserProfileView` (someone else's, plus a Подписаться/Отписаться button that coexists with the friend-request button — never either/or, per the owner's core ask that these stay separate entities). `CreatorPromoCard`/`CreatorOnboardingWidget`/`CreatorAudienceSection` all swapped their friend-count copy for subscriber-count copy.

**Test coverage.** No new unit tests: `SubscriptionsService`, like `FriendsService` before it, is pure CRUD glue with no extractable deterministic algorithm — this codebase's entire 516-test backend suite is exclusively pure-function tests (confirmed by inspection before deciding this, not assumed), and `FriendsService` itself has never had a dedicated test file either. `creator-matching.lib.test.ts`'s fixtures were renamed (`friendCount` → `audienceSize`); 516 backend / 80 frontend tests continue to pass. `npm run check` and a real `npm run build` clean on both stacks.

**Live-verified end to end**, real REST + a real Chromium session (scoped `next dev -p 3001`, owner's port 3000 untouched throughout). Two throwaway accounts, A subscribed to B while explicitly NOT friends — confirmed B's post was absent from A's feed before subscribing and present immediately after; confirmed the exact same, completely unmodified `POST native-ads/feed/select` call attached a real native ad to that post for A once a campaign was assigned to B's creator profile — the single strongest proof that the traffic-connection claim holds, not just by code reading. Confirmed self-subscribe is rejected, unsubscribing removes the post from the feed again, and `GET users/:id/followers`/`following` return the real paginated rows. Confirmed `GET /creators/eligibility` reports `subscriberCount` sourced from real `Subscription` rows, independent of friend count. In the browser: the follow button toggling live with the follower count updating optimistically (no reload) on both the profile page and from inside the "following" list modal's own unfollow action; the followers/following modals rendering real data with zero console errors; `CreatorPromoCard`'s locked state genuinely showing "🔒 Creator доступен от 2 000 подписчиков" and a live "0 / 2 000 подписчиков" progress bar. All test accounts, the seeded campaign/creative/assignment, and the test creator profile were deleted afterward.

This is a genuinely completed, bounded slice — no "not built" callout, since every part of the owner's ask (separate entity, subscribe function, follower/following counters, traffic-linking, eligibility switch) was implemented and verified live in this same pass.
